# Core coherent physics (highest risk — verify first)

Part of the [physics validation ledger](physics-validation-ledger.md). See the [validation methodology](methodology.md) for the status lifecycle and the [domain inventories](domain-inventories.md) for a claim-by-claim index.

## `optimization-fixed-window-yield`

- **Claim:** a fixed-band incoherent PXR/CBS source objective is the integral of the piecewise-linear node density over explicit in-grid energy bounds, in photons/(sr incident electron); no detector acceptance, current or macro-electron factor is applied
- **Code:** `optimization/scoring.py::FixedWindowYield`
- **Source:** [energy-grid semantics](../physics/radiation-physics/energy-grid-semantics.md), `eq-grid-physical-integral` and density units; definition of the integral of a piecewise-linear interpolant
- **Status:** anchored
- **Checks:** constant and linear analytic densities on nonuniform nodes with interpolated endpoints; zero and signed limits; component isolation; explicit rejection of unsupported quadrature, joint-field line corrections, invalid coverage and nonfinite data
- **Anchor:** `tests/optimization/test_scoring.py`
- **Notes:** post-processing claim only; does not validate the underlying PXR/CBS model or establish angular convergence. Node data must resolve the spectral structure. [Independent verification](radiation-physics/optimization-fixed-window-yield.md). Human sign-off belongs to #277.

## `optical-planar-tr`

- **Claim:** Optical TR of transported planar-stack crossings is the exact planar Maxwell (Ginzburg–Frank) crossing amplitude with explicit measured scalar permittivity per layer, summed per history with crossing phases `exp[i(omega t - k_par . r_par)]`; matched media and vanishing films give zero and the conductor limit is the Ginzburg–Frank form. Locally straight formation is bounded from transported straight runs and unbounded use is refused
- **Code:** `montecarlo/spectrum/optical_transition.py::optical_transition_radiation`; `montecarlo/runner/transition_emission.py::case_optical_transition`; `montecarlo/spectrum/optical_transition_validity.py::{formation_error_bound, coherent_formation_error_bound}`; `montecarlo/spectrum/transition_radiation.py::interface_transition_radiation_amplitude`; `materials/optical.py::gold_johnson_christy_response`
- **Source:** Ginzburg and Tsytovich (1990) Ch. 2 planar boundary problem; [optical planar TR and CTR](../physics/radiation-physics/optical-transition-radiation.md#single-electron-source); published comparison: Coenen, Vesseur and Polman, arXiv:1107.3632v1 Fig. 3 and APL 99, 143103 (2011), DOI 10.1063/1.3644985, Figs. 3 and 4c with Johnson–Christy optics (PRB 6, 4370 (1972))
- **Status:** anchored
- **Checks:** Fresh-context derivation (single-interface continuity, crossing phase, linear own-field split, formation length) matches; independent closed form agrees with the solver to 6e-10 over complex ε, β and θ; matched medium and vanishing film zero; conductor and finite-ε gold Ginzburg–Frank closed forms; case wiring equals the #103 history scorer; out-of-band, side-exit and charge-mismatch refusals; formation measure zero for infinite straight runs and monotone for shorter runs; Coenen Fig. 3 independently re-digitized (16 values within 0.004e-7/nm); real RTX 5080 CPU/CUDA parity of complex history fields and yields within 5e-15 of peak
- **Anchor:** `tests/montecarlo/spectrum/test_transition_radiation_optical.py` (conductor and finite-ε gold closed forms, 1e-9); `tests/montecarlo/spectrum/test_transition_radiation_optical_anchor.py` (published Coenen Fig. 3 normalized shape within 0.006 over 600–900 nm, 0.04 over 375–550 nm); `checks/optical_transition_cuda.py` (measurement only)
- **Notes:** Issue #615 implementation of the #533 research claim. [Fresh-context verification](radiation-physics/optical-planar-tr.md): `rederived` at `3caced48`; the owner applied `anchored` with this scope: absolute normalization is anchored by the conductor and finite-ε closed forms, the published comparison anchors only the normalized spectral shape of a *calculation* (Fig. 3, withdrawn preprint; APL version not consulted). Evidence gates, not claimed: the published absolute curve is a constant 0.696 of the hemisphere integral (independently reproduced; APL text confirms a full-hemisphere calculation, an effective-energy error is excluded, the paper's absolute rate check rests on an unitemized ~50 % efficiency, and an independent transcription of the paper's cited theory reproduces our hemisphere integral to 1e-3. That theory is García de Abajo, RMP 82, 209 Sec. IV.C, with the per-sr Γ_CL normalization; the transcription is of the same physics, not independent physics, and was reviewed rederived at `6e12e4de0`. Eq. (56) as printed in the arXiv:0903.1669 preprint omits sin θ, which would give 1.26–1.36×, the wrong direction. So the cited theory does not explain the published curve; the cause of 0.696 is unknown); the measured Fig. 4c angular patterns (APL version) are reproduced in normalized shape with the stated collection operator: azimuth-integrated flux over solid angle (dN/dθ ∝ sinθ dN/dΩ), 40 nm band-pass, no polarizer, θ 24–84° (RMS 0.023 at 400 nm, 0.05 at 900 nm; per-sr convention excluded at RMS 0.14, perfect conductor at 0.36/0.23; fresh-context re-digitized and reviewed at `6e12e4de0`). The sinθ reading is inferred from the data, because the text is ambiguous. The comparison does not constrain absolute yield, the band-pass model, entering vs exiting charge, φ dependence or polarization, or angles below about 30°; the 375–550 nm shape residual is systematic (3–5%). Formation measure is heuristic; `coherent_formation_error_bound` propagates it to the pair term and the total yield the case refuses on (fresh-context rederived at `b0860fbfa`). [CUDA measurements](check-records/optical-transition-cuda.json) and [passing exact-revision record](check-records/optical_transition_cuda.jsonl) at `816a0b59`. Source-only: no finite radiator, wave optics or detector response. Consistent gold scene (`au` transport tables + Johnson–Christy optics, 30 keV, `test_gold_transport_with_gold_optics_scene`): the scene runs and gives 0.9–1.3× the entry-only yield (backscatter exits interfere); the formation bound (~1.5) refuses it at the default tolerance. Opt-in phase formation policy (`kink_phase_tol` / `formation_kink_phase`): transported kink lists bound the path's departure from the straight extrapolation within L (exact geometry; the sub-tolerance-is-straight step is heuristic); tolerance 0 reproduces the default exactly; at 0.1 rad the HOPG/Au-optics scene bound goes from 0.171 to 0 and the gold scene from 1.47 to 0.57 (still refused). Not yet fresh-context reviewed. Human sign-off belongs to #277. Formation validity (2026-11-09 fresh-context review at `d882faf32`, verdict `discrepancy`): the formation depth was compared with path lengths, anti-conservative by $1/|\hat u_z|$ for oblique crossings in both policies; fixed (path formation length, pinned by `test_formation_length_is_a_path_length_for_oblique_crossings`), and the opt-in phase policy now also tests the current-direction term $D/L$. The corrected code was re-reviewed `rederived` at `405d97a44`; `test_phase_policy_also_bounds_the_current_direction_change` pins the $D/L$ term. Scored spectra are unaffected.

## `optical-stack-fast-solver`

- **Claim:** For a flat isotropic stack, the TE/TM reflection recursion and its unrolled top-wave response solve the same boundary system as the dense planar TR solve; layer phase factors satisfy `|p| <= 1`, so thick absorbing and evanescent layers cannot overflow and cost is O(layers) per energy/direction pair.
- **Code:** `montecarlo/spectrum/transition_radiation_stack.py::{stack_response, stack_amplitude, straight_charge_amplitude}`; `montecarlo/spectrum/transition_radiation_stack_kernel.py::{device_stack_response, device_stack_amplitude}`
- **Source:** Tangential continuity of the Ginzburg–Frank boundary problem (`tr-detected-omission-bound`), Ginzburg & Tsytovich 1990 Ch. 2; TE/TM reflection recursion as in Born & Wolf, *Principles of Optics*, Ch. 1 stratified media; [derivation](../physics/radiation-physics/optical-transition-radiation.md#many-layer-stacks)
- **Status:** rederived
- **Checks:** Fresh-context derivation matches NumPy and C++ term by term; mpmath reference over adversarial stacks; [write-up](radiation-physics/optical-stack-fast-solver.md). Agreement with the dense solve except where dense itself loses accuracy (oblique falling chains; dense system in 100 to 800-digit mpmath); matched, vanishing, closed single-interface and opaque-exit limits; Bloch stop-band decay; weak-contrast resonance.
- **Anchor:** `tests/montecarlo/spectrum/test_transition_radiation_stack.py`; `tests/montecarlo/spectrum/test_transition_radiation_stack_cuda.py`
- **Notes:** Issue #962. A faster solve of existing physics, not a new radiation mechanism: results from flat stacks combine interference, TR Bragg-reflected by a layer period (macroscopic DTR) and own-field diffraction, with a reciprocal vector along `z` only. Shares `lambda = 0` and the lossless Cherenkov pole with the dense system. The recursion also has a removable pole at `D_i = 0` (bound mode of the lower half-stack in lossless evanescent media), where dense stays finite, and rounds `1 + rho` where `rho -> -1`. Non-finite results and the propagated estimate of that rounding above 1e-8 raise `LinAlgError` for the whole call. The estimate covers only that rounding; conditioning near lossless resonances and phase rounding `u sum|lambda d|` are not estimated and can be larger, up to full scale at a resonance, where dense is also unreliable. Deep responses can lose pointwise accuracy in lossless stacks; absorbing stacks keep it. Stacks below `FAST_STACK_MIN_LAYERS` stay on the dense path, so their bits are unchanged. CPU/CUDA evidence: `check-records/optical-stack-cuda.json`.

## `optical-ctr-population`

- **Claim:** For independent identically distributed electrons the optical TR power per physical electron is `E|S|^2 + (N-1)|F|^2 |E S|^2` with `N = Q/e` from the bunch charge alone, estimated from `M` sampled histories with positive importance weights by `Sum w|S|^2/W` and `(|Sum wS|^2 - Sum w^2|S|^2)/(W^2 - W_2)`; `|F|^2 = 1` for histories carrying their own offsets, the Gaussian or resolved empirical characteristic function at `kappa = (omega, -omega n_x, -omega n_y)/hbar c` only for offset-free histories; correlated sources only as an explicit realization; unsupported contracts refused
- **Code:** `montecarlo/spectrum/optical_transition.py::{OpticalBunchSource, optical_transition_radiation, gaussian_coherent_band_eV}`; `montecarlo/spectrum/transition_radiation_crossings.py::history_field_sums`; `montecarlo/spectrum/optical_transition_validity.py::{radiated_energy_fraction, out_of_band_coherent_energy_eV, pec_crossing_energy_per_eV, coherent_formation_error_bound}`
- **Source:** Squared field-sum expansion, Shkvarunets and Fiorito, arXiv:0712.2848, Eq. (1); self-normalized importance sampling of the two moments; [bunch population and sampling law](../physics/radiation-physics/optical-transition-radiation.md#bunch-population-and-sampling-law)
- **Status:** anchored
- **Checks:** Fresh-context derivation of the population law, weighted estimators (consistent; exactly unbiased for unit weights), charge-scaling moments, κ phase convention, Gaussian/empirical transforms, double-count refusal, realization law and coherent window matches; brute-force pair sums agree to 3e-16; N=1, F=0 and F=1 limits; sample-count invariance at fixed charge; sampled vs analytic Gaussian; importance-weight consistency and scale invariance; empirical sample/profile transforms, microbunch harmonic and resolution refusal; catalog 200 fs RMS window; refusals of double offsets, correlated laws, weighted realizations and N ≠ M; band-edge coherence and in-band energy-budget refusals; real RTX 5080 CPU/CUDA parity of pair reductions within 5e-15 of peak
- **Anchor:** `tests/montecarlo/spectrum/test_transition_radiation_optical.py` (closed-form and limiting-case references only); `tests/montecarlo/spectrum/test_optical_ctr_casalbuoni_anchor.py` and `tests/montecarlo/spectrum/test_optical_ctr_schroeder_anchor.py` (published calculations, perfect conductor); `checks/optical_transition_cuda.py` (measurement only)
- **Notes:** Issue #615 implementation of the #533 research claim; extends `tr-coherent-bunch` and `coherent-physical-bunch-population` without changing their statuses. [Fresh-context verification](radiation-physics/optical-ctr-population.md): `rederived` at `3caced48`; the two published comparisons below were fresh-context reviewed at `f844eeff8` (re-derivation matches; 15 mutations run), which supports `anchored` in this scope: published calculations, perfect conductor, far field, forward conductor-exit crossing. Not pinned by any published value: phase signs, N−1 versus N, the self term, importance weights, correlated or tilted laws, real permittivities, backward geometry, finite screens, near field, measurements. Schmidt et al., arXiv:1803.00608, was ruled out as an anchor: its absolute check needs the plot-only beamline response and Fig. 9 lacks the screen radius. Out-of-band coherent energy is added to the budget from a perfect-conductor image-charge envelope with analytic Gaussian `erf`/`erfc` tails (`out_of_band_coherent_energy_eV`; Gaussian laws without t–x correlation; other laws are recorded unbounded); envelope formula and `coherent_formation_error_bound` were fresh-context verified at `b0860fbfa` (formula and propagation match). Its validity statement was a medium scope discrepancy, since narrowed: the conductor envelope fails for Cherenkov exit faces (refused on in-band `beta^2 Re eps >= 1` as a proxy) and grazing low-|ε| media (≤1.6×, documented); quadrature refused above γ = 5; B is a sample estimate. [CUDA record](check-records/optical_transition_cuda.jsonl) at `816a0b59`. Published population comparison (2026-11-09): Schroeder et al., PRE 69, 016501 (2004) Fig. 3, digitized by the owner (`tests/data/optical/schroeder-2004-fig3.json`). Fully coherent (F = 1) conductor-exit CTR of a 5-MeV Boltzmann momentum spread (u_t = 10; g fixed by Fig. 2) run through the production crossing scorer's distinct-pair estimator (`test_optical_ctr_schroeder_anchor.py`). The figure plots the square root of the paper's Eq. (36) (log-log exponent 0.992, scale 0.997), an inferred reading. Collimated: matches Eq. (36) quadrature to 1e-2 and the figure to 0.7 % rms (max 5 %). Divergent (rms 0.05 rad): matches to 1.5 % for θ ≥ 0.08; below, the figure exceeds the paper's own Eqs. (17)/(18) by up to about 20 %; an independent quadrature of those equations agrees with the scorer to 1 %, so that part is excluded as a source figure/equation discrepancy. The excluded range is where the divergence effect is largest; the retained range still carries 16 % (0.08 rad) to 4 % (0.15 rad) of it. This comparison involves neither N nor a form factor. Scope: pair estimator with energy and divergence spread and the oblique conductor amplitude; the Gaussian form factor itself (F ≠ 1) is not exercised by this comparison. Published absolute energy and form factors (2026-11-10): Casalbuoni, Schmidt and Schmüser, TESLA Report 2005-15, Eq. (37) and Figs. 14–15 (Zotero `YG9F9AS9`; `tests/data/optical/casalbuoni-2005-fig14-15.json`, curve vertices read from the PDF vector paths; `test_optical_ctr_casalbuoni_anchor.py`). Fig. 14 dashed curves: far-field energy in µJ/GHz inside 100 and 10 mrad cones from an infinite conducting screen, 1 nC, γ = 1000, zero length, uniform disc of radius 50, 150, 350, 1000 µm, 0.6–28 THz, all parameters stated. Through the production scorer with N = Q/e, as an `empirical` density profile (8 curves) and as `sampled` crossing positions with |F|² = 1 (2 curves, distinct-pair estimator alone): within 1 % of the figure (reviewer: at most 0.73 % for profiles, 0.83 % for the sampled lattice) and, against the reviewer's quadrature of Eq. (37), within 4e-4 for resolved profiles and 2e-3 for the sampled lattice. The figure is about 0.5 % (0.2–0.7 %) above Eq. (37) with CODATA constants (cause not identified). The 1000 µm, 100 mrad curve below 1.2 THz lies up to 1.9 % under its own equation and is excluded as a source figure/equation difference; a smaller shortfall of the same kind shows at 350 µm, 100 mrad. Fig. 15 left: the `gaussian` law at σ = 50 fs matches |F_long|² within 0.003 from 0.5 to 9 THz, as a form factor and as an energy ratio. Scope: absolute N² scale from the charge, transverse disc and Gaussian time form factors, pair estimator with transverse phases (magnitude only: the laws are even, so phase signs are not tested); a perfect conductor in the far field. Not covered: measured data, finite screens and near field (solid curves, Fig. 15 right), measured optical response, correlated or tilted laws. No instrument-level bunch inference (#617). Human sign-off belongs to #277.

## `frank-tamm-homogeneous-dielectric`

- **Claim:** The internal bulk Cherenkov yield of one charge `ze` at constant `beta` in an unbounded homogeneous isotropic nonmagnetic weakly absorbing dielectric with explicit permittivity is the Frank–Tamm `d^2N/(dx dE) = (alpha z^2/hbar c)(1 - 1/(beta^2 n^2))` for `beta n > 1` and zero otherwise, with `n = Re sqrt(epsilon)` and emission on the cone `cos theta_C = 1/(beta n)`; the threshold is local in photon energy; `frank_tamm_yield` refuses active media, `Re epsilon <= 0` and loss tangents above the gate
- **Code:** `montecarlo/spectrum/cherenkov.py::{frank_tamm_yield, cherenkov_threshold_beta, BulkEmissionYield, ChargedPassage}`
- **Source:** Frank and Tamm, Dokl. Akad. Nauk SSSR 14, 109 (1937); Jackson, *Classical Electrodynamics* 3rd ed. §13.5; [bulk Cherenkov radiation](../physics/radiation-physics/cherenkov-radiation.md#source-equation); independent reference Geant4 11.4.2 `G4Cerenkov`
- **Status:** anchored
- **Assumptions:** infinite homogeneous isotropic nonmagnetic medium, scalar local permittivity; constant `beta` straight path per call (caller segments slowing down), no recoil; weak loss only (default loss tangent ≤ 1e-3), sharp lossless threshold kept; internal source yield only: no interface/transition radiation, escape, absorption along the photon path, strong absorption or absorption-edge X-ray Cherenkov
- **Checks:** prefactor `alpha/hbar c = 369.810 eV^-1 cm^-1`; nondispersive closed form and cone; `z^2` and charge-sign independence; zero yield at and below threshold, linear onset above it; vacuum and `n < 1` zero; `beta -> 1` cone `1/n`; dispersive band integral for `n^2 = a + b E^2` against its arctan closed form to 1e-8; per-energy threshold; weak-loss continuity; out-of-scope refusals. Geant4 11.4.2 `G4Cerenkov` summed over the same recorded steps (job 2280): photon totals within 0.15 % (|z| ≤ 1.2) for mu- at beta 0.99 in n = 1.33, mu- at the dispersive fused-silica threshold and alpha (z = 2) at beta 0.95 in n = 1.5; energy-spectrum reduced chi-square 0.74–1.15 and angle 0.46–1.38; a below-threshold proton emits nothing in both
- **Anchor:** `tests/montecarlo/spectrum/test_cherenkov.py` (analytic limits); `tests/montecarlo/spectrum/test_cherenkov_geant4_reference.py` (binned Geant4 references in `checks/cherenkov_geant4/reference`)
- **Notes:** Issue #587. [Fresh-context verification](radiation-physics/frank-tamm-homogeneous-dielectric.md): `rederived` at `3cb78c29` (units, signs, limits, source-to-code and Geant4 setup; totals independently recomputed without PyRITE code); the owner applied `anchored` from the green analytic and Geant4 anchors. The `O(tan delta)` bound on the unmodelled absorptive correction is an order statement only, neither derived in coefficient nor tested. Standalone source term: no catalog support, transport coupling or detected optical/IR workflow (#533). The planar-stack TR solve already contains the Cherenkov pole of a Cherenkov-active layer, so this bulk term must not be added to a TR result for the same layer without a double-counting decision. Geant4 is an independent implementation of the same lossless formula, so it anchors normalization, dispersive threshold and cone, not the weak-loss treatment. Human sign-off belongs to #277.

## `optical-finite-boundary-field`

- **Claim:** Vector equivalent surface currents with the finite-distance outgoing Green function are a candidate finite-radiator TR/DR model; DR can be emitted without an electron crossing the material.
- **Code:** `src/pyrite/montecarlo/spectrum/optical_finite_screen.py::circular_pec_transition_field`, `src/pyrite/montecarlo/spectrum/optical_finite_screen.py::observe_screen_green`, `src/pyrite/montecarlo/spectrum/optical_finite_screen.py::hemisphere_energy_per_eV` (opt-in NumPy/CuPy source kernels; host transport handoff `src/pyrite/montecarlo/spectrum/optical_finite_transport.py::entrance_pec_screens_from_crossings`)
- **Source:** Shkvarunets and Fiorito, arXiv:0712.2848, Eqs. (15), (17), (19)–(25); Schmidt et al., arXiv:1803.00608, Eqs. (11)–(15); [finite-source requirements](../research/physics/optical-ir-emission.md#finite-targets-diffraction-radiation-and-the-pre-wave-zone).
- **Status:** anchored
- **Checks:** Tests (`tests/montecarlo/spectrum/test_optical_finite_screen.py`, `test_optical_finite_oblique.py`, `test_optical_finite_bunch.py`, `test_optical_finite_transport.py`): large-disc Ginzburg–Frank absolute angular yield incl. 30 keV limit, zero screen, aperture DR without crossing and radius suppression, mesh refinement, charge-sign/arrival-phase linearity, outgoing-energy conservation under propagation, oblique source vs independent moving-charge Fourier transform, N² coherent scaling; window-independent angular-quadrature hemisphere energy of a 5 µm disc equals the analytic Ginzburg–Frank hemisphere yield to 1e-4 (FFT k-grid totals are biased by the light-circle 1/kz density, documented). Fresh-context derivation 2026-10-23 at `3a98f542` ([write-up](radiation-physics/optical-finite-boundary-field.md)): matches; independent oblique incident-field time integral (3e-8), Ginzburg–Frank angular and hemisphere limits, 30° oblique infinite-plane k-space reference, annulus Hankel reference (0.3 %), and far/near-field Hankel angular-spectrum references for the Green operator (1–2e-5). Fresh source-first backend-scope re-derivation (same write-up, 2026-10-23) matches the full-speed oblique phase/Bessel source, weighted distinct-pair subtraction/denominator and finite-plane E/H bilinear normalization. [Native-source RTX 5080 parity](check-records/optical-source-cuda.json), SLURM 2279: 15 tests pass (eight source/real-Case checks plus seven retained device-operator checks), including CPU-Bessel refusal, device array ownership, weighted/Gaussian/realization moments, N² and phase cancellation. Open: published finite-disc numerical comparison, spectral/source/acceptance convergence and automatic production integration.
- **Anchor:** `tests/montecarlo/spectrum/test_optical_finite_schmidt.py`: published Schmidt et al. (arXiv:1803.00608) Eqs. (12)–(15) and Figs. 6–7. Vector solver with exact Green observation equals the absolute far-field Eq. (14) and the near-field Eq. (15) to 3e-3 for the Fig. 7(a) disc (a = 3 mm, D = 4 m, λ = 0.3 mm, γ = 100), and Eq. (15) to 1 % for the Fig. 7(b) disc (a = 30 mm, D = 0.2 m) at γθ ≥ 0.5. Published lobe positions are reproduced. Measured DR candidate Phys. Rev. E 63, 056501 (2001): full geometry/data not acquired.
- **Notes:** #616 bounded PEC disc/annulus magnetic-equivalent-current reference with straight (normal/oblique) incidence. Perfect-conductor equivalents need a stated validity envelope before applying them to real optical films. Physical-optics source (−E_inc,t on the metal, zero off it), exact only for an unbounded PEC; requires radii and edge distances ≫ λ. Owner applied `anchored` (2026-11-09) after fresh-context review at `6e12e4de0` (verdict rederived, anchored scope supported). Scope: published *theoretical* finite-disc calculation (Schmidt Eqs. 12–15, Figs. 6–7), not a measurement. The anchor checks normalization, discretization and the Green operator; it shares the physical-optics disc source with Schmidt, so it does not test that approximation. Near the on-axis minimum of Fig. 7(b) the vector |E|² exceeds scalar Eq. (15) by up to 5.7 % because of the longitudinal near-field E_z ≈ (a/D)E_ρ. The transverse field alone follows Eq. (15) to 2e-3 (pinned). The periodic FFT propagation of that case does not converge locally (wraparound errors of 0.4–1.5× at a 205 mm window); `observe_screen_green`/`collect_screen_green` are the references there. Measured slit DR (Castellano et al., PRE 63, 056501, Fig. 8; `checks/castellano_slit_dr.py`, screening only): focal-plane detection of the finite screen cannot reproduce the measured slit dependence (rms ≥ 0.21 for every aperture). The paper's near-field geometry reaches rms 0.04–0.05 only with the unpublished detector distance and aperture fitted (b/f ≈ 3–5, θ_d ≈ 0.05–0.1 rad), so this is not a measured anchor. Ref. [13] (NIM A 435, 297) is generic theory and gives no TTF detector geometry. Its κ ≤ k source truncation (the J0 term of PRE Eq. 3) changes the ratios by < 1 % (b/f ≤ 7). Leissner et al. (PAC 1999, PRE ref. [15]) give no distances, but the topology (a collimating parabola, then a focusing parabola onto horn detectors) is an imaging relay. With PRE's 200 mrad pupil, the image-plane model has one free parameter, the screen-referred detector radius ρ_d. It fits with rms 0.036 (ρ_d = 10 mm, 0.1 rad half-angle) or 0.017 (15 mm, 0.2 rad). Leissner's thesis (PITHA 99/4) supplies the relay: two f = 200 mm, 90 mm parabolas (unit magnification, pupil 0.20–0.22 rad) and 55.9 → 6.2 mm horns on 5 mm crystals. With no fitted parameter, the equal-étendue disc horn model (r_t/sin θ_a) gives Fig. 8 rms 0.015–0.086 over the pupil and throat/crystal range. The other equal-étendue readings (whole mouth with an angular limit, throat at the image) give ≥ 0.19. The agreement is conditional on the horn's phase-space acceptance and on the 1998 distances applying in 2000, so the status is unchanged. Gate: a horn acceptance model or measurement.

## `optical-wavefront-propagation`

- **Claim:** Passive vector angular-spectrum propagation between reference planes, combined with explicitly modeled polarization/optical elements, can retain CTR phase in finite-distance collection where a far-field angular detector is insufficient.
- **Code:** `src/pyrite/montecarlo/spectrum/optical_wavefront.py::propagate_vacuum`, `src/pyrite/montecarlo/spectrum/optical_wavefront.py::forward_power_w`, `src/pyrite/montecarlo/spectrum/optical_finite_screen.py::propagate_screen_field`, `src/pyrite/montecarlo/spectrum/optical_finite_collection.py::collect_screen_field`, `src/pyrite/montecarlo/spectrum/optical_finite_collection.py::collect_screen_green`, `src/pyrite/detectors/optical_elements.py::apply_optical_elements`, `src/pyrite/montecarlo/spectrum/optical_finite_convergence.py::finite_screen_collection_convergence`, `src/pyrite/montecarlo/spectrum/optical_finite_convergence.py::finite_aperture_spectral_convergence` (opt-in, bounded NumPy/CuPy parity)
- **Source:** Vacuum Maxwell angular-spectrum identity; Casalbuoni, Schmidt and Schmüser, TESLA Report 2005-15, Eqs. (11), (12), (19), (25), (33), Figs. 8 and 15; Schmidt et al., arXiv:1803.00608, Appendix D; [equation and instrument boundary](../research/physics/optical-ir-emission.md#propagation-detectors-and-observables).
- **Status:** anchored
- **Checks:** Tests (`tests/montecarlo/spectrum/test_optical_wavefront.py`, `test_optical_finite_collection.py`, `test_optical_interferometer_handoff.py`): plane-wave phase, oblique p/s power with longitudinal field, evanescent decay, negative-distance refusal, propagation composition and flux conservation, finite-plane aperture collection, paraxial Jones handoff vs full-vector flux; measured passive Jones elements (Malus law, lossless retarder composition, passivity preserved by interpolation); independent pitch/window convergence diagnostic; non-periodic direct-Green aperture collection (H = Faraday curl of E, checked by finite differences to 1e-8) agrees with a 102 µm-window FFT to 2e-4. Fresh-context derivation 2026-10-23 at `3a98f542` ([write-up](radiation-physics/optical-wavefront-propagation.md)): vacuum angular spectrum, phasor vs two-sided (πħZ0)^-1 normalization, Faraday H, s/p Jones, TJT† passivity and paraxial handoff match to ≤1e-9. ## `optical-instrument-observable`

- **Claim:** A calibrated linear response maps optical spectral energy per pulse to detector signal; an ideal balanced two-port field interferometer measures its response-weighted cosine transform, which alone does not uniquely determine bunch shape.
- **Code:** `src/pyrite/detectors/optical_energy.py::CalibratedPulseEnergyDetector`, `src/pyrite/detectors/optical_energy.py::CalibratedPulseEnergyDetector.predict_batch`, `src/pyrite/detectors/optical_interferometer.py::polarized_interferogram`, `src/pyrite/detectors/optical_interferometer.py::IdealMartinPuplett`, `src/pyrite/_optical_coherency.py::OpticalSpectralCoherency`, `src/pyrite/detectors/optical_ctr_inference.py::fit_gaussian_ctr`, `src/pyrite/detectors/optical_ctr_inference.py::GaussianCTRTemplates`, `src/pyrite/detectors/optical_ctr_jitter.py::ChargeJitterModel` (opt-in, synthetic-calibration tested)
- **Source:** Linear detector integration and two-arm field interference; DIPAC 1999 IT07, Fourier-transform spectroscopy/Martin–Puplett setup; Schmidt et al., arXiv:1803.00608, phase-retrieval ambiguity; [equations and units](../research/physics/optical-ir-emission.md#propagation-detectors-and-observables).
- **Status:** anchored
- **Checks:** Tests (`tests/detectors/test_optical_energy.py`, `test_optical_interferometer.py`, `test_optical_ctr_inference.py`): J/pulse and W units, saturation, calibration hashing, double-pass OPD, port energy conservation, zero/large-delay limits, polarization, synthetic Gaussian-duration recovery with open-interval/multimodality reporting and refusal of high-band-only identification; exact shot-averaged charge-jitter pair factor <N(N-1)> checked against a direct two-point moment. Fresh-context derivation 2026-10-23 ([write-up](detectors/optical-instrument-observable.md)): ideal Martin–Puplett Jones transfer, double pass, OPD-jitter damping, detector integral, Gaussian intensity form factor, ⟨N(N−1)⟩ pair factor and Δχ² threshold match symbolically and numerically (independent Jones matrices with random coherency, non-45° grids and lossy arms/ports to 8.5e-10). Scoped fresh-context check of batched energy/V/W and two-port reductions (same write-up, 2026-10-23) matches independent nonuniform integration and a lossy/non-45-degree/jittered field oracle. [Remote RTX 5080 parity](check-records/optical-device-reductions-cuda.json) (SLURM 2277): seven tests pass, with device storage, calibrated batch units, two-port voltages and persistence checked. Charge-jitter scan likelihood (`test_optical_ctr_jitter.py`): raw and per-shot-normalized means and port covariances against a direct shot simulation at $\bar N=40$ and $6\times10^6$, $r=0$ reduction to the chi-square fit, duration recovery with the parameter-dependent covariance. Scan convergence diagnostic (`test_optical_interferogram_convergence.py`): energy-node, mirror-step and mirror-range refinement each flagged alone; recovered fringe spectrum equals $\eta\,g\,S(E)$ to 2e-3 of its peak. Open: measured calibration/data, measured interferogram reproduction, convergence-justified production tolerances.
- **Anchor:** `tests/detectors/test_optical_interferometer_leissner.py`: Leissner et al., PAC 1999, p. 2172, Eq. (5) closed-form normalized difference interferogram reproduced by `polarized_interferogram` to 1e-10; fitted-curve features of Figs. 3–4 at the published σ = 2 ps (first zero 2.24 ± 0.2 ps, side minima 4.1 ± 0.3 ps at −0.611 ± 0.03 of the central maximum). Scope: ideal 45° Martin–Puplett Jones transfer, double-pass OPD and energy-weighted detector integral, normalized to the central maximum. Not exercised: measured calibration, digitized data points, absolute modulation depth, lossy optics, inference. Measured bunch-shape comparison (Schmidt et al. CTR/TDS) still pending numerical response and data.
- **Notes:** #617 ideal two-port Martin–Puplett and conditional Gaussian-duration fit only. No measured detector calibration, interferogram reproduction or uniquely inferred bunch profile is claimed. Fresh-context review 2026-11-09 at `405d97a44` (same write-up): charge-jitter likelihood, published interferogram and scan convergence all `rederived`; jitter-fit 95 % coverage 0.941 ± 0.003 over 7200 fits; raw-mode covariance needs near-Gaussian charge (43 % off for a gamma law at r = 0.3). The owner applied `anchored` with the scope in the Anchor field; the inference and the jitter likelihood are rederived, not anchored.

## `optical-phase-retrieval`

- **Claim:** From a finite-band longitudinal form-factor magnitude `|f(E)|` with a declared missing-band continuation, the Kramers–Kronig minimal phase `psi(w) = -(2w/pi) PV int_0^inf ln(|f(u)|/|f(w)|)/(u^2-w^2) du` and `rho(t) = (1/pi) int_0^inf |f| cos(psi - w t) dw` (convention `f = int rho e^{i w t} dt`) give the minimal-phase profile only; time reversal, arrival time, zeros in the other half plane and the out-of-band spectrum are not identified, and the result is never reported as unique. Declared charge, transverse, response and spectral-noise uncertainties are propagated by sampling through the same map.
- **Code:** `src/pyrite/detectors/optical_phase_retrieval.py::minimal_phase`, `src/pyrite/detectors/optical_phase_retrieval.py::reconstruct_minimal_phase_profile`, `src/pyrite/detectors/optical_phase_retrieval.py::MissingBandModel`, `src/pyrite/detectors/optical_phase_retrieval.py::propagate_reconstruction_uncertainty`, `src/pyrite/detectors/optical_phase_retrieval.py::FormFactorUncertainty` (opt-in, CPU)
- **Source:** Leissner et al., PAC 1999, p. 2172, Eqs. (2), (6), (7) (Zotero `WT4HERCE`), citing Lai and Sievers, NIM A 397, 221 (1997); printed Eq. (7) carries `+wz/c`, i.e. `rho(-t)` in the Eq. (2) convention (a time reversal). Closed-form segment integrals for piecewise-linear `ln|f|` and the power-law tail `(1/w)[g(X) artanh(w/X) - p chi_2(w/X)]`; [model and assumptions](../physics/radiation-physics/optical-wavefront-and-detectors.md#minimal-phase-bunch-profile-reconstruction-617).
- **Status:** rederived
- **Checks:** `tests/detectors/test_optical_phase_retrieval.py`: analytic minimal-phase gamma-2 profile `t e^{-t/tau}/tau^2` (`|f| = 1/(1+x^2)`, `psi = 2 arctan x`) recovered in phase and profile; Gaussian-band phase against scipy Cauchy-weight PV quadrature of Eq. (6); reconstructed profile reproduces the measured `|f|` by direct transform; flat-band power-law tail against quadrature to 1e-9; two-bunch profile with zeros in the upper half plane returned as its minimal-phase partner (leading bunch larger) with the deep in-band minima (heuristic, relative to adjacent peaks) reported at `(2k+1) pi hbar/T`, none skipped; minimal-phase two-bunch recovered as itself; low/high continuation changes the result and is recorded; charge uncertainty leaves the minimal phase invariant (constant continuation) and enters only through the `|f(0)| = 1` normalization; seeded response/transverse/noise spread. Fresh-context derivation 2026-11-10 at `aca5952a8` ([write-up](detectors/optical-phase-retrieval.md)): Eq. (6) sign, segment and power-law-tail closed forms, inverse transform and charge/systematic sampling match; independent quadrature to 6e-12 rad; printed Eq. (7) equals `rho(-t)` to 1.7e-5 of peak. Findings F1 (minima heuristic missed the first dip) and F3 (divide rather than multiply by the uncertain factor) applied afterwards.
- **Anchor:** none
- **Notes:** #617. Leissner et al. Fig. 6 (TTF reconstruction, ~4 ps FWHM) is not reproducible: the measured interferogram data, the fourth-order low-frequency polynomial and its matching point are not published (the paper reports ~1 ps FWHM sensitivity to them). Uncertainty sampling covers declared input uncertainty only, not the reconstruction ambiguities; charge, transverse and response terms are each fully correlated across frequency (conservative, declared). Not propagated into `fit_gaussian_ctr` beyond its existing scale prior.

## `optical-lattice-applicability`

- **Claim:** Atomic elastic diffraction of propagating optical/IR waves has no nonzero Bragg order when wavelength greatly exceeds atomic spacing; this does not exclude off-shell near-field coupling or tuned low-frequency lattice-force motion. Optical grating diffraction requires macroscopic periodic response rather than an extrapolated X-ray susceptibility harmonic.
- **Code:** `docs/research/physics/optical-ir-emission.md` (research applicability statement; no implementation)
- **Source:** Elastic wavevector matching, g no greater than twice the propagating wavenumber; own/free-field distinction of Artru and Rullhusen, NIM B 145, 1–7 (1998), DOI 10.1016/S0168-583X(98)00241-9; [assumptions and CBS/DTR exceptions](../research/physics/optical-ir-emission.md#pxr-cbs-and-dtr-at-long-wavelength).
- **Status:** unverified
- **Checks:** Proposed only: units, propagating Bragg inequality in a lossless isotropic medium, homogenization limit, evanescent-current and near-transverse CBS exceptions; do not infer a universal zero-emission theorem.
- **Anchor:** None executed. This scope boundary is research-only; no optical atomic-DTR or grating-DTR implementation is recommended without a selected geometry/source and published anchor.
- **Notes:** #533 explicitly includes DTR. No fresh-context applicability verification performed. Existing dtr-kinematic-boundary, dtr-two-beam-boundary, dtr-transported-current and line-tr-phase-reconciliation statuses are unchanged.

## `dtr-two-beam-boundary`

- **Claim:** Projecting Maxwell's curl equations onto two transverse harmonics gives a driven eight-component tangential field system; matching outgoing eigenmodes across a planar stack retains repeated diffraction in one crystal layer and the complex interference of the observed and diffracted charge harmonics
- **Code:** `montecarlo/spectrum/dtr_two_beam.py::{TwoBeamField, _maxwell, _modes, _background, _source_faces, two_beam_straight_field}`
- **Source:** Maxwell curl equations with local isotropic permittivity; Caticha, Phys. Rev. A 40, 4322 (1989), two-wave boundary problem (formulation context, not a transcription of numbered equations). Author derivation in [experimental DTR](../physics/radiation-physics/diffracted-transition-radiation.md#two-beam-straight-passage-diagnostic).
- **Status:** rederived
- **Checks:** Zero coupling recovers the homogeneous TR vector in both passage senses; weak coupling recovers the existing first-Born PXR+DTR complex vector; finite one-way coupling matches Born through capped/uncapped stacks with oblique reciprocal vectors in both passage senses; reverse coupling adds the leading second-order transition and third-order line corrections; translation phases, straight-trajectory reference invariance, transversality and spectral/angular broadcasting hold; surface termination rotates only the diffracted source row; a passive opaque slab stays finite; active truncated permittivity refuses
- **Anchor:** `tests/montecarlo/spectrum/test_transition_radiation_two_beam.py` (internal solver consistency and limits only; no published or independent anchor). `validation.dtr_reference --two-beam` exports the alternating-stack Born/two-beam comparison, source-row densities and signed interference; `tests/montecarlo/spectrum/test_transition_radiation_dtr_reference.py` checks accounting, distinct provenance and weak/thick reference behavior. This comparison does not add an independent anchor.
- **Notes:** Issues #103/#581. This claim covers the experimental CPU straight-passage boundary diagnostic. The opt-in transported-current production extension and CBS source have separate claims below. No mosaic average, recoil, device parity, independently verified extinction law or published reproduction. The longitudinal external-current contact term is retained when eliminating Ez. **2026-11-12 fresh-context verdict `rederived`** ([independent derivation](radiation-physics/dtr-two-beam-boundary.md)): ordered Maxwell blocks, longitudinal-current contact term, harmonic gauge, outgoing modal integrals, boundary continuity and complex source-row interference match. Six independent direct forced-curl/exponential boundary cases cover both passage senses and zero/one-way/two-way coupling; maximum relative field error is 1.20e-13. All 32 existing two-beam tests pass. Maxwell source equations support this verdict; the full Caticha paper was unavailable, so no numbered paper-equation or published-result agreement is claimed. Published anchoring remains pending; this does not complete #581's combined acceptance claim. Human sign-off remains in #277.

Six independent forced-curl boundary cases cover bent/horizontal currents, both passage senses and zero/one-way/two-way coupling; maximum full-field relative error is 4.24e-14 and correction error is 5.60e-12. Explicit history-pair enumeration agrees within 3.55e-15; 24 focused history/production/docs tests pass. Maxwell equations support the derivation, with no numbered paper-equation or published-spectrum agreement claimed. The locally straight crossing-TR baseline approximation and internal-consistency anchor classification remain. Human sign-off remains in #277.

## `line-tr-phase-reconciliation`

- **Claim:** Varying the transported point-charge current by the relativistic periodic-potential displacement gives moving polarization P and magnetization M=P cross v. Their Maxwell source, including longitudinal P, yields the same vacuum/on-resonance outgoing-field conversion for the Feranchuk PXR and CBS amplitudes, fixing their relative sign and common phase before a joint boundary solve.

(13)–(14), with the relativistic extension derived from the equation of motion and Maxwell current variation; [CBS phase derivation](../physics/radiation-physics/diffracted-transition-radiation.md#cbs-phase-in-the-same-maxwell-basis).
- **Status:** rederived
- **Checks:** Complex CBS vacuum field matches the relativistic Eq. (14) amplitude and absolute finite-passage phase; weak-coupling joint PXR/CBS field matches their sum with one common conversion; no intensity-fitted phase. Production replaces the legacy line scorer when selected.
- **Anchor:** `tests/montecarlo/spectrum/test_dtr_history.py::{test_cbs_vacuum_resonance_has_feranchuk_amplitude_and_absolute_phase,test_pxr_and_cbs_have_the_same_feranchuk_to_maxwell_phase}`
- **Notes:** The potential perturbation is locally straight and first order; v.g=0 refuses. **2026-11-13 fresh-context verdict `rederived`** ([independent derivation](radiation-physics/line-tr-phase-reconciliation.md)): relative PXR/CBS sign, common outgoing-field conversion and absolute finite-passage phase match in the classical vacuum/on-resonance scope, including longitudinal polarization, magnetization and harmonic conjugation. Printed Feranchuk Eq. (14) is nonrelativistic; the write-up derives its relativistic extension rather than attributing it to the printed equation. Three independent complex-field comparisons have maximum relative error 2.69e-12; 11 focused tests pass. No absorbing-medium legacy-line agreement, quantum-amplitude equivalence, published anchor or adjacent boundary/transport validation is claimed. Issues #103/#581 retain their broader requirements; human sign-off remains in #277.

## `line-recoil-resonance`

- **Claim:** exact vacuum photon root from the relativistic final-electron mass shell with a static crystal taking reciprocal momentum `+hbar*g`; physical roots lie in `(0,T]`, with `T` the incident kinetic energy; stable forward evaluation retains `1-beta` after beta rounds to one
- **Code:** `materials/recoil.py::{recoil_resonance_eV,_coefficients}`
- **Source:** Shi et al., *Transverse recoil imprinted on free-electron radiation*, Nature Communications 15, 7803 (2024), DOI `10.1038/s41467-024-52050-w`, exact conservation Eq. (6), with reciprocal label negated relative to the paper; not its small-recoil Eq. (7)
- **Status:** anchored
- **Checks:** energy/momentum units; positive final-energy branch; zero/negative/too-transverse transfer forbidden; final electron at rest; forward/backward geometries; soft-transfer classical limit and the additional transverse-lattice-recoil smallness condition; NumPy paired broadcasting
- **Anchor:** `tests/materials/test_recoil.py::{test_resonance_matches_unsquared_mass_shell,test_hopg_forward_root_no_longer_exceeds_the_beam_energy,test_forbidden_branches_return_nan,test_all_kinetic_energy_endpoint_and_broadcasting,test_forward_denominator_survives_beta_rounding_to_unity,test_classical_limit_requires_small_lattice_recoil_too}` (65-digit unsquared mass-shell bisection)
- **Notes:** Since #1099, production lines (`line-recoil-in-medium-root`) and `materials/emission.py::photon_energies` (default `recoil=True`) use this root. No in-medium or quantum-intensity claim here. Fresh-context verification independently rederived the source equation before code inspection, matched implementation and rotated/broadcast high-precision checks, and returned `rederived`; the owner applies `anchored` with the named passing regression tests. [Derivation](radiation-physics/line-recoil-resonance.md). Human sign-off pending.

## `line-recoil-validity-screen`

- **Claim:** exact vacuum line-recoil shift `E_exact/E_classical - 1`, with `E_classical = beta*u.Q/(1-beta*u.n)` the transfer-free limit of the `line-recoil-resonance` root on the same stable denominator; first order `-Q.Q/(2E*beta*u.Q) - (Q.n/E)/(1-beta*u.n)`; NaN for a nonpositive classical root, `+inf` for a positive classical root with a forbidden exact root
- **Code:** `materials/recoil.py::recoil_shift_fraction`
- **Source:** ratio of the `line-recoil-resonance` root (Shi et al. 2024, Eq. (6), reciprocal label negated) to its `Q -> 0` limit; first-order expansion author-derived
- **Status:** anchored
- **Checks:** 65-digit unsquared mass-shell root over the classical root (forward, backward, oblique, 30 keV–50 MeV); first-order expansion at small transfer; ultrarelativistic forward limit `-photon/E`; transverse transfer exceeding `photon/E`; NaN/`+inf` branches; float64 `beta -> 1`
- **Anchor:** `tests/materials/test_recoil.py::{test_shift_fraction_matches_mass_shell_over_classical,test_shift_fraction_first_order_limit_and_forward_photon_ratio,test_transverse_transfer_shifts_more_than_photon_over_energy,test_shift_fraction_branches_and_broadcasting}`
- **Notes:** Issue #249 diagnostic. #1099 retired the `recoil_shift_exceeds_limit` screen count (`RECOIL_SHIFT_LIMIT`, `_recoil_count`): production lines now sit on the exact root (`line-recoil-in-medium-root`), and the fraction is kept as a classical-comparison diagnostic. Vacuum free-electron kinematics only; whether the shift applies to a Bloch electron in a finite slab is answered by `line-recoil-in-crystal` (#527). The longitudinal term has magnitude `Q/(2*beta*E)`, ~1 % for 30 keV HOPG (002). Fresh-context verification derived before implementation inspection and returned `rederived`; independent 75-digit unsquared mass-shell checks agree within `1.6e-16` absolute shift. The owner applies `anchored` with the named passing regressions. [Derivation and evidence](radiation-physics/line-recoil-validity-screen.md); its screen-count section is historical. Human sign-off pending.

## `line-recoil-in-crystal`

- **Claim:** research claim (#527). Inside a kinematic crystal slab, the zero-phonon PXR/CBS line centre is the exact vacuum root of `line-recoil-resonance`. The lattice takes `+hbar*g` without energy; initial and final free-like Bloch branches obey the vacuum mass shell up to the common mean inner potential and Bloch branch shifts. A finite slab broadens the line around that centre. At 10–15 keV graphite/hBN (002)/(004) geometries, the omitted in-medium corrections move the harmonic defect `2E2 - E4` by ≤ 0.6 eV. The components are: mean inner potential (cancels in the defect, linear in `g`; single lines +0.3 to +0.7 eV); photon refraction with exit-face tangential continuity (`k_eff = omega (n + delta h)`, +0.25 to +0.58 eV on the defect over the 13 rows and both detector-azimuth conventions, ≤ 0.3 eV per line at the sized geometries); and entrance refraction (< 0.001 eV). Two-beam Bloch branch shifts of the free-like branch depend on the unreported in-plane crystal azimuth (median ≈ 0.4–0.5 eV on the defect, up to several eV near a strong Bragg condition). The excitation-weighted branch centroid is unshifted at two-beam order, so at EDS resolution they broaden rather than move the line. The straight-path exact root reproduces Huang et al.'s measured defect, `18.19 ± 0.66` eV measured against `19.10` eV predicted (19.5 eV with exit-face photon refraction, −2.0σ). The straight classical root gives `0` and is excluded at 27σ (≥ 11σ if the ±0.5 eV calibration error is uncorrelated between the two lines). Huang's own scattering Monte Carlo predicts ≈ 15.7 eV (quantum) and ≈ −2.5 eV (classical) for Fig. 1d, so the straight-path agreement does not test their transport/peak-fit bias. Not claimed: many-beam dynamical diffraction, electron Bragg-resonant geometries, phonon-assisted lines, absolute line energies (Huang's tilts were fitted with their quantum model), peak-fit bias from multiple scattering, the unreported detector azimuth (`phi_obs` = 0 pinned; 180° gives a 18.04 eV prediction), any production kernel change.
- **Code:** measurement only — no production code; `checks/recoil_in_crystal_527.py` against `materials/recoil.py::recoil_resonance_eV`
- **Source:** Huang et al., *Quantum recoil in free-electron interactions with atomic lattices*, Nat. Photon. 17, 224–230 (2023), DOI `10.1038/s41566-022-01132-6`, Eq. (3), Methods (observation angles, EDS calibration) and SI Table S4 (pinned in `tests/data/recoil/huang-2023-table-s4.json`), SI §S5 (±0.5 eV calibration), SI §S11 (photon refraction); Shentcis et al., Nat. Photon. 14, 686 (2020) (60–300 keV WSe₂; shift ≤ 1.6 eV against ~80 eV resolution, consistent but untested); [research note](../research/physics/quantum-recoil-in-crystal-527.md) §2
- **Status:** anchored
- **Checks:** classical defect identically zero for collinear harmonics, invariant under linear gain error, offset additive; measured-minus-predicted mean within 2σ (statistical ⊕ calibration); classical excluded > 10σ; `U0` from `cr-mean-inner-potential` applied to both states; first-order exit-face Snell decrement from `refractive_index`; tangential-momentum entrance refraction; two-beam branch shift reducing to `U_h^2/d` off Bragg and bounded by `|U_h|` at Bragg, azimuth-dependent, with excitation-weighted centroid unshifted (illustrative, not gated)
- **Anchor:** `tests/materials/test_recoil.py::test_huang_2023_harmonic_defect_matches_in_crystal_lines`; `checks/recoil_in_crystal_527.py`
- **Notes:** Issue #527 gate requested by #249. One experimental group and two vdW materials. Shi et al. (2024) is not used: its 30/300 keV Si benchmark belongs to option B. Production recoil-aware line kinematics are recommended as a separate issue that bumps `CXR_ESCAPE_MODEL` (research note §4). Fresh-context verification (2026-11-11) rederived the central statement and returned `discrepancy` on the correction budget: bulk instead of exit-face photon refraction, an entrance-bend sign error, a single-azimuth Bloch figure, and the missing Huang Monte Carlo caveat. All are corrected here and in the check. The re-check of the corrected row, check and note returned `rederived`. The owner applies `anchored` with the named passing regression test, which pins the measured-versus-exact-root defect; the correction budget is pinned by the check script. [Validation write-up](radiation-physics/line-recoil-in-crystal.md). Human sign-off pending.

## `line-recoil-in-medium-root`

- **Claim:** production PXR/CBS line root on a first-order Snell escape piece with exact electron recoil: `omega = (v.g - r_num)/(1 - v.n + r_den - delta(omega) v.h)`, `r_num = hbar c g^2/(2E)`, `r_den = hbar c n.g/E`, `h = -e/(e.n)`. It is exact at `delta = 0` (`line-recoil-resonance`) and at `hbar g -> 0` (`xray-in-medium-resonance`), and drops only the mixed `delta (Q.h - hbar omega)/E` and `O(delta^2)` terms. The `sinc^2` half-width and energy Jacobian carry `kappa = E/(E - hbar omega)` (`(1 - beta_f.n) = kappa D_exact`, `line-recoil-formation`). A line needs `D_exact > 0` and `hbar omega <= T`. Phased routes add the age phase `kappa (x D_vac + r_den) t`, `kappa (x v.g - r_num) t` (`x = hbar omega/E`), and keep the escape phase geometric, so a coherent self-term sits `O(x delta)` from the incoherent root
- **Code:** `montecarlo/spectrum/cxr/_recoil.py::{_line_recoil_terms,_recoil_slope_factor,_recoil_line_allowed,_recoil_formation_line}`, `montecarlo/spectrum/cxr/_kernels.py::_in_medium_kinematics`; CUDA twin `montecarlo/spectrum/coherent_prologue_jit_kernel.py::_coherent_prologue_kernel`
- **Source:** Shi et al. (2024), Eq. (6), DOI `10.1038/s41467-024-52050-w` (reciprocal label negated), closed with the first-order Snell photon momentum `k_eff = omega (n + delta h)` of `xray-in-medium-resonance`; Huang et al. (2023) SI Table S4 measured harmonic defect as the experimental anchor; reduction to the production denominator author-derived
- **Status:** anchored
- **Checks:** 65-digit unsquared mass shell at `delta = 0` (root rtol 1e-14, `kappa D` slope 1e-13); 65-digit in-medium mass shell within the mixed-order bound `[|delta|(|Q.h| + hbar omega) + delta^2 h^2 hbar omega]/(E D)`; bitwise classical Snell root at `hbar -> 0`; Huang defect through production straight segments; Shi 2024 Fig. 2 per-reflection roots and their MeV collapse; SI §S10 forward lines; coherent/incoherent agreement and split invariance; CUDA prologue against the CPU formation line
- **Anchor:** `tests/montecarlo/spectrum/test_line_recoil_exact.py::{test_kernel_root_and_slope_match_the_65_digit_mass_shell_at_zero_decrement,test_snell_root_differs_from_the_in_medium_mass_shell_only_at_mixed_order,test_zero_hbar_hook_is_the_classical_snell_root,test_huang_2023_harmonic_defect_through_production_lines,test_shi_fig2_geometry_lines_sit_on_each_reflections_exact_root,test_shi_fig2_splits_collapse_toward_mev,test_shi_si_s10_forward_lines_sit_on_the_exact_root,test_classical_hook_recovers_the_classical_roots}`; `tests/montecarlo/spectrum/test_xray_dispersion.py::test_single_segment_coherent_line_sits_on_the_escape_path_root`; CUDA-gated `tests/montecarlo/coherent/test_coherent_formation_absorption_cuda.py::test_prologue_exact_recoil_matches_the_cpu_formation_line`
- **Notes:** Issue #1099 (#527 option A). The per-hkl, batched, coverage and `reflection_rows` routes call these helpers (comment-marked at each call). Huang 13 rows, simulated mean defect 19.46 eV against measured 18.19 ± 0.66 eV (−1.92σ, stat ⊕ calibration). The classical production lines give 0.41 eV (refraction only) and are excluded at 27σ. `CXR_ESCAPE_MODEL = "segment-mean-v6-exact-recoil"`. Pinned catalogue families are scored as complete symmetry orbits so that reflections sharing `g_z` split. The retired `recoil_shift_exceeds_limit` count is superseded. No transverse-recoil-resolved spectra, Bloch-branch shifts or intensity change beyond the root's Jacobian. Fresh-context verification (2026-11-11) derived from Shi Eq. (6) and the Snell momentum before reading the implementation, matched the root, `kappa D` slope, guard and age phase term by term (CPU and CUDA source), confirmed the mixed-order bound with a 60-digit check over 159 random geometries (10 keV–100 MeV), and returned `rederived`; the owner applies `anchored` with the named passing anchors. Observations: the age phase is exact per constant-momentum flight only (`line-recoil-formation` scope); formation `kappa` is taken at the vacuum root (`O(x delta)`). ## `quantum-recoil-observability`

- **Claim:** research screen (#527 option B). For an unshaped (plane-wave) electron, the transverse-recoil spectrum of Shi et al. (2024) is the incoherent sum over reflections of per-reflection lines centred on each reflection's exact root (`line-recoil-resonance`). Pathways to distinct final transverse momenta do not interfere. The screen estimates observability by rigidly shifting PyRITE's classical per-reflection PXR (`mc_cxr_spectrum`, one reflection per call) from the classical to the exact root, on straight and transported segments, with a Gaussian detector. Straight 30 nm Si [001], θ = 100°: the classical peaks sit on the classical roots, and at 5 MeV the classical heights match Shi Fig. 2b (ratio 0.96 / 0.95 for n_z = −2 / −3). Transported results, 2000 electrons:
  - **Fig. 2 large-angle geometry:** the tallest prototype peak moves −35 eV (30 keV) and −20 eV (300 keV), with 0.4× the classical height and the comb skewed to low energy. With a 70 eV detector the peak moves −39 / −28 eV and the FWHM grows 1.4–1.5×. Shi's tallest QM line sits at the same (0,±2,2) root (Fig. 2f/2d pixel reads 1383.5 / 3105.4 eV), so the peak positions agree; the prototype's QM/CL height (0.36–0.41) is below Shi's (≈ 0.9).
  - **SI §S10 forward case (100 keV, 100 nm, 2.5°):** the peak moves −125 eV and the line spreads from 15 to 90 eV FWHM. With a 70 eV detector the envelope peak moves −121 eV and the FWHM grows 3.4× (83 → 283 eV); after transport the leading (1̄,±1) shell is a shoulder, not a separate peak. The −125 eV peak is the |g⊥|² = 10 shell and depends on PyRITE's relative shell weights (|g⊥|² = 2 at 0.74 of it).
  - **HOPG (004), 11.7 keV, 85 nm, 130°:** a rigid −35 eV shift (longitudinal only, g⊥ = 0).
  - **Multiple scattering:** at these thicknesses it changes peak shifts by ≤ 2.5 eV and widths by ≤ 30 eV (largest: forward, 70 eV detector); the nominal-direction recoil shift errs by ≤ 5.5 eV rms on scattered flights (mean ≤ 0.5 eV).

  Not claimed: Shi's quantum per-pathway intensities. At 30/300 keV PyRITE's classical n_z = −2 peak is 1.8× / 2.5× Shi's CL (digitized), and the prototype's tallest line is 0.36–0.41× CL against Shi ≈ 0.9×. #1103 attributes both to Shi's CL being PXR-only while PyRITE's classical line carries CBS (`shi-2024-fig2-intensity-attribution`); the prototype keeps PXR+CBS row weights, so relative line heights, and any peak shift that depends on them (notably the forward case), remain unvalidated against Shi. Also not claimed: the recoil Jacobian, direction-dependent shifts on scattered flights (nominal-direction shift used), backgrounds (bremsstrahlung, characteristic, forward CBS), beam divergence, shaped electrons (option C), and any production kernel change.
- **Code:** measurement only — no production code; `checks/transverse_recoil_527.py` against `materials/recoil.py::recoil_resonance_eV` and `montecarlo/spectrum/cxr` (`mc_cxr_spectrum`)
- **Source:** Shi, Wong, Huang, Wong & Kaminer, Nat. Commun. 15, 7803 (2024), DOI `10.1038/s41467-024-52050-w`, Eqs. (1)–(3), (6)–(9), Fig. 2 and SI §S6, §S10; [research note](../research/physics/transverse-recoil-527.md)
- **Status:** rederived
- **Checks:** classical straight-segment peaks within 2 eV of the classical root at 30 keV, 300 keV and 5 MeV; 5 MeV classical heights within 15 % of Shi Fig. 2b visual reads; rigid shift preserves each row's integral; transported and straight results agree within the stated bounds
- **Anchor:** `checks/transverse_recoil_527.py` (measurement script; no regression test)
- **Notes:** Issue #527. Shi Fig. 2 heights are visual reads of a published figure (about ±10 %), not digitized data. Fresh-context verification (2026-11-11) rederived the physics and reproduced every table number; it returned `discrepancy` on claim wording: the tallest-Shi-line identification, the 30 keV n_z = −3 CL read, the forward-case scattering bound and resolvability wording. All are corrected here, in the note and in the check; the re-check returned `rederived`. No regression test pins the screen, so the status stays `rederived`. **2026-11-12 (#1103):** after #1099 moved production lines onto the exact root, the check's classical spectra are taken under the ħ → 0 hook (`_RECOIL_HBARC_EV_ANG = 0`), which restores the prototype definition; a 2000-electron re-run reproduces every transported table number above, and Part 1 now reads the vector-digitized Fig. 2 (`tests/data/recoil/shi-2024-fig2.json`). [Validation write-up](radiation-physics/quantum-recoil-observability.md). Production per-reflection exact roots are [#1099](https://github.com/Quantum-Light-Matter-Cooperative-QLMC/pyrite/issues/1099). Human sign-off pending.

## `shi-2024-fig2-intensity-attribution`

- **Claim:** research attribution (#1103). Shi et al. (2024) Fig. 2 "CL" is the classical PXR-only rate (Eq. (1) with the deflection δv(t) dropped), while their "QM" carries PXR, coherent bremsstrahlung and their interference; PyRITE's classical line is PXR + CBS + interference. On Shi's geometry (straight 30 nm Si [001], θ = 100°, |h|,|k| ≤ 8) this accounts for PyRITE's 1.8× / 2.5× classical excess at 30 / 300 keV, n_z = −2, and for Shi's QM/CL area excess (3.9 / 2.9 at n_z = −2, 5.8 / 4.3 at n_z = −3; ≈ 1.1 at 5 MeV). An independent per-reflection evaluation of Shi SI Eqs. (S12)–(S17) with Shi's inputs (Salvat Yukawa, no f′, no Debye–Waller) gives Shi's n_z = −2 CL peak as its PXR-only rate (1.06 / 0.73 / 1.16 at 30 keV / 300 keV / 5 MeV, against 0.27 / 0.33 / 1.02 with CB) and Shi's QM/CL area ratio as (PXR+CB, exact roots)/(PXR, classical) (4.11 / 2.25 / 1.14 against 3.88 / 2.91 / 1.13). PyRITE's PXR-only shortfall against Shi (0.47 at 30 keV, n_z = −2) is the crystal potential: Si K-edge f′ = −1.78 (|χ₀₂₂|² × 0.64 at 1.42 keV; × 0.60 with Debye–Waller) against Shi's Yukawa, which has neither f′ nor Debye–Waller; Waasmaier–Kirfel f₀ against Yukawa is about a 2 % effect. In the classical limit (5 MeV) the independent elements match PyRITE's `A_PXR`, `A_CBS` and their constructive interference row by row within 2.3 %. Not claimed: absolute 30/300 keV intensities against Shi (different crystal potential; Fig. 2d/f n_z = −3 y-axis assignment ambiguous; the two axis scales differ by 3.3× in d and 3.5× in f), quantum CB corrections (exact intermediate denominators and transition currents raise CB relative to PXR by a median 1.36× (n_z = −2) and 1.19× (n_z = −3) at 30 keV; not in production), or the residual 0.5–0.8× in the 300 keV and n_z = −3 area ratios, which the independent model of Shi's own inputs shares
- **Code:** measurement only — no production code; `checks/shi_2024_matrix_elements_1103.py` against `montecarlo/spectrum/cxr/_spectrum.py::mc_cxr_spectrum` (classical hook `montecarlo/spectrum/cxr/_recoil.py::_RECOIL_HBARC_EV_ANG`); reference data from `checks/digitize_shi_2024_fig2_1103.py`
- **Source:** Shi, Wong, Huang, Wong & Kaminer, Nat. Commun. 15, 7803 (2024), DOI `10.1038/s41467-024-52050-w`, Eqs. (1)–(3), Fig. 2b/d/f, SI Eqs. (S3), (S9), (S12)–(S17); [research note](../research/physics/shi-2024-intensity-1103.md)
- **Status:** anchored
- **Checks:** classical limit: independent elements with PyRITE couplings match PyRITE per row at 5 MeV (PXR scale spread ≤ 2.1 %, CB/PXR and interference/PXR within 2.3 %); CBS on both sides collapses the QM/CL area ratio to 1.00–1.04 in the independent model and 0.89–1.07 in production (recoil Jacobian plus energy-dependent K-edge couplings evaluated at the shifted roots; 30 keV n_z = −3 lines straddle the Si K edge); digitized CL peaks on the classical roots (≤ 0.2 %), tallest QM line on the (0,±2,2) exact root (≤ 4 eV), 5 MeV QM/CL area ≈ 1 (correspondence limit)
- **Anchor:** `tests/montecarlo/spectrum/test_shi_2024_fig2_intensity.py::{test_digitized_cl_peaks_sit_on_the_classical_root,test_digitized_tallest_qm_line_is_the_0_pm2_2_root,test_shi_qm_over_cl_area_is_full_cxr_over_classical_pxr,test_sub_mev_area_excess_needs_cbs_on_one_side_only,test_mev_classical_heights_match_shi_cl}`
- **Notes:** Issue #1103. Reference curves are vector-path extractions of the publisher PDF (sha256 in `tests/data/recoil/shi-2024-fig2.json`); peaks agree with the earlier manual reads within 3 %. Shi's stated approximation `u_q ≈ u_{q_i}` (all currents v_i) suppresses CB intensity to 0.06–0.35× of the classical value (amplitude 0.25–0.59×); the resulting QM/CL area ratios are only 1.29 / 1.13 / 1.03 and cannot produce their QM. Their curves therefore imply CB with momentum-dependent currents. Fresh-context verification (2026-11-12) rederived the attribution with an absolutely normalized model of Eq. (1) (no calibration against PyRITE): production PXR, CBS and interference match its classical elements within 0.8 % at 30 keV, 300 keV and 5 MeV, and every Claim number reproduces. It returned `discrepancy` on wording only: the f′ factor (−1.78, × 0.64; 0.60 includes Debye–Waller), the CBS-on-both-sides range (production 0.89–1.07, not 1.00 ± 0.08), the axis-scale ratio, the per-n_z quantum-CB medians and the basis of the `u_q ≈ u_{q_i}` suppression. All are corrected here and in the research note; the re-check (2026-11-12) returned `rederived`, and the owner applies `anchored` with the named passing anchors. [Validation write-up](radiation-physics/shi-2024-fig2-intensity-attribution.md). Human sign-off pending.

## `quantum-shaped-cxr-interference`

- **Claim:** research reference (#1102, #527 option C). For one electron with density matrix `ρ_ij` over equal-energy plane-wave components `q_i` in a static, laterally periodic slab, the CXR rate of Shi et al. (2024) Eq. (3) is `ω Σ_σ Tr(A_σ ρ A_σ†)` with `A_σ(f,i) = Σ_{g: q_i⊥+g⊥=Q_f} M_σ(q_i,g) F(Δ_ig)`, an explicit trace over final electrons `(Q_f, E − ħω)` (Shi sign `q_f = q_i + g − k`; slab formation amplitude `F`, mismatch `Δ = q_iz + g_z − k_z − q_fz`). Consequences: (i) cross terms need `q_i⊥ − q_j⊥` in the projected reciprocal lattice, otherwise they vanish identically; (ii) the plane-wave limit is `Σ_{g⊥} |Σ_{g_z} M F|²`, which reduces to the #527 incoherent per-reflection sum with each line on its `line-recoil-resonance` root up to `O(1/(|Δg_z| L))` same-`g⊥` cross terms (≤ 1.9 % at peaks, 30 keV, 30 nm); (iii) a mixture gives `Σ ρ_ii R_i`; (iv) `(|q1⟩ + e^{iφ}|q2⟩)/√2` gives `R(φ) = (R1+R2)/2 + Re(e^{−iφ} C)`, `|C| ≤ √(R1 R2)`, phase average = mixture; (v) translating the electron by `d` maps `φ → φ + G⊥·d`, so Gaussian registration jitter multiplies the cross term by `exp(−|G⊥|²σ²/2)`. For Shi Fig. 5 (30 keV, 30 nm Si [001], θ = 110°, `q1,2 = (1, ±2, q_z) 2π/a`) the peak coherent/mixture ratio is 1.96 at φ = 0 and 0.06 at φ = π (window-integrated ±0.95). Those components are exact Si (040) electron-Bragg partners (Pendellösung `ξ = πħv_z/|U_G|` = 73 nm, Shi Yukawa potential); a two-beam Bloch treatment, with the degenerate CB pole assigned to the electron state, changes the phase table by ≤ 0.03, preserves `Re ρ_12`, and gives an unshaped Bragg-tilted entry no `cos φ` signature. Not claimed: Shi's absolute intensities or sub-MeV per-reflection weights, many-beam/probe states, X-ray dynamical diffraction, absorption, Debye–Waller, transport, any production code
- **Code:** measurement only — no production code; `checks/shaped_electron_1102.py` against `materials/recoil.py::recoil_resonance_eV` and `quantum/_longitudinal.py::slab_formation_amplitude`
- **Source:** Shi, Wong, Huang, Wong & Kaminer, Nat. Commun. 15, 7803 (2024), DOI `10.1038/s41467-024-52050-w`, main-text Eq. (3), Fig. 5, SI Eqs. (S3), (S12)–(S17), §S6–§S7; two-beam electron diffraction (standard Pendellösung, author derivation in the note §3); [research note](../research/physics/shaped-electron-cxr-1102.md)
- **Status:** rederived
- **Checks:** explicit final-state trace (not assumed); mixture equals `(R1+R2)/2` (3e-15); phase average equals mixture (3e-15); non-lattice offsets share no final state; plane-wave lines at `Δ = 0` on `recoil_resonance_eV` roots (≤ 2e-13 Å⁻¹); registration jitter sampled against `exp(−|G⊥|²σ²/2)`; two-beam mixture invariant; coherent/mixture ≤ 2 (Cauchy–Schwarz)
- **Anchor:** `checks/shaped_electron_1102.py` (measurement script; no regression test)
- **Notes:** Issue #1102; decision **defer** (note §6). The Fig. 5 ratios are consistent with Shi's "almost twice" (SI §S7). That figure shows 2.6 keV angular maps, while Fig. 5 is at 2.0 keV, so it is not the same observable and was not digitized. No shaped-electron CXR measurement exists to anchor against. The rate omits the `1/v_fz` final density of states, which changes the Fig. 5 ratios by 6e-5. The plane-wave residual of 1.9 % holds at 30 nm; it oscillates with thickness (3.6 % at 60 nm). Fresh-context verification (2026-11-11) rederived the physics and reproduced every number. It returned `discrepancy` on wording only: the Shi §S7 comparison, the attribution of the two-hole aperture to Shi, and the note §5 preparation wording. All are corrected here and in the note. No regression test pins the claim, so the status stays `rederived`. [Validation write-up](radiation-physics/quantum-shaped-cxr-interference.md). Human sign-off pending.

## `tr-interface-energy-scale`

- **Claim:** Relativistic vacuum/material single-interface TR energy scale `W ≈ alpha gamma hbar omega_p / 3` and photon roll-off scale of order `gamma hbar omega_p`
- **Code:** `docs/research/physics/transition-radiation-recommendations.md` (research claim; no production implementation)
- **Source:** PDG 2020, Passage of Particles Through Matter, §34.7.3, Eq. (34.45)
- **Status:** rederived
- **Checks:** Exact single-interface emission (`tr-detected-omission-bound` solver) with Drude `eps`, integrated over the forward hemisphere and all photon energies: `W / (alpha gamma hbar omega_p / 3)` = 0.955 at `gamma = 40`, 0.991 (0.9907) at `gamma = 197` (deficit ≈ `1.8/gamma`); matched medium (`omega_p -> 0`) gives zero
- **Anchor:** `tests/montecarlo/spectrum/test_transition_radiation.py::test_interface_energy_scale_from_the_exact_solution` (consistency with the exact solution, not an external reference)
- **Notes:** Relativistic estimate with an `O(1/gamma)` correction; must not be extrapolated to `T -> 0`. A total-energy scale only: not a photon count and not a bound on any detected band (see [Transition radiation](../physics/radiation-physics/transition-radiation.md)). Emission below `3 hbar omega_p` carries ~35% of `W` at `gamma = 40`. **2026-10-16 fresh-context verdict `rederived`** ([write-up](radiation-physics/tr-interface-energy-scale.md)): `int_0^inf [(1+2 nu^2) ln(1+nu^-2) - 2] d nu = pi/3` gives `W = alpha gamma hbar omega_p / 3`; an independent boundary solve and adaptive quadrature give 0.9167/0.9555/0.9816/0.9907 at `gamma` = 20.6/40/98.8/197; the research-note table is correct after rounding. Human sign-off pending.

## `tr-crossing-superposition`

- **Claim:** The planar-stack TR vacuum amplitude of a straight charge is the sum over its interface crossings of the stack response to the own-field jump at the crossing velocity, phased `exp[i(omega t_c - k_perp . r_perp,c)]`; on transported tracks the per-history coherent sum of those crossing amplitudes (entry, composition-changing layer boundaries, top/bottom exits) gives the TR yield per incident electron, histories incoherent
- **Code:** `montecarlo/spectrum/transition_radiation.py::interface_transition_radiation_amplitude`; `montecarlo/spectrum/transition_radiation_crossings.py::{boundary_crossings, crossing_amplitudes, crossing_transition_radiation}`; CUDA twin `montecarlo/spectrum/transition_radiation_jit_kernel.py::history_sums` (compiled from the shared C++ source constant and using `montecarlo/spectrum/transition_radiation.py::interface_response`); run-case producer `montecarlo/runner/transition_emission.py::{case_transition_spectrum, block_transition_spectrum}`
- **Source:** Linearity of the Ginzburg–Frank boundary-value solve (`tr-detected-omission-bound`); Ginzburg & Tsytovich 1990, Ch. 2; regular-radiator reference: Garibyan, Sov. Phys. JETP 33, 23 (1971); Grichine, Phys. Lett. B 525, 225 (2002), as in the Geant4 XTR Physics Reference Manual
- **Status:** rederived
- **Checks:** Required: crossing phase equals `exp(i q z_i)` on a straight line; jump sign and media indexing; entrance-face frame rotation (pi about `x`, stack reversed, interface `N - i`) and back-rotation of the vector; charge sign; `beta` from crossing kinetic energy; history grouping and chunk boundaries; normalization `E n_z^2 |sum a|^2 / (16 pi^4 Ne)`. Limits: straight-track sum equals the full solve in both senses; equal media give zero; thickness average of the coherent film equals the crossing-incoherent sum; substep invariance
- **Anchor:** `tests/montecarlo/spectrum/test_transition_radiation_crossings.py::test_regular_radiator_reproduces_the_garibian_stack_formula` (published small-angle regular-radiator density, 10 transparent foils at `gamma = 2000`, 2–40 keV, `theta <= 3/gamma`: within `2e-4` of the peak; with exact formation phases in the same stack factor, `1e-5` relative above `1e-3` of the peak); other tests in the file check consistency with the exact solve
- **Notes:** Issue #103. Locally-straight-passage approximation per crossing; deflection between crossings is bremsstrahlung. CTR is `tr-coherent-bunch` and detector integration `tr-detector-integration`; no recoil. The fused CUDA kernel (`transition_radiation_jit_kernel`) re-implements the crossing amplitude and history sum in complex128 C++; its host-C++ build of the same source agrees with `crossing_amplitudes` to 1e-11 (`tests/montecarlo/spectrum/test_transition_radiation_kernel.py`), and the device launch, reduction and CuPy parity passed all 16 hardware tests on an RTX 5080 on 2026-10-18 ([hardware evidence](radiation-physics/tr-cuda-parity.md)). The regular-radiator reproduction is a published closed form, not measured data, and transparent only: the absorbing-radiator flux form was not reproduced. **2026-10-18 fresh-context verdict `rederived`** ([write-up](radiation-physics/tr-crossing-superposition.md)): crossing phase, jump sign and indexing, the entrance-face pi rotation (reversed stack, interface `N - i`, back-rotation), charge sign, beta from kinetic energy, history-aligned chunks with `cumsum` differencing and the normalization reproduce; an independent unrotated slab solver agrees to the alpha literal (6.1e-10) on both faces, for straight lines and transported mixed-charge multi-track histories. The regular-radiator reference equals the transparent geometric series; gate margins 1.9x (small angle, 1.07e-4 of peak) and 3.4x (exact phase, 3.0e-6). Caveats applied: the `max_elements` chunk bound now holds except for a single history above it; `boundary_crossings` carries the marker. Open caveats: the locally-straight condition (no significant deflection within `1/|q - lambda|` of a crossing) is not checked at runtime; the anchor covers only a straight, transparent, normal-incidence exit face; the CuPy path and a real shell cascade were not exercised. Human sign-off pending.

## `tr-detector-integration`

- **Claim:** TR photons per incident electron in each planar-detector pixel and energy channel are the integral of the crossing-superposition density over the pixel solid angle and the channel, evaluated by composite trapezoid nodes per channel and `s x s` point sub-pixels (`A cos / r^2`), doubled one axis at a time toward the larger last change until a third of the larger last change is within `rtol` of the table peak; the lab-to-slab direction map is transport's tilt rotation
- **Code:** `montecarlo/spectrum/transition_radiation_detector.py::{detected_transition_radiation, _channel_table, _channel_nodes, _sub_rays}`
- **Source:** Quadrature of `tr-crossing-superposition`; detector rays from `_planar_geometry.planar_rays` (`positioned-filter-attenuation`)
- **Status:** rederived
- **Checks:** Required: solid-angle weights and sub-pixel ordering; trapezoid weights and shared channel edges; lab-to-slab rotation direction; the one-face condition; the `1/3` asymptotic error factor for second-order rules; an axis at its limit keeps its last measured change. Limits: a single exit interface integrates to the Ginzburg–Frank closed form; a transparent film's formation-zone fringes integrate to a dense Gauss–Legendre reference; pixel tables sum to the whole plane; tilting the slab and moving the detector by the same angle agree
- **Anchor:** `tests/montecarlo/spectrum/test_transition_radiation_detector.py` (closed form within `1e-3`, dense fringe reference within `5e-4`)
- **Notes:** Issue #103. The error estimate is asymptotic, not a bound: a fringe no level resolves is invisible to it. Point source at the lab origin (the crossings' far-field origin); incident photons only (filters, detector response and acquisition are read-time factors). Cost scales as pixels x `s^2` x energy nodes x crossings, so a large pixel grid needs coarse angular tiling or the CUDA path (not implemented).

## `tr-coherent-bunch`

- **Claim:** Coherent TR of a bunch of `N` independent electrons, per electron, is `E n_z^2 [G + (N-1)/(M-1) F (P - G)] / (16 pi^4 M)` with `G = sum_e |S_e|^2` and `P = |sum_e S_e|^2` over `M` sampled histories, `S_e` the history's crossing-superposition vector amplitude phased with its own arrival offset and transverse entry; `F = 1` for sampled offsets, an analytic form factor only for offset-free histories
- **Code:** `montecarlo/spectrum/transition_radiation_crossings.py::{crossing_transition_radiation, _bunch_power}`; `montecarlo/runner/transition_emission.py::case_transition_spectrum`
- **Source:** Distinct-pair estimator of `coherent-physical-bunch-population` (`coherent_population.pair_scale`) applied to the `tr-crossing-superposition` amplitudes; bunch form factor as in `coherent-inter-electron-decoherence`
- **Status:** rederived
- **Checks:** Required: the cross-history vector sum accumulates across chunks; the estimator's two moments (`G/M`, `(P-G)/(M(M-1))`); `N = None` means `N = M`; the analytic factor multiplies the excess only. Limits: identical in-phase histories give `1 + (N-1) F` per electron (`N^2` per point bunch); `N = 1` gives the per-history yield; sampled Gaussian `t0` and transverse offsets give `exp(-w^2 sigma_t^2 - (w sin(theta) sigma_x)^2)`; a long bunch averages to the incoherent sum; chunk invariance on transported histories
- **Anchor:** `tests/montecarlo/spectrum/test_transition_radiation_ctr.py` (exact identical-history limits to `1e-12`; sampled form factors within three standard deviations `(1 - F) sqrt(2 / (M F))` of the pair estimate); `tests/montecarlo/spectrum/test_transition_radiation_crossings.py::test_transported_yield_is_substep_and_chunk_invariant` (bunch-mode chunk invariance)
- **Notes:** [CUDA parity evidence](radiation-physics/tr-cuda-parity.md): all 16 TR/CTR hardware tests passed on an RTX 5080 on 2026-10-18; this supplements the earlier fresh-context record below. Issue #103. Electrons independent and identically distributed; transport independent of the offsets only for an analytic `form_factor`. Signed, unclipped estimate: negative bins warn (`CoherentSamplingWarning`); no sampling-error certificate. Not wired into `simulate()` or checkpoint provenance.
