TR–line interference is `tr-line-cross-term`. **2026-10-18 fresh-context verdict `rederived`** ([write-up](radiation-physics/tr-coherent-bunch.md)): estimator moments, `N = None -> M`, the cross-chunk field, the excess-only analytic `F` and the Gaussian form factor reproduce; an independent brute-force pair sum agrees to `2e-15` across `N` and chunk budgets (37 histories, three-layer absorbing stack, scrambled order, mixed charge). Caveats applied: the sampled-offset test used `sqrt(2(1-F)/(MF))`, which overstates the pair-estimate s.d. by `1/sqrt(1-F)`; the offset-independence assumption is needed only for an analytic `F`. Open caveats: `boundary_crossings` does not reject finite footprints (side exits are counted); no sampling-variance estimate; CuPy path not exercised; `physical_electrons=0` returns the self term. Human sign-off pending.

## `tr-line-cross-term`

- **Claim:** The ensemble average of the interference between TR (the `g = 0`, homogeneous-`eps` field) and a coherent PXR/CBS field of reciprocal vector `g` is multiplied by the characteristic function of the beam entry distribution at `g_parallel` (`exp(-(g_parallel sigma_perp)^2 / 2)` for a Gaussian spot of per-axis r.m.s. `sigma_perp`; cross-electron terms at most `exp(-(g_parallel sigma_perp)^2 / 4)` per pair), so the `g = 0` TR and the coherent lines are additive for `g_parallel sigma_perp >> 1`; `g` along the surface normal (symmetric Bragg, including default-orientation (00l) rows), a point spot, channeling, finite footprints near an edge and grooves are excluded
- **Code:** `docs/physics/radiation-physics/transition-radiation.md` § Interference with PXR and CBS (component-accounting decision; no cross-term implementation)
- **Source:** Lateral translation invariance of amorphous transport through a laterally infinite stack; far-field phase `exp[i(w t - (w n + g) . r)]` of `coherent-emission` and `exp[i(w t - k_perp . r_perp)]` of `tr-crossing-superposition`
- **Status:** rederived
- **Checks:** Required: both fields use the same vacuum `w n_parallel` at one detector direction, so only `g_parallel` survives in the relative phase; transport and the stack are invariant under in-plane translation; the result extends to cross-electron terms. Limits: `g_parallel = 0` and the point-spot limit keep the cross term; symmetric Laue (`g` in the surface plane) is the most suppressed
- **Anchor:** none (analytic decision)
- **Notes:** Issue #103. Surface-normal `g` (symmetric Bragg; the default (00l) HOPG/hBN rows and the central mosaic node) need the surface termination, a reconciled absolute phase of `A_PXR + A_CBS` against the Maxwell solve, and diffracted TR (DTR); none is implemented. DTR interferes with PXR for every `g` without suppression; not implemented. **2026-10-18 fresh-context verdict `discrepancy`** ([write-up](radiation-physics/tr-line-cross-term.md)): phase conventions confirmed (vacuum `w n` in both fields, slab-frame `g`, depth-only escape phase), but the stated factor was `|rho~|^2`, not `rho~`, the cross-electron factor was misidentified, and 'surface-parallel'/'Laue' should read surface-normal `g` / symmetric Bragg; DTR-PXR interference is unsuppressed for all `g`. All corrections applied to the claim and page; the re-check of the corrected text gives `rederived` (same write-up). Human sign-off pending. Escape refraction of the line field depends only on depth for planar faces and adds no in-plane phase.

## `tr-detected-omission-bound`

- **Claim:** Exact classical TR of a straight, uniform-velocity charge through a planar stack (boundary-value solve of tangential `E`/`H` continuity, any `eps`, speed, incidence, emission direction, layer count) and the per-observable omission bound `b = max_E T(E)/S(E) <= target_rse / 3` for positive linear detector responses
- **Code:** `montecarlo/spectrum/transition_radiation.py::planar_transition_radiation`, `ginzburg_frank_interface`, `medium_dielectric`, `composition_dielectric` (run-case layers: `montecarlo/runner/transition_emission.py::case_layer_eps`), `omission_tolerance`, `transition_radiation_omission`; study `checks/transition_radiation_omission.py`
- **Source:** Ginzburg & Tsytovich 1990, Ch. 2 (Ginzburg–Frank boundary problem); Jackson 3rd ed. §13.7, Eqs. 13.91–13.92; DeRaad, Tsai & Erber, Phys. Rev. D 18, 2152 (1978)
- **Status:** rederived
- **Checks:** Required: field normalization (`phi`, `E_p`, `H_p`, `1/|v_z|`), far-field factor `omega cos^2 theta |a|^2 / (16 pi^4)`, `eps` sign convention vs `chi_0`; limits: closed Ginzburg–Frank form at normal incidence (both senses), conductor image charge at oblique incidence, zero contrast, vanishing/opaque/transparent film, Jackson 13.91 limit; positivity argument for the bound and the `rse/3` rule
- **Anchor:** `tests/montecarlo/spectrum/test_transition_radiation.py` (analytic limits); decision evidence `checks/transition_radiation_omission.py`
- **Notes:** Issue #102. Decisions per application row are in [Transition radiation](../physics/radiation-physics/transition-radiation.md): keV X-rays from tens-of-keV beams omitted (`b <= 1.5e-4`, 2026-11-19); EUV, forward MeV soft X-ray, 100 MeV and multilayer radiators escalated to #103. The DTR/PXR amplitude ratio on that page is a screening estimate outside this claim. **2026-10-16 fresh-context verdict `rederived`** ([write-up](radiation-physics/tr-detected-omission-bound.md)): field normalization, far field, branch choice, Ginzburg–Frank in both senses, conductor/zero-contrast/Jackson 13.91 limits and the `eps` convention match; an independent vector multilayer solver agrees to 6e-10 (the alpha literal). Its caveats were applied before landing: non-positive source energies now give an infinite bias instead of being dropped; the `c B / L` net-line bound is stated for a continuum-only source; the crossing factor is 5, not 3, because entry and exit through one face add as amplitudes (<= 4x). The factor remains a sampled, expectation-level bound (escaping secondaries < 1 per primary asserted; finer angle grids +3.7%), covered by the margins of the omit decisions. Human sign-off pending. **2026-11-10 rerun (#1065):** the study pins the inelastic model per scene (shell-soft-hard where conduction-band shell data cover every layer, else continuous; this reproduces the pre-971df7c69 `auto` default) and gives every scene from 3 MeV a fixed coarse CXR grid; every omit/escalate decision is unchanged, but fail-side EUV and soft-X-ray margins shifted (tens-of-keV EUV about 2x, MeV EUV up to 40x, WSe₂ 0.3--1 keV 1.3 → 0.25 with E* 743 → 633 eV), attributed in #1070 to intended changes: Ter-Mikaelian suppression in coupled BremsLib (`f7fa036bb`, #439; tens-of-keV and MeV EUV) and the electron-fraction-weighted SBETHE compound shell correction (`1f22f89c8`, `sbethe-corrected-v2`; WSe₂/MoS₂). **2026-11-19 rerun (#1070):** no lines scored (`cxr_source="none"`, #1327, so no lattice-share correction); the default restricted straggling for shell transport (#642) moves the shell scenes (Si 30 keV film EUV 30 → 34, WSe₂ 0.3--1 keV 0.25 → 0.50), and `straggling=False` reproduces 2026-11-10 exactly. Decisions unchanged; the 10 µm Si 30 keV 0.3--1 keV bound (1.6674e-2 against 1.6667e-2) is at the tolerance and counts as passing.

## `coherent-line-spectrum`

- **Claim:** `\|A_PXR + A_CBS\|²` segment-sum line spectrum, exact mosaic average
- **Code:** `montecarlo/spectrum/cxr/_spectrum.py::mc_cxr_spectrum`
- **Source:** Feranchuk–Spence 2000 Eq.(10),(12); Zhai 2025
- **Status:** rederived
- **Checks:** units; prefactor, squaring, summation order, per-electron normalization, and mosaic weighting reproduced term for term (absolute normalization to `6.7e-10` relative, whose entire budget is the repository's truncated fine-structure literal); incoherence structure confirmed by exact `1/N` subdivision falloff, bitwise reflection additivity, and invariance of the per-electron spectrum under electron replication; mosaic average is a unit-weight Gauss–Hermite intensity average collapsing to the perfect crystal as `η→0`
- **Anchor:** `src/pyrite/validation/_anchor_conditions.py::single_segment_anchor` (not a CI-green regression test on this row's normalization; it compares against `feranchuk_line_flux`, the `discrepancy` `closed-form-flux` row)
- **Notes:** interference is non-separable; highest priority; **2026-07-11**: tilt convention flipped to Zhai's positive θ (`docs/physics/geometry/tilt-convention.md`) — simulated **intensities** at positive-θ grids differ from the old negative-θ (mirror) outputs by ~2× at peak; any intensity-dependent check of this row needs re-verification in fresh context. **2026-08-15**: fresh-context re-derivation matches with no divergent factor, sign, exponent, or unit. This row certifies how `\|A\|²` is *used*, not `A` itself (see `cbs-amplitude`). Integrated yield is exact but **line peak height is segmentation dependent**, so absolute peak comparisons against a paper inherit an implicit dependence on the transport's segment-length distribution. The prefactor is frozen at `ω_res` rather than the grid frequency — consistent with the existing narrow-line freezing of `χ_g`/`U_g`/`μ`, odd about line centre, cancels to first order on integration. `components=True` is **not** an additive split: `spec_pxr + spec_cbs` differs from `spec` by the interference term (+22% at the checked point), and the docstring says so only for the coherent path. The `line-energy-dispersion` harmonic sign is resolved (#338) as a relabelling. Its Friedel-mate coupling fix changes `|A|²` only for noncentrosymmetric crystals with `f″`. Human sign-off pending. [validation write-up](radiation-physics/coherent-line-spectrum.md)

## `coherent-emission`

- **Claim:** opt-in phased segment sum `dN/dE dΩ ∝ \|Σ_j A_j·exp{i[ω(t_abs,j−n̂·r_j)−g·r_j]}\|²`; intra-electron plus inter-electron/superradiant cross terms and Gaussian inter-electron form factor `exp[−(ωσ_z)²]`
- **Code:** `montecarlo/spectrum/cxr/_spectrum.py::mc_cxr_spectrum` (`coherent=True`); profile policy `campaign/config.py::default_settings` (incoherent default) and `campaign/profiles.py::profile_run_settings` (`"coherent"`/`"both"`, surfaced by `pyrite profile set --emission` and `pyrite profile add/remove --coherent`)
- **Source:** Feranchuk–Spence 2000; Zhai 2025; far-field phased-array sum; Gaussian characteristic function
- **Status:** rederived
- **Checks:** units/sign; repository `S(+g)` ↔ `χ_g exp(−ig·r)` mapping; straight-trajectory phase stationarity at `ω=v·g/(1−v·n̂)`; full/cutoff sinc paths; mosaic placement; self/coincident/qualified decoherent limits
- **Anchor:** `tests/montecarlo/coherent/test_coherent_emission.py`
- **Notes:** **2026-10-05 (#338):** the `S(+g) ↔ χ_g exp(−ig·r)` derivation took `exp[i(k·r−ωt)]`. The module's `f′+if″` is `exp(+iωt)`, so with `f″≠0` the amplitude must use `conj(χ(−hkl))`, now supplied by `_reflection_tables` (`line-energy-dispersion`). The phase is unchanged. Fresh-context derivation matches exact phase with no divergent sign/factor. Broad per-electron `t0` removes only inter-electron terms, giving `Σ_e\|Σ_{j∈e}E_j\|²`; intra-electron segment coherence survives. Coherent/incoherent identities remain distinct; physical-charge/macro-particle weighting is a separate unfinished claim. **2026-08-19:** the inter-electron form factor here covers the longitudinal `σ_z` only — the transverse partner for a finite `beam_fwhm_mm` is missing and is the `transverse-bunch-form-factor` `discrepancy` row, which blocks sign-off on this row for any run with a finite spot. **2026-08-21:** both offsets (longitudinal and transverse) are now blended analytically — see `coherent-inter-electron-decoherence`, which supersedes the ad hoc per-offset treatment described here with the combined closed form and its implementation. [validation write-up](radiation-physics/coherent-emission.md)

## `coherent-segment-midpoint-time`

- **Claim:** coherent finite-segment phase pairs stored midpoint position with midpoint transport age `t_mid = t_ang + L_ang/(2β)` while preserving `t_ang` as segment-start age
- **Code:** `montecarlo/spectrum/cxr/_spectrum.py::mc_cxr_spectrum` (`coherent=True`)
- **Source:** centered constant-amplitude finite-time integral; Feranchuk–Spence 2000 Eqs. (8), (10), (12)–(14) as cited under `coherent-emission`; fresh first-principles centered-integral derivation (original equation numbering not independently inspected in the 2026-10-20 pass)
- **Status:** rederived
- **Checks:** units; zero-length and transparent limits; phase sign; midpoint clock and escape-piece offsets; exact subdivision with the same frozen coefficient and continuous clock, complete affine escape partition and no active piece-dependent cutoff. Absorbed coherent/incoherent self-terms differ pointwise; Parseval equality in detuning becomes equality in energy only with the matching Jacobian, frozen prefactors and full window
- **Anchor:** `tests/montecarlo/coherent/test_coherent_emission.py::test_straight_flight_is_invariant_to_two_half_segments`; `::test_single_segment_coherent_equals_incoherent_self_term` (tolerance-based, not exact absorbed equality); `tests/montecarlo/coherent/test_coherent_formation_absorption.py`; `tests/montecarlo/spectrum/test_xray_dispersion.py::test_dispersive_formation_integral_has_the_derivative_jacobian`
- **Notes:** **2026-10-20 fresh-context verdict: rederived.** Midpoint pairing, piece clock offsets and the current full formation integral match with no factor, sign, unit or convention discrepancy. Both midpoint subdivision routes passed. The historical vacuum-only / midpoint escape approximation had first-order splitting residuals (7.8e-6 of peak at 40 Å); #181 replaced it with integrated escape phase and damping (historical residual 1.8e-15). Those old errors are not a current kernel limitation. Full dispersion alone does not spoil exact partitioning, but its derivative Jacobian prevents unconditional energy-area equality. Original-paper retrieval was unavailable; the verifier derived from the centered-integral contract before source inspection. [Current derivation, conditions and historical provenance](radiation-physics/coherent-segment-midpoint-time.md). Human sign-off remains in #277.

## `coherent-formation-absorption`

- **Claim:** the phased-field line reductions (coherent route; flight-grouped incoherent reduction) give each linear escape piece the exact formation integral under absorption and the in-medium escape leg: field `t_L e^{iΦ_c} F`, `F = e^{−τ_c/2} sinh(w)/w = [e^{−τ_end/2}e^{iv} − e^{−τ_start/2}e^{−iv}]/(2w)`, `w = iv − q`, `q = (τ_end − τ_start)/4`, `v = a_vac(E − E_vac) − δ(E)ω(E)ΔL_esc/2` on the vacuum sinc centre/width
- **Code:** `montecarlo/spectrum/cxr/_formation.py`; `montecarlo/spectrum/cxr/_setup.py::_prepare_spectrum` (escape-piece rows); `montecarlo/spectrum/cxr/_batched.py::_batched_coherent_block`, `montecarlo/spectrum/cxr/_per_hkl.py` (coherent and flight-grouped), host flight-grouped reduction `montecarlo/spectrum/cxr/_cpu_reduce.py::accumulate_grouped_formation`; CUDA `montecarlo/spectrum/coherent_jit_kernel.py::run_coherent_reduction_kernel`, `montecarlo/spectrum/coherent_prologue_jit_kernel.py::run_coherent_prologue_kernel`, `montecarlo/spectrum/coherent_stream_jit_kernel.py::run_coherent_field_accumulation_kernel`, `montecarlo/spectrum/coherent_grouped_jit_kernel.py::run_coherent_grouped_intensity_kernel`
- **Source:** Feranchuk–Spence 2000 Eqs. (8), (10), (12)–(14) finite-time field with the Beer–Lambert amplitude `e^{−τ/2}` (`self-absorption`) and the escape-leg phase `−δωL_esc` (`xray-in-medium-propagation-phase`) integrated along the piece
- **Status:** rederived
- **Checks:** units; `μ → 0` gives the sinc; uniform damping gives the midpoint model; opaque end finite; `\|F\| ≤ 1/\|v\|`; quadrature agreement to 1e-9; Parseval `∫\|F\|²dv = π⟨e^{−τ}⟩` (the coherent self-term integrates to the `segment-escape-average` yield); coherent spectrum split invariant under absorption and refraction on the batched slab, batched finite-box face switch and per-reflection routes; single-segment coherent peak on the closed-form escape-path root; CUDA formation branches against the NumPy reference (gated)
- **Anchor:** `tests/montecarlo/coherent/test_coherent_formation_absorption.py`; `tests/montecarlo/spectrum/test_grouped_formation_reduce.py`; `tests/montecarlo/coherent/test_coherent_emission.py::test_straight_flight_is_invariant_to_two_half_segments`; `tests/montecarlo/spectrum/test_xray_dispersion.py::test_single_segment_coherent_line_sits_on_the_escape_path_root`; `tests/montecarlo/spectrum/test_substep_invariance.py::test_incoherent_cxr_converges_under_substep_refinement`; `tests/montecarlo/coherent/test_coherent_formation_absorption_cuda.py` (CUDA-gated)
- **Notes:** Issue #181, second slice. Replaces the midpoint escape amplitude and the real sinc of the phased-field routes. The coherent line centre is the root of `v = 0` -- for a flat exit face the Snell-refracted root (`δ/(−n_z)` per depth) -- not the bulk `1 − Re n (v·n̂)` root of `xray-in-medium-resonance`, which would count the medium twice inside a piece and break split invariance; amplitudes stay frozen at the bulk `E_res` and the incoherent route keeps the bulk root, so coherent and incoherent line centres differ at `O(δ)` (0.21 eV of 1600 eV at hopg 002, 100 keV, 119°). The two-half split residual fell from 7.8e-6 to 1.8e-15 of peak; the flight-grouped substep ladder converges ~30× tighter. `CXR_ESCAPE_MODEL = "segment-mean-v2-coherent-formation"` forks line spectra. All CUDA kernels transpile and compile to PTX without a device; on hardware (RTX 5080, 2026-09-26) the CUDA-gated parity tests and the CUDA-backend run of the CPU formation tests pass, the split-invariance device bound scaling with the flight phase `k0 L / β` (float32 piece ages). **2026-09-26 fresh-context verification:** matches term for term (start/end ordering, refractive sign, vacuum centre/width, Parseval, limits, series/small-q branches, piece ages and midpoints, `sinc_cutoff` window); the escape-path centre is the first-order Snell root for any planar exit face (exact Snell 1600.5859 eV vs escape-path 1600.5858 eV vs bulk 1600.3781 eV), so the vacuum choice is correct and the bulk-root incoherent route is the less accurate one (residual $O(\delta)$ offset). Independent brute-force field quadrature matches `mc_cxr_spectrum` to 1e-9 (slab, box face switch, batched and per-hkl, 1/3/16 pieces, flight-grouped, cutoff); CUDA formation branches match the NumPy reference algebraically. Minor: the series truncation is $\approx q^4/8$ ($1.2\times10^{-13}$), not the stated $O(|w|^4/120)<10^{-14}$; energy-integrated coherent/incoherent yields differ by an $O(\delta)$ Jacobian ($2\times10^{-4}$), not only grid truncation. Human sign-off pending. **2026-10-05 (#187):** the historical bulk-root offset is removed; both routes and couplings use the escape-path Snell root. Full δ(E) stays, leaving Jacobian `J = D − E δ′ v·∇L`; exact energy-integrated equality is a constant-index limit, pinned alongside the dispersive residual by `test_dispersive_formation_integral_has_the_derivative_jacobian`. **2026-11-12 (#1152):** the host flight-grouped reduction moved from dense `(rows, n_E)` profiles plus `np.add.reduceat` to the fused numba kernel `accumulate_grouped_formation`, which evaluates the same `F` expressions (closed form and series branch) and phase one element at a time; it matches the former dense formulation to rtol 1e-11 and the per-flight coherent-sum definition check to 1e-9. Implementation change only; equation and status unchanged. The float32 CuPy route (same per-flight segmented intensity kernel as the coherent streaming route) references every row's phase terms to its flight's first row in float64 -- exact, since a per-flight phase cancels in |field|^2 -- from float64 twins of the row midpoints and ages that device staging keeps beside its float32 rows (`cxr/_position_twins.py`); without them |r| ~ 2e7 Angstrom footprints quantize `n_hat.r` by ~1 Angstrom (~0.7 rad within a flight). On hardware (Radeon 8060S, 2026-11-13, jobs 12247/12248 vs host 12249/12250, same source) the HOPG/Si half-cap replays match the host to 2.4e-5/8.8e-5 of peak with integrals to 1e-6; NVIDIA and AMD agreed to 3e-7 before the fix. [derivation record](radiation-physics/coherent-formation-absorption.md)

## `temporal-intensity-profile`

- **Claim:** the opt-in line temporal profile is `I(t) = Σ_rows w |E_row(t)|²` with the unitary transform `E(t) = (2πħc)^{-1/2} ∫ E(E) e^{−iEt/ħc} dE`, so `∫ I dt = ∫ d²N/dE dΩ dE`; incoherent lines are boxes of duration `D t_L = 2ħc a` centred on `d = t_abs − n̂·r` carrying their sinc² mass `wπ/a`; coherent rows are FFT'd on a conjugate uniform energy grid, with bunch offsets averaged as `Σ_e|S_e|² + |F⁻¹[χ Σ S^geo]|² − Σ_e|F⁻¹[χ S^geo_e]|²`
- **Code:** `montecarlo/spectrum/cxr/_temporal.py`; hooks in `montecarlo/spectrum/cxr/_per_hkl.py::{_accumulate_reflection,_temporal_coherent_row}`; `montecarlo/runner/case_driver.py::_spectrum_case_impl`
- **Source:** Fourier transform of the `coherent-emission` field (Feranchuk–Spence 2000 Eqs. (8), (10), (12)–(14) finite-time factor); Parseval/Plancherel; `coherent-formation-absorption` piece Parseval; `coherent-inter-electron-decoherence` offset characteristic function
- **Status:** anchored
- **Checks:** units; Parseval against the stored spectrum for both policies; single flight is a box at the arrival time with rms `D t_L/√12` from both routes; two flights give two separated equal pulses; a long bunch adds its arrival variance and makes the policies agree; exact box quadrature; option off leaves spectra and identity unchanged
- **Anchor:** `tests/montecarlo/coherent/test_temporal_profile.py`
- **Notes:** Issue #292. Narrow-band (amplitude frozen at resonance); incoherent boxes are flat (row-mean transmission) and drop the `δL_esc` group delay; the coherent profile is band-limited to the line axis. 2026-10-01 fresh-context verification: matches term for term (sign/causality, box duration and mass, FFT phase bookkeeping, units, ⟨e^{-τ}⟩ and its q→0 limit, offset blend and its Parseval reduction, `temporal_tau_geo ≡ d_all_geom`); independent quadrature agrees (coherent row 2e-4, Parseval 6e-5, offset ensemble within MC noise). Minor: incoherent boxes use the bulk-root width (O(δ) duration difference, Parseval-consistent with the spectrum); energy-loss substep boxes tile only to O(Δβ t_L), mass exact; finite-footprint time shape assumes transport drew t0 with `longitudinal_rms_fs`. Anchored by the regression tests above. Human sign-off pending (#277). [derivation record](radiation-physics/temporal-intensity-profile.md)

## `cross-reflection-coherence`

- **Claim:** the coherent segment sum drops the cross-reflection terms of `\|Σ_g F_g\|²`, keeping `Σ_g \|F_g\|²` — distinct reflections (and mosaic orientations) add as intensities while the segment sum inside a row keeps its phase
- **Code:** `montecarlo/spectrum/cxr/_spectrum.py::mc_cxr_spectrum` (`coherent=True`, the per-`(reflection, orientation)` squaring); inherited by `montecarlo/spectrum/coherent_jit_kernel.py::run_coherent_reduction_kernel`
- **Source:** none — an approximation to the phased-array sum derived under `coherent-emission`, not a separate equation
- **Status:** filtered
- **Checks:** per-reflection coherent spectra re-sum to the shipped all-reflections call to `1.2e-07`–`2.7e-07` of peak (float32 reassociation), so the split is a faithful decomposition. Two independent suppression mechanisms measured over hopg 30 keV/500 nm, h-BN 30 keV/921 nm, and hopg 100 keV/10 µm: the Cauchy–Schwarz bound `2√(S_g S_g')/Σ_g S_g` is `9.4e-04`–`1.4e-02` at the line centres of the forward harmonics `(0,0,2)`/`(0,0,4)` that carry the yield (the anti-parallel `(0,0,±)` partners carry `≤1.8e-05` of peak and their own maxima sit where the bound is vacuous at `1.2`–`1.5`), `9.3e-02`/`1.9e-01`/`4.7e-01` at the worst bin above `1e-3` of peak (all of them inter-line valleys carrying `1`–`3e-3` of peak), and `1.0e-02`/`1.4e-02`/`1.6e-02` of the integrated yield; the independent reciprocal-lattice decorrelation factor `\|⟨exp(−i Δg·r)⟩\|` is `8.3e-03`/`8.4e-03`/`4.0e-02`, consistent with the `1/√n_seg` random walk that makes the dropped term zero-mean rather than merely bounded
- **Anchor:** `checks/cross_reflection_coherence.py` (diagnostic, not a CI-green regression test)
- **Notes:** Closes the long-standing `mc_cxr_spectrum` docstring assertion that "their resonances are spectrally separated, so cross-g coherence is negligible", which was previously an in-code `TODO` with no ledger row. Measured only on the catalog's basal-plane families `{(0,0,±2),(0,0,±4)}`, whose harmonics are separated by roughly their own resonance energy — a reflection set with near-degenerate resonances at the chosen observation angle is **not** covered and would break the approximation. The bound is worst-case in relative phase and the decorrelation factor is a Monte Carlo quantity; the two columns are read together, not multiplied. Promotion to `rederived` needs a fresh-context derivation carrying the decorrelation factor explicitly rather than the bound used here. [validation write-up](radiation-physics/cross-reflection-coherence.md)

## `transverse-bunch-form-factor`

- **Claim:** for a collimated Gaussian spot of lab covariance `Σ` on a face tilted to the beam, with each electron's `t0` carrying its vacuum face-arrival delay `s*/β` (`s* = p0·b`), the cross-electron average of offset-free fields is `F_perp = exp(-Kᵀ Σ_f K)`, `K = (Q - ω b/β)_xy = ω(n̂ - b/β)_xy + g_xy`, `Σ_f = J Σ Jᵀ` with `J = ∂p0_xy/∂(u,v)` (equivalently `exp(-κᵀΣκ)`, `κ = -Jᵀ K`); zero tilt recovers `exp[-(q⊥σ⊥)²]`, `q⊥ = (ω n̂ + g)⊥`. Finite footprints pair offset-free fields with `F_z F_perp` per row. Historical (2026-08-19) claim: the coherent sum applied no transverse form factor and evaluated one sampled realization
- **Code:** `montecarlo/spectrum/coherent_transverse.py::{transverse_spot,transverse_form_factor,transverse_envelope,require_footprint_support}`; `montecarlo/spectrum/cxr/_setup.py::_prepare_spectrum` (finite-footprint offset-free fields); `montecarlo/spectrum/cxr/_per_hkl.py::_row_decoherence_factor`; `montecarlo/spectrum/cxr/_temporal.py::coherent_offset_chi`; `montecarlo/spectrum/coherent_windows.py::_row_decoherence`; `montecarlo/transport/beam_entry.py::{beam_entry_record,face_arrival_delay_ang}` (applied in transport's `simulate_trajectories` and the electron-block `finalize_population_fields`); projection geometry under `grazing-beam-projection`
- **Source:** Gaussian characteristic function of a phase linear in the lab offset; ray–plane intersection and constant-velocity vacuum flight for `p0` and `s*`; translation invariance of the target field (laterally infinite slab, or spot well inside a finite footprint). No literature equation
- **Status:** anchored
- **Checks:** **2026-10-08 (#370):** units (`K` in 1/Å, `Σ_f` in Å²); zero tilt `→ exp(-Q⊥ᵀΣQ⊥)`; `Σ→0 → 1`; `K = 0` (phase matched) `→ 1` for any spot although `Q⊥ ≠ 0`; closed form equals an independent ray-plane construction and a 400k-draw brute-force offset average (isotropic and elliptical spots); reducer reproduces `|S|²[1+(N-1)F_z F_perp]` on tilted identical tracks; window envelope nonincreasing and `≥ F_perp`. units; the `σ⊥→0` limit returns the point source exactly. **Fails realization independence:** single-`n̂` coherent peak height scatters `std/mean` `0.31`–`0.41` across transport seeds at spots of 1 µm / 50 µm / 1 mm (min/max `0.32`–`0.40`), against a `0.074` Monte Carlo counting-noise floor measured on the incoherent path from the same trajectories. The contrast does **not** fall with electron count, as speckle contrast from randomly phased emitters is `N`-independent. Seed-averaging recovers a spot-independent enhancement of `2.83`–`3.25` over incoherent at `N_e = 300` (`2.80`–`3.74` at `N_e = 80`), stable in both spot size and electron count — the intra-electron floor the form factor would give deterministically. Angular integration does not recover it numerically: face-integral L1 error against an `n_side=11` reference plateaus at `11.3%` by `n_side=9` (`49.0%→4.1%` for the point source, `81.2%→11.3%` at a 50 µm spot) where the incoherent path converges `5.47%→0.01%`
- **Anchor:** `tests/montecarlo/coherent/test_coherent_transverse_tilt.py` (`::test_tilted_gaussian_spot_matches_brute_force_offset_average`, `::test_phase_matched_tilt_is_fully_coherent_despite_transverse_momentum`, `::test_tilted_finite_footprint_reducer_applies_the_analytic_transverse_factor`); `tests/montecarlo/coherent/test_coherent_emission.py::test_finite_footprint_transverse_offsets_need_a_recorded_spot`; historical diagnostic `checks/coherent_transverse_coherence.py`
- **Notes:** **2026-08-19 DISCREPANCY.** Distinct from, and much larger than, the far-field curvature term: the far-field expansion keeps the linear retardation exactly and drops only `omega r⊥²/2R`, which is `0.066` rad at the 400 mm Timepix distance for this case's 6.5 µm emitting volume — but `0.87` rad at the 30 mm `detector_directions` default and `2.6` rad at 10 mm, so short working distances need `r⊥ ≪ sqrt(λR/π)` checked rather than assumed (bounded in [Coherent PXR and CBS radiation](../physics/radiation-physics/coherent-radiation.md)). Tiling the detector face is **not** a viable substitute for the analytic average: the speckle angular scale `λ/D⊥ ≈ 2e-04` rad against `Δθ ≈ 2°` puts ~180 fringes across the face, so convergence needs `n_side ≈ 180` (~32000 directions per case). `beam_fwhm_mm=None` is not a safe fallback either — it is the fully degenerate point source whose cross-electron terms are maximally constructive, giving an enhancement linear in the simulated electron count (`33.2×` at `N_e=80`, `107.5×` at `N_e=300`) and so a property of the sampling rather than the physics. This row invalidated the `simulate_trajectories` docstring assertion that a finite spot has "zero effect on the emitted spectrum", which was scoped to the incoherent policy in the same change. Integrated flux is far less affected than lineshape (`≤0.6%` across `n_side` for the point source); the peak height and width are what move. **2026-08-21: implemented and promoted to `rederived`** — see `coherent-inter-electron-decoherence` for the combined (longitudinal + transverse) closed form, its fresh-context re-derivation, and the implementation that replaces the single-realization sum with the analytic ensemble average described here. [validation write-up](radiation-physics/transverse-bunch-form-factor.md) **2026-10-08 (#370):** finite-footprint fields are now offset-free and averaged analytically; the previous conditional-on-sampled-transverse treatment left `(N-1)F_z(P-G)` dominated by random transverse phases (SLURM 1150 `CoherentSamplingError`). Transport now adds the face-arrival delay `s*/β` to analytic-spot `t0` (GDF beams already carried their drift); the pulse front is perpendicular to the beam. On the tilted HOPG (002) geometries the delay multiplies the transverse exponent by `12.0` (45°/135°, 60 keV) and `1.67` (5°/0°, 30 keV). Refusals: no recorded spot, position–slope correlated Twiss (`α≠0`), energy spread on a tilted face, any missed entry, or face spot `6σ` plus offset-free emission/escape reach beyond the footprint half-width; all apply only where `F_z > 0`. Windows use the nonincreasing bound `F_z(E) sup_{E'≥E} F_perp(E')`; the full-axis audit relaxes its lower factor bound to zero; flat omission keeps its conservative `F_z` certificate. Coherent identity `physical-distinct-pairs-v2`, window revision 8. Fresh-context verification (2026-10-08): independent derivation written before reading code matches `F_perp = exp(-Kᵀ J Σ Jᵀ K)` term for term with no divergent factor, sign or unit; independent Rodrigues basis and ray-plane construction reproduce `beam_entry_basis`, `project_beam_entry`, `face_arrival_path_ang` and `face_arrival_delay_ang`; 400k-draw Monte Carlo over 6 random tilts/azimuths and 3 energies agrees within `2.2e-3`; omitting the delay gives e.g. `7e-5` against `0.69`; window envelope never below the true supremum and nonincreasing; refusals sound. Residual scope: nominal beam axis (as `grazing-beam-projection`), footprint reach measured at segment midpoints (covered by the 6σ margin), tilted infinite slab with energy spread not refused (F≈0 for µm–mm spots), CUDA runtime parity not exercised. Human sign-off pending (#277). **2026-10-09:** reduced `hopg_short`-class smoke (HOPG 60 keV, 10 µm, 45°/135°, 0.1 mm, 1 as, 1 pC, 200 samples) completes on CUDA float64 without `CoherentSamplingError` (SLURM 1169, `73ccbfe0`): 5,482,423 coordinates, spectrum 14,367 s, coherent/incoherent yield 1.00012; record `check-records/coherent-physical-population/short-bunch-370.json`.

## `coherent-inter-electron-decoherence`

- **Claim:** the coherent segment sum's inter-electron cross terms are blended analytically instead of summed as one stochastic realization: `⟨|E_tot|²⟩ = (1-F)·Σ_e|S_e|² + F·|Σ_e S_e|²`, where `S_e` is electron `e`'s own coherent segment sum evaluated at its offset-free position/time, and `F = exp[-(ωσ_z)² - (q⊥σ⊥)²]` is the joint longitudinal×transverse decoherence factor — exactly the product of the two single-offset form factors already on record (`coherent-emission`'s `exp[-(ωσ_z)²]`, `transverse-bunch-form-factor`'s `exp[-(q⊥σ⊥)²]`), because the two offsets are drawn independently (separate RNG child streams) so their joint characteristic function factorizes. Implemented via the EMPIRICAL characteristic function of the actual per-electron offset population (`F = |mean_e exp(i·(ωt0_e − q⊥·Δr⊥,e))|²`, from `initial_t0_ang`/`initial_r_ang`) rather than a per-policy closed-form `σ`, so it needs no new resolution logic for `bunch_length_fs`, `long_offsets_fs`, `compressed`, `microtrain`, or elliptical/Courant–Snyder transverse spots, and converges to the closed form via ordinary `1/√Nₑ` statistics instead of the non-converging speckle the naive sum shows
- **Code:** `montecarlo/spectrum/cxr/_spectrum.py::mc_cxr_spectrum` (`coherent=True`, both the per-hkl `_accumulate_per_hkl` path and the batched `(n_seg, N_g)` path — shared `_row_decoherence_factor`/`_coherent_electron_grouped_row` helpers); all three float32 CUDA-JIT fast paths carry the blend natively. The flat and grouped reductions reuse the same accumulating kernels, and issue #60 adds one launch-uniform `sinc_cutoff` branch to both CUDA reducers so the same segment window is applied before either square. The streaming path blends per row in plain CuPy array math instead of `finalize_coherent_fields`, whose fused collapse would sum rows before `F` could multiply them.
- **Source:** independent fresh-context derivation (per-electron phase factoring + Gaussian characteristic function, recovering both `coherent-emission`'s and `transverse-bunch-form-factor`'s single-offset results as the `σ_z→0`/`σ⊥→0` special cases); no published source beyond the two rows it combines
- **Status:** rederived
- **Checks:** units (all dimensionless products in the `c=1`, Å convention); both limiting cases (`σ_z,σ⊥→0 → F→1 →` exact `N²` coherent limit; `ωσ_z≫1` and/or `q⊥σ⊥≫1 → F→0 →` intra-electron floor, "and/or" because `F` is a product of two independent exponentials); `test_coherent_decoherence_blend_matches_reference_formula` checks the actual blended output against an independently-computed reference built ENTIRELY from already-validated, decoherence-inactive `mc_cxr_spectrum` sub-calls (the fully-coherent flat term and the per-electron floor) plus the empirical `F` computed independently in the test — not a self-consistency check against the same code; `test_coherent_decoherence_inactive_by_default` pins bit-for-bit equality with the pre-existing path whenever no offset population is supplied (every pre-existing fixture/profile)
- **Anchor:** `tests/montecarlo/coherent/test_coherent_emission.py::test_coherent_decoherence_blend_matches_reference_formula`, `::test_coherent_decoherence_inactive_by_default`, `::test_long_gaussian_bunch_supports_finite_footprint`, `::test_coherent_decoherence_blend_holds_on_the_per_hkl_route`, `::test_grooved_transverse_offsets_refuse_the_empirical_blend`, `::test_grooved_transverse_offsets_refuse_the_analytic_average`, `::test_grooved_transverse_offsets_keep_complete_field_pairs`; CUDA paths: `tests/montecarlo/spectrum/test_xray_dispersion_cuda.py::test_coherent_decoherence_blend_matches_reference_formula_on_device`, `::test_coherent_decoherence_jit_paths_match_the_generic_fallback`, `::test_coherent_decoherence_jit_paths_support_sinc_cutoff`, `::test_coherent_decoherence_gaussian_bunch_finite_footprint_uses_jit_blend`, `::test_coherent_decoherence_inactive_device_dispatch_is_bit_for_bit` (driven through `::test_coherent_decoherence_device_suite`, which re-runs them with the session's CPU backend pin lifted)
- **Notes:** **2026-08-21.** Landed from issue #57 (originally "coherent bunch length set by radiation line wavelength" — that symptom was already fixed; investigation found this was the actual cause of persistent, N-independent "coherent enhancement" on any run with a finite spot or bunch, e.g. the `hopg_hbn` default profile's `gaussian_200fs` beam, which sets both together). The combined offset-free-amplitude factorization remains scoped to the infinite slab. Finite footprints now use the separately ledgered `finite-footprint-longitudinal-decoherence` conditional average: sampled transverse amplitude/phase stays coupled while the independent Gaussian arrival time blends continuously between short-bunch enhancement and the long-bunch grouped floor. Other exclusions remain: correlated `t0`–`Δr⊥` and non-i.i.d. offsets across electrons. Elliptical and Courant–Snyder transverse spots remain covered by the empirical characteristic function for the infinite-slab branch. **2026-09-19:** issue #60 removed the `sinc_cutoff` implementation exclusion on the CPU/CuPy and CUDA-JIT routes. **GPU parity:** native CUDA-JIT support exists on all three fast paths. Existing RTX 5080 measurements remain: the streaming blend is `0.22`–`0.26×` at 2 rows, `2.0`–`5.8×` faster at 18 rows, and `5.3`–`10.2×` at 50 rows. Remaining performance follow-ups: fused segmented reduction and a calibrated dispatch heuristic. The blazed-groove transverse ensemble average remains unresolved. [validation write-up](radiation-physics/coherent-inter-electron-decoherence.md) **2026-10-08 (#370):** on a tilted face each analytic-spot electron's `t0` now includes its face-arrival delay `s*/β`, so the empirical phase `ω t0_e − q⊥·Δr⊥,e` (from `initial_t0_ang`/`initial_r_ang`) carries the delay term without code change here; its expectation is `transverse-bunch-form-factor`'s `exp(-Kᵀ Σ_f K)` with `K = (Q − ω b/β)_xy`. On the tilted HOPG (002) geometries the delay multiplies the transverse exponent by `12.0` (45°/135°, 60 keV) and `1.67` (5°/0°, 30 keV); both exponents are already `≫1` for µm–mm spots, so `F` stays numerically zero there. The finite-footprint exclusion is replaced by offset-free fields with analytic `F_z F_perp`. Fresh-context verification (2026-10-08): with `t0_e = t0 + s*_e/β` and `s* = b_xy·p0_xy` the empirical phase equals `ω t0 − K·p0_xy` per electron; its expectation `F_z F_perp + (1 − F_z F_perp)/N_e` converges to `F_z F_perp`; empirical estimator agrees with Monte Carlo to `1e-9`; no double counting of the delay in block joins, `F_z`, or finite-footprint offset-free phases. Human sign-off pending (#277).

## `finite-footprint-longitudinal-decoherence`

- **Claim:** conditional on one sampled finite-footprint transverse/transport realization, independent Gaussian longitudinal arrival offsets give `⟨|Σ_e S_e exp(iωt_e)|²⟩_(t|S) = (1-F_z)Σ_e|S_e|² + F_z|Σ_eS_e|²`, with `F_z=exp[-(ωcσ_t)²]`; each `S_e` retains its actual transverse phase, hit/miss history, escape distance, attenuation, and transport state
- **Code:** `montecarlo/spectrum/cxr/_spectrum.py::mc_cxr_spectrum` (`coherent=True`, finite `crystal_width_ang`/`crystal_height_ang` branch)
- **Source:** Gaussian characteristic function and conditional expectation; implementation-context derivation only, no external source
- **Status:** rederived
- **Checks:** dimensionless `ωcσ_t`; `σ_t→0` recovers the full sampled finite-crystal coherent field; `ωcσ_t→∞` recovers the per-electron coherent floor; one electron is invariant for every `F_z`
- **Anchor:** `tests/montecarlo/coherent/test_coherent_emission.py::test_finite_footprint_partially_coherent_longitudinal_blend`; CUDA: `tests/montecarlo/spectrum/test_xray_dispersion_cuda.py::test_coherent_decoherence_gaussian_bunch_finite_footprint_uses_jit_blend`
- **Notes:** This removes the unsupported-grid failure for short Gaussian bunches such as `hopg_short` without discarding finite geometry. It averages longitudinal timing exactly under the stated independence assumption, but does not average over transverse bunch/transport realizations; partially coherent finite-footprint output can retain transverse diffraction/speckle. Physical-charge/macro-particle weighting remains a separate unfinished claim. Fresh-context verification completed 2026-10-05: the conditional Gaussian average, grouped/flat blend, retained finite geometry and inspected CUDA call structure match term for term. Independent three-electron Gaussian quadrature agrees to 1.14e-13 absolute; the one-electron finite-prism limit agrees to 6.94e-16 peak-scaled across short, partial and long bunches. Existing CPU anchors pass. CUDA runtime parity was not exercised by this verifier; human sign-off remains pending. [validation write-up](radiation-physics/finite-footprint-longitudinal-decoherence.md) **2026-10-08 (#370):** scope narrowed. Fields are now offset-free (face point and full arrival removed) on every finite-footprint row; the conditional-on-sampled-transverse realization no longer exists. This row's `F_z` blend is unchanged and alone covers bunches without transverse offsets (anchor updated to zero `initial_r_ang`); with transverse offsets the cross term is `F_z F_perp` (`transverse-bunch-form-factor`).

## `coherent-flat-term-omission`

- **Claim:** Replacing the physical estimator $T=G+\frac{N-1}{M-1}F(A-G)$ by $G$ changes a row at an evaluated energy by at most $F(N-1)G$ for $M\ge2$ sampled fields and physical population $N\ge1$. Here $G=\sum_e\|S_e\|^2$, $A=\|\sum_e S_e\|^2$; absent physical population recovers the sampled blend with $N=M$. Physical $N\le1$ has zero pair excess; sampled $M=1$ has $A=G$. Omit only where a nonnegative finite factor certifies the bound against the grouped floor.
- **Code:** `montecarlo/spectrum/cxr/_per_hkl.py::_flat_energy_keep`, `::_flat_omission_weight`, `::_coherent_population`, `::_decoherence_blend`; `montecarlo/runner/cxr_grid.py::coherent_flat_omission_limit`
- **Source:** Cauchy-Schwarz on sampled electron fields and the distinct-pair estimator in `coherent-physical-bunch-population`; historical `coherent-inter-electron-decoherence` and finite-footprint Gaussian law.
- **Status:** anchored
- **Checks:** Dimensionless factors; $F\to0$ gives the grouped floor; physical $N\le1$ has no pair excess; limit zero preserves the full reducer. Physical infinite slabs use $F=1$; historical slabs use the empirical row factor. Finite footprints additionally enclose the analytic Gaussian using `gaussian_form_factor_bounds`; actual rounded pair-scale and positive bound products are enclosed outward. Certificate covers evaluated coordinates, not continuous bin integrals or relative error against a vanishing coherent spectrum.
- **Anchor:** `tests/montecarlo/coherent/test_coherent_flat_omission.py`
- **Notes:** Issue #362, port onto current #350. Default omission share $10^{-4}$ is separate from the decoherence-grid share; combined feature-window charges total $4\times10^{-4}$ of $5\times10^{-4}$. Coefficient audits disable omission and refuse active omission. Historical thin CPU and SLURM 1136 CUDA timings in the write-up used sampled-population weighting; they do not establish speedup for physical-charge production. Physical slabs generally cannot omit at small limits. Revised physical-population bound has fresh-context verification and 39 passing CPU omission regressions, including aligned-field references for $N$ below and above $M$. SLURM 1162 passed all 39 selected physical-population CUDA checks across streaming, eager CuPy, and reduction-JIT routes in 22.37 seconds. Historical timings remain scoped to the sampled-population model; the new checks establish no production-workload speedup. [validation write-up](beam-transport/coherent-flat-term-omission.md) **2026-10-08 (#370):** the Gaussian certificate bisects for the first certified ascending candidate (identical result to the previous scan; a non-ascending grid keeps every candidate). With the transverse factor nearly every energy is a candidate, and the scan's per-candidate 50-digit interval exponential stalled the reduced `hopg_short` smoke beyond 90 min. Certification still uses `F_z` alone (conservative under `F_perp ≤ 1`). Anchor: `tests/montecarlo/coherent/test_coherent_flat_omission.py::test_gaussian_certification_bisects_instead_of_scanning`. **2026-10-09 (#365):** finite-footprint rows with a recorded spot are now additionally certified by the row's `F_z F_perp` (`coherent-transverse-flat-omission`); the `F_z`-only certificate above is unchanged and still runs first.

## `coherent-transverse-flat-omission`

- **Claim:** On a finite footprint with a recorded Gaussian spot, the flat-term omission bound $\lvert\Delta\rvert\le F(N-1)G/M$ may use the row's full reducer factor $F_r=F_zF_{\perp,r}$. A coordinate is omitted for a row where a directed upper bound $U_r\ge F_r$ satisfies $U_rW\le\ell$: an interval enclosure of $e^{-\max(q,0)}$, $q=\mathbf K^{\mathsf T}\Sigma_f\mathbf K$, at binary64 inputs. Bisection covers a monotone side only where certified signs of the affine $q'$ hold at both ends; there $F_zF_\perp$ is nonincreasing (or $F_\perp$ alone nondecreasing, with $F_z\le1$). A whole-range interval enclosure covers constant or far-off rows. Every row must pass; the longitudinal certificate and $\ell$ are unchanged.
- **Code:** `montecarlo/spectrum/coherent_transverse.py::{transverse_form_factor_upper,transverse_slope_sign}`; `montecarlo/spectrum/cxr/_per_hkl.py::{_transverse_certified,_row_transverse_certified}`
- **Source:** `coherent-flat-term-omission` (Cauchy–Schwarz, valid for any $F\in[0,1]$) with the `transverse-bunch-form-factor` and `finite-footprint-longitudinal-decoherence` laws; monotonicity of $e^{-q}$ for affine $q'$ of fixed sign.
- **Status:** rederived
- **Checks:** $q$ dimensionless ($\mathbf K$ in Å$^{-1}$, $\Sigma_f$ in Å$^2$). $\Sigma_f\to0$ recovers the longitudinal certificate; exact phase matching keeps the resonance (undecided slope); $\mathbf a=0$ certifies only by the whole-range bound; µm–mm spots certify whole rows; $F_z\to1$ omits where $F_\perp$ certifies. Real-valued law at binary64 inputs; backend casting/product/reduction roundoff remain under the existing working-precision allowance.
- **Anchor:** `tests/montecarlo/coherent/test_coherent_transverse_omission.py`
- **Notes:** Issue #365 (adaptive phase retention; umbrella `adaptive-coherent-phase-retention`). Addresses the conservatism recorded under `coherent-flat-term-omission` (#370) and `coherent-line-grid-windowed-resolution` (`hopg_short` Cauchy–Schwarz switch). Coherent identity `physical-distinct-pairs-v3` because the default omission limit $10^{-4}$ now omits more finite-footprint coordinates. Retains complete per-electron fields; no intra-electron or cross-boundary simplification. **Fresh-context verification 2026-10-09 (`1e94883a`):** the derivation matches term for term: same binary64 inputs and ω, the PSD clamp, a true interval square, and sound bisection and side nudging. Discrepancy: `_row_transverse_certified` treated a binary64 underflow of `upper_z*perp` as a certified zero. Counterexample ℓ=1e-318, W=1e10, omitted FW=5.75e-315. Only ℓ<2.5e-324·W is affected; the default 1e-4 is not. Across 700 randomized and adversarial rows there were 0 violations against an 80-digit reference, and the anchors pass 95/95 on CPU. **Owner resolution (same day):** products are now rounded outward unconditionally; regression `test_underflowed_bound_product_is_never_a_certified_zero` fails before the fix. **Fresh-context re-verification 2026-10-09 (`619a8ab3`):** rederived. The fix is the only `src` change. Every product is rounded outward, so $U\ge F_zF_\perp$ and $UW\ge F_rW$ for all $W>0$, including $W<1$. Helpers return values in $[2^{-1074},1]$ and nonfinite inputs certify nothing. The counterexample is no longer certified. 1,500 randomized subnormal-limit rows (10,810 certified coordinates) show 0 violations against an 80-digit reference; the same harness finds 18 on `1e94883a`. Anchors pass 96/96 on CPU. `anchored` awaits green CI. Human sign-off pending (#277). Residual scope: real-valued law at binary64 inputs; REAL casting, backend product and reduction roundoff stay under the working-precision allowance; CUDA runtime not exercised. [validation write-up](beam-transport/coherent-transverse-flat-omission.md)

## `coherent-line-hkl-batch`

- **Claim:** batched `(n_seg, N_g)` evaluation of the coherent line path: steps 1–6 (kinematics, detuning, gathered interpolation, escape length) are shared with the incoherent batch, the complex per-polarization amplitude `A_PXR + A_CBS` is kept on the full grid, and `\|Σ_j\|²` is taken per `(reflection, mosaic orientation)` row — so reflections and mosaic orientations stay **incoherent** while the segment sum inside a row keeps its phase. No new equation; identical to the per-hkl `_accumulate_per_hkl` loop up to float reassociation
- **Code:** `montecarlo/spectrum/cxr/_spectrum.py::mc_cxr_spectrum` (batched branch, `coherent=True`)
- **Source:** none — amplitudes unchanged from `coherent-emission` / `cbs-amplitude`; this is an evaluation-order claim
- **Status:** filtered
- **Checks:** limiting cases inherited and re-run green: single-segment coherent == incoherent self-term, `N²` in-phase build-up, on-resonance phase cancellation. Direct numerical diff vs the per-hkl loop on hopg 30 keV / Ne=10000 / 1.83e6 segments / 4 reflections / 831 bins, float32: max abs `2.58e-14` = `1.7e-6` of peak, max relative `2.0e-5` on bins above peak×1e-6, sum relative difference `0.0` — inside the `√N·eps ≈ 1.6e-4` float32 reassociation envelope
- **Anchor:** `tests/montecarlo/coherent/test_coherent_emission.py`; `tests/montecarlo/spectrum/test_chunk_invariance.py`
- **Notes:** Routing: coherent runs with `sinc_cutoff`, `groove`, or `layers` stay on the proven per-hkl loop, bit-for-bit. `coherent_jit_kernel.run_coherent_reduction_kernel` signature is **unchanged** (per-`(reflection, orientation)`); squaring is structurally per-row so a g-batched kernel cannot fuse the reduction. Perf: line phase 0.3600 s → 0.1820 s (1.98×) on a 3060 Ti. Fresh-context verification + human sign-off pending

## `line-hkl-batch`

- **Claim:** batched `(n_seg, N_g)` evaluation of the **incoherent** line path: kinematics, detuning, the shared interpolation bracket for `chi`/`U`/`mu`, escape length, both polarization amplitudes, and the `\|A\|²` accumulation run ONCE over an `(n_seg, N_g)` grid instead of `N_g` separate per-hkl `_accumulate_reflection` passes. `g`-independent segment quantities are hoisted out of the pass, length-3 contractions are spelled elementwise (see `line-gemv-elementwise`), and the sinc reduction is taken over the union bin order. No new equation; identical to the per-hkl `_accumulate_per_hkl` loop up to float reassociation
- **Code:** `montecarlo/spectrum/cxr/_spectrum.py::mc_cxr_spectrum` (batched branch, `coherent=False`); NumPy-backend node-quadrature sinc reduction in `montecarlo/spectrum/cxr/_cpu_reduce.py::accumulate_sincsq`
- **Source:** none — amplitudes unchanged from `pxr-amplitude` / `cbs-amplitude` / `finite-time-lineshape`; this is an evaluation-order claim
- **Status:** filtered
- **Checks:** Direct A/B against the per-hkl `_accumulate_per_hkl` reference loop, reached by source-patching the branch gate (the loop is otherwise unreachable for an ungrooved, unlayered, incoherent run). hopg, CPU `REAL = float64`, 250 bins: 25 keV / Ne=40 / 14,970 segments at 2 and 4 reflections, and 60 keV / Ne=120 / 96,478 segments at 2 reflections. Max abs difference `1.70e-15`–`1.15e-14` of peak; max relative `6.26e-15`–`1.59e-14` on bins above peak×1e-6; integral relative difference `0.0`–`7.83e-16` — all inside the `√n_seg·eps ≈ 2.7e-14`–`6.9e-14` float64 reassociation envelope. `components=True` PXR/CBS split agrees to `2.34e-14`. **Single-segment / single-reflection limit is bitwise identical between the two arms.** Shared-bracket gather: at-node identity exact, below/above-grid endpoint clamps exact, float32 gather vs per-reflection `np.interp` max relative `3.31e-07` (float64 `2.10e-16`)
- **Anchor:** `tests/montecarlo/spectrum/test_chunk_invariance.py`; `tests/montecarlo/transport/test_montecarlo.py`; `tests/montecarlo/transport/test_cxr_cpu_reduce.py`; `tests/montecarlo/spectrum/test_layered_batched_route.py`
- **Notes:** CPU reduction (#286): on the NumPy backend, both the batched fallback and the per-hkl node path (no `sinc_cutoff`, no bin-mean) contract `w @ sinc²` in a fused numba `prange` kernel over energies instead of the dense lineshape matrix + BLAS matvec. Per-element profile arithmetic is unchanged; only the sum order over lines differs. Si 300 keV, 10 µm, 4k e⁻, 400 bins, same tracks: max abs difference `2.8e-14` of peak, integral relative `2.8e-14`; kernel vs dense reference `rtol=1e-12` in the anchor test. Routing: coherent-with-`sinc_cutoff`, coherent layered/grooved, flight-grouped, temporal and coefficient-capture runs stay on the per-hkl loop, bit-for-bit (CPU node-quadrature reductions excepted, as above). Incoherent layered and grooved absorbers take the batched route since #1194: the escape pieces are g-independent and only the per-layer exact `mu(E_res)` varies per row, evaluated by `_escape_mu.exact_mu_table` — every EPDL channel folded onto the union of channel knots, where the weighted sum is again lin-lin, right-continuous at edges — instead of the single slab's `line-absorption-tabulation`. A/B vs the per-hkl loop (`_policy._BATCH_LAYERED_ESCAPE`), hopg, 4 reflections, 48 varied rows, CPU `REAL = float64`: three-layer MoS₂/Si/C stack exiting front and back, and a blazed groove, node and bin-mean: max abs difference `4.6e-16`–`7.8e-16` of peak, max relative `5.6e-16`–`1.1e-15` above peak×1e-6, integral relative `≤ 2.4e-16`; the folded table reproduces the host `_mu_total_inv_ang` within 4 ulp of `REAL` over 5 eV–200 keV including every photoionization edge and its float32 neighbours (`tests/montecarlo/spectrum/test_layered_batched_route.py`). Substituting the tabulated `mu` there fails every layered and grooved A/B case, so the gate resolves the escape model. The per-hkl loop evaluates the same device tables (built once per call instead of one host EPDL call per row and layer), so an end-to-end A/B also runs the per-hkl arm on the host `_mu_total_inv_ang` as an independent reference. On device (CuPy float32, RTX 5080, frozen layered MoS₂-on-Si g30 replay, seed 642501, node axis 0.5–10 keV, 512-reflection chunks 0 and 78): max abs difference `1.9e-6`–`3.5e-6` of peak, max relative `2.5e-6`–`1.2e-5` above peak×1e-6, integral relative `2.8e-7`–`7.9e-7`; the prologue runs 11–20× faster (#1194, job 12551). The A/B above is float64 — the CPU backend's `REAL`; there is no way to force float32 without a CUDA device, so the production float32 envelope is inferred (`eps` ratio ≈ 2e7, so ~1e-7 expected) and the sibling `coherent-line-hkl-batch` measured `2.0e-5` on device. Golden regen for the float32 spectrum goldens is still owed. Absorption is tabulated on **both** arms of the single-slab A/B, so this row does not cover `line-absorption-tabulation`; on layered/grooved runs both arms use the exact folded `mu`. **#1194 extension: `rederived`** by fresh-context verification (2026-11-14; [derivation](radiation-physics/line-hkl-batch.md)): g-independence of the escape pieces, `(nb, N_g, K)` layer order, routing, and the union-knot fold re-derived; the fold matched `_mu_total_inv_ang` for all 100 elements and six mixtures at every knot, its float64/float32 neighbours and interior points to ≤4 float64 ulp with an identical NaN pattern. Above-last-knot extrapolation and the below-threshold zero are unreachable in band, since every EPDL2025 channel spans `[EPDL_E_MIN_EV, EPDL_E_MAX_EV]`. The verifier found that the original midpoint probe could round onto the next knot between float-adjacent knots (unreachable with EPDL2025, whose smallest relative knot gap is `1.05e-9`). The table now probes at each knot under the host's `side="right"` rule, with a synthetic regression test. The single-slab part of the row was not re-run and stays `filtered`. Fresh-context verification + human sign-off pending

## `line-amplitude-fusion`

- **Claim:** incoherent `\|A_PXR + A_CBS\|²` evaluated by expanding the complex amplitude onto real/imaginary components with REAL scalar shape factors `f_pxr = [(v·k_g)((k+g)·e) − k²(v·e)] / detuning` and `f_cbs = −[{g·e − (v·g)(v·e)} + (v·e){k·g − (k·v)(v·g)}/(v·g)] / [γ (v·g)]`, so `Re A = χ_re f_pxr + u_re f_cbs`, `Im A = χ_im f_pxr + u_im f_cbs`, `\|A\|² = Re² + Im²`, and the components as `\|χ\|² f_pxr²` and `\|eU_g/m\|² f_cbs²`. One fused real kernel; algebraically identical to the complex expression, reassociated (`χ·(N/detuning)` vs `(χ/detuning)·N`)
- **Code:** `montecarlo/spectrum/cxr/_kernels.py::_cxr_amp_sq_core` (+ its call site in `mc_cxr_spectrum`'s batched incoherent branch)
- **Source:** none new — same `A_PXR`/`A_CBS` as `pxr-amplitude` / `cbs-amplitude`; algebraic expansion of `\|z\|²` for `z = χ f_pxr + (eU_g/m) f_cbs`
- **Status:** filtered
- **Checks:** Against the retained complex expression (the coherent branch's verbatim form), 2e6 adversarial samples per dtype. float32: `\|A\|²` median `1.06e-07` (0.9 eps), p99 `5.58e-07`, max `3.12e-04` = 2613 eps; `\|A_PXR\|²` max `6.66e-07` (5.6 eps); `\|A_CBS\|²` max `7.39e-07` (6.2 eps). float64: `\|A\|²` median `1.92e-16`, max `3.83e-13` (1725 eps). The `\|A\|²` tail is entirely PXR/CBS near-cancellation — at the worst sample `\|A\|² / max(\|A_PXR\|², \|A_CBS\|²) = 7.3e-07`; restricted to the 98.3% of samples with `\|A\|² > 0.1 max(…)` the float32 max falls to `1.46e-06` (12 eps). Limits: `U_g→0` zeroes `\|A_CBS\|²` exactly and reproduces `\|A_PXR\|²` to `6.21e-16`; `χ→0` zeroes `\|A_PXR\|²` exactly and reproduces `\|A_CBS\|²` to `6.57e-16`; `χ = U = 0` is identically zero; `\|A\|²` is quadratic in `(χ, U)` to `6.93e-14` under a ×3 rescale. All three outputs non-negative by construction
- **Anchor:** `tests/montecarlo/spectrum/test_chunk_invariance.py`; end-to-end via the `line-hkl-batch` A/B
- **Notes:** Issue #187 passes the same nonzero `k_eff·e` term as the eager complex branch; full-vector algebra is independently verified under `xray-in-medium-resonance`. Incoherent path only: the coherent branch keeps the un-reassociated complex expression (`pol_A.append(A_PXR + A_CBS)`), so coherent goldens are unaffected. Wrapped in `xp.fuse()` on CuPy; plain elementwise arithmetic on NumPy. The float32 tail is a property of the *cancellation regime*, not of the fusion — the complex arm loses the same digits, the two just lose different ones. Golden regen for the line/PXR/CBS float32 goldens is still owed. Fresh-context verification + human sign-off pending

## `line-gemv-elementwise`

- **Claim:** the length-3 contractions of the line path (`v·g`, `v·n̂`, `k·g`, `v·e`, the `k_g·k_g` / `v·k_g` einsums, and the `r·g` phase) evaluated as `a₀b₀ + a₁b₁ + a₂b₂` rather than as a cuBLAS GEMV/GEMM with inner dimension 3. One helper serves both `M @ v` (scalar `b`) and the row-wise `einsum('ij,ij->i')` (columns of a second `(N,3)`). No new equation; a fixed left-to-right association of a three-term inner product
- **Code:** `montecarlo/spectrum/cxr/_kernels.py::_dot3_core` (via `::_matvec3`, `::_rowdot3`)
- **Source:** none — definition of the Euclidean inner product in 3-D
- **Status:** filtered
- **Checks:** 2e6 random `(N,3)` rows on the NumPy backend. float32: **bitwise identical** to both `np.einsum('ij,ij->i', A, B)` and `A @ v`. float64: max relative `2.50e-11` (row dot) and `9.06e-11` (matvec) against the BLAS-backed reference — reassociation only, no term dropped. Symmetry `a·b == b·a` bitwise; `a·a ≥ 0` everywhere; the orthogonality limit `e_x·e_y` is exactly `0.0` with no drift. Units inherit from the operands (the helper is dimensionless bookkeeping)
- **Anchor:** `tests/montecarlo/spectrum/test_chunk_invariance.py` (via the batched path)
- **Notes:** Motivation is launch cost, not arithmetic: cuBLAS is tuned for large `K`, so the skinny `K=3` contractions were dispatched as ~6.3k `internal::gemvx` launches carrying ~24% of the 300 keV line-path GPU time; spelled elementwise they fuse into a single CuPy kernel with no cuBLAS handle. The docstring's "runs bit-for-bit identical on NumPy" is confirmed for float32 and **false for float64**, where NumPy's reference dispatches through BLAS — production `REAL` is float32 on device, so the claim holds where it is load-bearing. On-device float32 behaviour is not covered by the CPU measurement and needs a CUDA-box confirmation. Fresh-context verification + human sign-off pending

## `line-absorption-tabulation`

- **Claim:** self-absorption `τ = L_esc · μ(E_res)` from per-element `log(μ_i)` tables interpolated linearly in `log(E)` and summed as `μ = Σ_i μ_i`. `μ_i` is the EPDL2025 total (`narrow-beam-total-attenuation`). The shared grid is an edge-resolved base mesh (node spacing `max(1 eV, 1e-3 E)`, the 1 eV mesh kept within 3 % of every basis/absorber EPDL photoionization edge; #1201) unioned with native Chantler nodes from the crystal basis and explicit absorbers, every retained runtime EPDL knot of each absorber, and a float32-adjacent node pair at each EPDL photoionization edge, so no mesh interval straddles a retained EPDL slope change and any interval straddling a jump is at most one float32 ulp wide; exact threshold nodes are retained, while interior float64 queries deliberately smooth the jump. Applies to the single-slab and finite-footprint branches, coherent and incoherent alike; layered `_stack_tau` and grooved escape keep exact per-point `μ`
- **Code:** `montecarlo/spectrum/cxr/_tabulation_mesh.py::_cxr_tabulation_grid`, `::_line_mesh`, `::_epdl_mesh_nodes`; `montecarlo/spectrum/cxr/_kernels.py::_elemental_log_mu_table`, `::_log_interp_fraction`, `::_interp_elemental_mu`; `montecarlo/spectrum/cxr/_spectrum.py::mc_cxr_spectrum`; `montecarlo/spectrum/coherent_prologue_jit_kernel.py::_interp_elemental_mu`
- **Source:** EPDL2025 MF=23 sections are lin-lin between knots (`narrow-beam-total-attenuation`); inside one lin-lin segment, log-log interpolation on a sub-interval departs from it only at second order in the node spacing. Compound coefficients add only after elemental interpolation. (Before issue #274: xraydb 4.5.8 `XrayDB._from_chantler`, `log(μ_i)` exactly linear on Chantler intervals.)
- **Status:** rederived
- **Checks:** **Issue #1201 (mesh):** against direct evaluation at 1 eV-mesh midpoints over 1 eV–360 keV, the excess error over the former uniform 1 eV mesh, scaled by the local ±2 % table maximum, is ≤ `1.3e-5` for `δ = 1 − Re n` and ≤ `1.5e-6` for `μ`. For `χ_g` it is ≤ `5.4e-5` in Si, MoS2, W, Ge and Pb and in GaAs (111). The weak GaAs (200) reaches `3.2e-4` (`1.4e-9` absolute), from Ga `f1` spline curvature at the 2211.8 eV Chantler node away from any edge, amplified by the `f_Ga − f_As` cancellation. Inside edge windows the mesh is the 1 eV mesh, so the excess there is zero. Off-edge error is measured exactly quadratic (Si (111) `χ_re` `8.6e-7`/`3.4e-6`/`1.36e-5` at `h/E` = 1/2/4e-3, theory `0.75 (h/E)²`; `f''`-only terms `2.5 (h/E)²`). Without edge windows a geometric mesh errs by `5e-3`–`1e-1` beside edges, because xraydb's `f1` is an interpolating cubic spline with ~1 eV structure there. The anchor pins the spacing, in-window equality with the 1 eV mesh, the prefix property for non-integral `lo`, and an off-edge `rtol=1e-4` bound. **Spectrum level (MoLab RTX PRO 6000, CUDA float32, code 43d0bae9f, frozen #642 Si 12083 and layered 12115, seed 642501; `checks/cxr_tab_grid_1201.py`):** on band (node and bin-mean) and automatic axes the selected sets are identical to the 1 eV mesh's. Yield moves ≤ `5.1e-7`, the maximum difference is ≤ `2.9e-6` of the peak, and the worst relative difference over nodes above 1e-3 of the peak is ≤ `2.4e-5`. Before the window fix (1f7d096e7) that last figure was `7.9e-4`. [record](../repo-design/compute/data/line-table-mesh-2026-11-15.json) **Issue #274:** adversarial midpoints of every mesh interval except the one-ulp edge intervals agree with direct `_mu_total_inv_ang` to `≤9e-5` relative at float64 and float32 (HOPG, MoS2, MoSe2, h-BN, WSe2 bands; worst MoSe2 near Se L2), bound `2e-4` in the anchor; exact node identity `5e-15`; Chantler couplings unchanged. Earlier (Chantler-era) checks: Pre-fix HOPG error was `2.72e-1` in `μ` and `5.23e-1` in 10000 Å transmission. Corrected adversarial midpoint checks cover HOPG, MoS2, and MoSe2: float64 direct-xraydb agreement `≤2e-12` relative; explicit float32 HOPG agreement `≤1e-4`. Units remain 1/Å; positivity, exact-node identity, endpoint clamps, zero-path transmission, and a Si absorber absent from the HOPG basis are anchored. Focused CPU and neighboring chunk-invariance checks pass
- **Anchor:** `tests/montecarlo/spectrum/test_line_absorption_tabulation.py`
- **Notes:** **#1201:** the base mesh changed from a uniform 1 eV mesh to the edge-resolved geometric mesh (Si 300 keV automatic axis: 362,299 → 9,322 nodes). The elemental `μ` interpolation rule is unchanged, but **#1201 fresh-context re-verification (2026-11-14): `rederived`.** The mesh construction, interpolation rules and root-support prefix match the claim; the `(h/E)²` law and its prefactor are confirmed. Spot checks were extended to the Ge, GaAs and Pb K/L edges. The verifier found two defects, both now fixed in code (43d0bae9f): geometric nodes inside edge windows (Si (111) `χ_im` excess `1.15e-3` at the K-edge sign change), and a missing 1 keV node for non-integral `lo` with `999 < hi < 1000`. GPU spectrum A/B of the tabulated mesh: see Checks. [derivation](radiation-physics/line-absorption-tabulation.md) Fresh-context independent verification rederived the pinned xraydb log-log rule through `μ_i ∝ f₂_i/E`, checked the per-element-then-sum source-to-code mapping, units, signs, and limiting cases, and found no physics divergence. The old divergent convention interpolated the compound total linearly in energy; interpolating the compound total in log space also leaves compound residuals because a sum of elemental power laws is not itself one power law. The corrected table adds no per-segment CPU/device synchronization. CUDA runtime/numerical/performance evidence and a spectrum-level exact-vs-tabulated A/B remain open; no remote job was authorized. **Shared-mesh side effect (#274):** the couplings and `Re n` are interpolated on the same mesh, so the added EPDL nodes also move their interpolated values (up to 15% in HOPG (002) components beside the C K edge, ≤0.2% for MoS2/WSe2). Against direct evaluation this is a refinement: the error is equal or smaller at all but ≤0.04% of 20,000 random energies per band, and the coupling functions themselves are bitwise unchanged. The pre-existing coupling interpolation error at the C K edge (largest relative to each table's peak in HOPG) is not addressed here. The verification above covered the retired Chantler `μ`. **2026-10-05 fresh-context EPDL verification:** the elemental log-log approximation, composition sum, units, sign, limits, exact-node/endpoint policies and streaming CUDA source flow match. Independent SHA-256-pinned raw-tape parsing confirms native lin-lin laws; smooth total errors are at most 0.0488% in the probed HOPG/MoS2/MoSe2 bands, including the separate 5e-4 runtime knot-thinning budget. The 2e-4 anchor bounds additional line-table error against processed EPDL, not raw-tape error. Carbon edge smoothing spans 3.05e-5 eV and produces a 362.6% midpoint coefficient error (10000 Å transmission 0.572 vs 0.0752); this narrow declared exception is excluded from smooth bounds. Exact threshold values are retained. GPU runtime parity and whole-spectrum A/B remain untested by this verifier. Stale Chantler-exactness setup comments and the helper docstring's omitted edge exception remain documentation findings. Human sign-off required. [implementation-context derivation](radiation-physics/line-absorption-tabulation.md)

## `line-reflection-coupling-tables`

- **Claim:** the materials helper tabulates crystallographic `χ_g(E)` and static `U_g(E)/m_e` on `E_tab` for all reflections at once by grouping the basis by element: `S = DWF[c_g + Σ_el P_el(f'_el + i f''_el)]` and `acc = DWF/g²·a_g` (static `Z − f0`, #513), with `P_el = Σ_{i∈el} exp(i2π hkl·R_i)`, `c_g = Σ_el P_el f0_el(g)`, `a_g = Σ_el P_el(Z_el − f0_el(g))`, then the `pxr-amplitude` / `cbs-amplitude` prefactors. The line paths instead consume `χ_line,g = conj(χ_-g)` and `conj(U_-g)/m_e = U_g/m_e`, supplied by `emission_coupling_tables`: reverse Miller indices and conjugate, equivalently replace `+i f_double_prime` by `-i f_double_prime` at the positive basis phase. The physical emission Fourier convention is owned by `line-energy-dispersion`. The energy arithmetic runs on the spectrum backend (CuPy on a GPU) in float64 before the REAL cast. Each reflection is tabulated once; mosaic orientation rows read their reflection's table through a row-to-reflection index (`ROW_TABLE` / `table_row`) instead of carrying copies. No new physical model: the direct tables match per-atom `chi_g`/`U_g` up to float64 summation order; emission tables additionally apply the stated conjugate Friedel pairing before the REAL cast. With zero anomalous imaginary factors, both table conventions coincide
- **Code:** `materials/crystal.py::reflection_coupling_tables`; `montecarlo/spectrum/cxr/_kernels.py::_reflection_tables`, `::_interp_gather_cxr_tables`; `montecarlo/spectrum/cxr/_batched.py::_batched_reflection_tables`; `montecarlo/spectrum/coherent_prologue_jit_kernel.py::run_coherent_prologue_kernel`
- **Source:** linearity of the couplings from `pxr-amplitude` / `cbs-amplitude` (linearity of `S` and `acc` in `F_i = f0_i(g) + f'_i(E) + i f''_i(E)`); the conjugate Friedel mapping is the `line-energy-dispersion` contract. This claim verifies evaluation order, convention mapping and storage, not the physical derivation of that emission convention
- **Status:** rederived
- **Checks:** Direct materials-table float64 parity against `chi_g` / `U_g/m_e` for HOPG (four reflections, anomalous), h-BN (with and without anomalous terms, `B = 0.4 Å²`) and MoSe2 (edge-prone Se, `B = 0.6 Å²`) over 30 eV–900 keV plus out-of-table energies: identical NaN masks; complex error `≤ 1e-12(|coupling| + max|coupling|)`, the absolute floor covering centric imaginary parts and an h-BN (100) structure factor that crosses zero between edges. Motivation (measured on the lab RTX 5080 host, `hopg_high_energy` 50 MeV, 600k-node line axis): the previous per-atom host tabulation, duplicated over 4 reflections × 25 mosaic orientations, ran single-threaded for minutes with host RSS rising past 34 GiB before any GPU kernel launched
- **Anchor:** `tests/materials/test_crystallography.py::test_reflection_coupling_tables_match_per_atom_couplings`, `::test_emission_coupling_tables_are_the_conjugate_friedel_mate`, `::test_emission_coupling_tables_reduce_to_crystallographic_without_fpp`; `tests/montecarlo/coherent/test_coherent_emission.py::test_runner_dual_spectra_from_one_transport`
- **Notes:** CUDA parity of the indexed `cxr_interp_gather_line_tables_f32` gather and the coherent prologue's `table_row` read is owed to the hardware suite on the remote host. Without mosaic (`ROW_TABLE = arange(N_g)`) both kernels read the same addresses as before. Initial fresh-context verification (2026-11-11, #1101) found an exact mismatch in the previous ledger wording: the materials helper computes the stated susceptibility with `+i f_double_prime`, but `_reflection_tables` calls `emission_coupling_tables` and consumes `conj(chi(-hkl))`, whose atomic factor has `-i f_double_prime`. The static coupling and indexed storage match. Four focused anchors pass, and an independent per-atom/metric oracle agrees within 1.08e-15 peak-relative; these established the direct-helper algebra while exposing the ledger mismatch. The owner corrected the Claim and Source fields to distinguish direct materials and emission tables. A new code-blind verifier independently rederived the corrected mapping from the original source before inspecting implementation or the prior report: no remaining discrepancy. Ten focused anchor cases pass; an independent anomalous 4H-SiC atom-sum/metric oracle matches direct, emission and static tables within 5.37e-16 peak-relative; the zero-imaginary-anomalous limit and CPU indexed gather pass exactly. Status advanced only after this fresh re-verification. No implementation repair or human sign-off applied. [Independent verification](radiation-physics/line-reflection-coupling-tables.md)

## `line-tabulation-nan-ceiling`

- **Claim:** shared line setup keeps the original finite-refraction mesh prefix and a NaN boundary node. With `use_henke=True`, its upper bound is the basis Chantler refraction endpoint (or the earlier all-table bound); every delta lookup and three-pass fixed-point root is unchanged. Static CBS coupling and EPDL attenuation may stay finite beyond that endpoint, so this is not equivalence of all table values. A last root update can cross the endpoint without a subsequent refraction lookup: incoherent/component routes retain their common total-weight rejection; coherent routes retain existing coefficient NaN masks rather than gaining a new rejection. With `use_henke=False`, retain the original all-table bound and unchanged mesh
- **Code:** `montecarlo/spectrum/cxr/_kernels.py::{_cxr_table_nan_ceiling,_cxr_setup_ceiling}`; `montecarlo/spectrum/cxr/_setup.py` (tabulation window)
- **Source:** evaluation-domain proof from linear interpolation and the three-pass recurrence in `xray-in-medium-resonance`, with Chantler NaN support from `materials/atomic.py::henke_dispersion`; EPDL attenuation support remains 100 GeV. [Independent derivation](radiation-physics/line-tabulation-root-support.md)
- **Status:** anchored
- **Checks:** finite-prefix/native-knot and exact CPU root agreement across the support boundary for basis/explicit Si absorbers, with and without anomalous refraction. Synthetic final-update crossing pins recurrence preservation. Original all-table ceiling tests still demonstrate finite attenuation/static coupling suffixes; no attenuation support is truncated globally
- **Anchor:** `tests/montecarlo/spectrum/test_line_absorption_tabulation.py::test_setup_bound_preserves_root_support_and_native_mesh_prefix`, `::test_last_root_update_can_cross_support_without_changing_on_shortened_mesh`, `::test_absorber_mu_outlives_the_chantler_coupling_ceiling`, `::test_nan_ceiling_is_inactive_without_anomalous_refraction`
- **Notes:** #286 restores bounded setup after #274 extended attenuation to 100 GeV. Fresh-context proof covers the distinction between unchanged recurrence, finite incoherent populations and preserved coherent NaN masks; no new high-energy optical physics is introduced. Job 3768 (code `67b50ee71`) passes 48 GPU FP32/fused and FP64 root-equivalence cases across HOPG/MoS2, gradients and windows above/below support. Its high-energy population/adaptive arms complete without OOM; old/new high-energy reference arrays are bitwise identical. [Hardware and production evidence](https://github.com/Quantum-Light-Matter-Cooperative-QLMC/pyrite/issues/286#issuecomment-6613466647). Agent status is never human sign-off

## `finite-time-lineshape`

- **Claim:** `\|Q\|² = t_L²·sinc_N²(P·t_L/π)`, `sinc_N(x)=sin(πx)/(πx)` (replaces absorption-limited δ)
- **Code:** `montecarlo/spectrum/cxr/_spectrum.py::mc_cxr_spectrum`
- **Source:** Feranchuk 2000 (finite interaction length)
- **Status:** anchored
- **Checks:** units and NumPy sinc convention; exact area `πt_L`; peak/width scaling and `t_L→∞` delta limit; energy-coordinate Jacobian
- **Anchor:** `tests/notebooks/test_anchor_figures.py::test_single_segment_lineshape_converges_to_closed_form`
- **Notes:** independent derivation matches the implementation's factor of two, `π`, `1−v·n̂`, and `ℏc`; [validation write-up](radiation-physics/finite-time-lineshape.md). **Frozen-root error near Bragg poles (#1166 item 3, bounded, no fix).** The kernel samples the photon field at the free root, so near a pole the `1/det` factor varies across the finite line. Against the finite-slab first-order Born line of a free electron (#1148 check `free_born_over_native`, 17 MeV Si(1-10), 2 µm, order-3 JUNGFRAU squares of 2.05e-4 sr), the error over all #1148 centres is: total band ≤ 0.5% (worst the (−1 −1 3) mirror, 1.0050; 45°, 1.0020; elsewhere ≤ 0.07%), and per reflection ≤ 1.7% ((1 1 −3), 15–16 eV from its pole). A single ray at the mirror is ×1.28; the aperture average removes most of it. On planar zone rows the source-owned Laue reflections use the slab kernel instead (`cr-planar-zone-pxr-modulation`), with no frozen root. Record: `docs/validation/check-records/planar-zone/zone-pxr-modulation-1148.json`

## `sinc-bin-integration`

- **Claim:** for the incoherent finite-time line profile $S(E) = \operatorname{sinc}^2[a(E-E_{\mathrm{res}})/\pi]$, the opt-in yield quadrature writes the exact bin mean $\bar S_i = \pi\{F(x_{i+1})-F(x_i)\}/[a(\epsilon_{i+1}-\epsilon_i)]$, where $x_i=a(\epsilon_i-E_{\mathrm{res}})/\pi$ and $F(x)=\operatorname{Si}(2\pi x)/\pi-x\operatorname{sinc}^2x$. Thus the summed in-window bin mass is independent of grid spacing; the array remains a density in photons/eV
- **Code:** `montecarlo/spectrum/cxr/_sine_integral.py` (shared coefficient source); `montecarlo/spectrum/cxr/_bin_mean_jit.py::_si_rational_complement`; `montecarlo/spectrum/cxr/_bin_quadrature.py::sincsq_bin_lineshape`, `::run_bin_mean_reduction_kernel`; host over-cap Fourier lines (#1320): `montecarlo/spectrum/cxr/_bin_mean_jit.py::host_near_far_bin_mean_reduce` (numba transliteration of `SINCSQ_BIN_PREAMBLE`'s `Si` and tail); `montecarlo/spectrum/cxr/_band_mass.py::sincsq_band_mass` (closed-form band mass, #1326); selected by `_cxr_grid_policy.py::resolve_cxr_grid_policy` and `montecarlo/spectrum/cxr/_setup.py::_prepare_spectrum`
- **Source:** the finite interaction-time profile of `finite-time-lineshape`; standard sine-integral antiderivative $\int \operatorname{sinc}^2x\,\mathrm{d}x = \operatorname{Si}(2\pi x)/\pi-x\operatorname{sinc}^2x$
- **Status:** anchored
- **Checks:** dimensions and normalization; derivative of $F$; $F(\pm\infty)=\pm1/2$ and whole-line mass $\pi/a$; bin width $\to0$ recovers node sampling; $a\to\infty$ concentrates the mass in the resonance bin; stable tail-complement evaluation avoids far-tail and float32 cancellation; uniform 3 eV/0.375 eV and nonuniform windowed-grid mass identities; CPU per-hkl/batched agreement; CUDA/CuPy matrix and fused-kernel FP64/float32 agreement measured on an RTX 5080 runner (2026-09-19)
