- **Anchor:** `tests/montecarlo/spectrum/test_sine_integral.py` (60-digit Si complement reference at the rational and outer branch joins), `tests/montecarlo/spectrum/test_sinc_bin_integration.py`, `tests/montecarlo/spectrum/test_sinc_bin_integration_cuda.py`, `tests/energy-grid/test_line_grid_quadrature.py`; real-trajectory/backend measurement `checks/sinc_bin_integration.py`
- **Notes:** opt-in and incoherent-only. The bin mean preserves integrated yield, not peak height or FWHM; coherent emission, flight-grouped numerical substeps, and `sinc_cutoff` are refused explicitly. The policy and case payload record the non-default quadrature, while default node sampling keeps historical identity. Fresh-context re-derivation (2026-09-17, [independent re-derivation](radiation-physics/sinc-bin-integration.md#independent-re-derivation)) matches the antiderivative, Jacobian, tail crossing term, signs, units, limiting cases, and the host-compiled CUDA preamble. The fixed-transport 300 keV measurement ran on the remote GPU box (2026-09-19): bin-mean yield is spacing-independent to $10^{-12}$ over 3 eV/0.375 eV/0.09375 eV and to $6\times10^{-8}$ on the #101 windowed axis, where node sampling at 3 eV is 5.3% low; CPU FP64, CUDA FP64, and fused float32 agree on bin-mean yield to $1.3\times10^{-15}$ and $2.2\times10^{-7}$ respectively. Issue #1407 replaces only the device/Numba Si complement on $4<t<48$ with the Cephes rational auxiliary fits (split at 8); the integral, midpoint series window, classification and envelope are unchanged. Fresh-context read-only verification at `827919145` returned `rederived`: auxiliary-function expression, all eight coefficient tuples, monic denominators, joins, tail normalization and unchanged classification/window agree. Independent 70-digit checks of 8,808 arguments measured maximum $t|\mathrm{error}|=5.578\times10^{-16}$ on $4<t<48$ for Numba and host-compiled generated C++; 107 bin means matched independent quadrature within $8.336\times10^{-16}$ absolute. Device execution was outside the verifier's evidence; separately King 15626 passed 83 CUDA/near-far/Fourier/boundary tests (one existing slow skip) at the same candidate. The named regressions re-pin the claim at `anchored`. Human sign-off pending. [validation write-up](radiation-physics/sinc-bin-integration.md) Production routes (`sincsq_bin_lineshape`, `run_bin_mean_reduction_kernel`) evaluate this exact mean only within `BIN_MEAN_EXACT_WIDTHS` = 64 first-zero widths of each resonance and use the averaged envelope beyond ([`sinc-bin-far-envelope`](#sinc-bin-far-envelope)); `exact_widths=None` and `sincsq_bin_masses` keep the exact mean everywhere.

## `sinc-bin-far-envelope`

- **Claim:** beyond $K$ first-zero widths of a resonance (both bin edges with $x_i \ge K$ or $x_{i+1} \le -K$, $x=(E-E_{\mathrm{res}})/w$), the production bin-mean routes write the exact bin mean of the $\sin^2$-averaged envelope, $1/(2\pi^2 x_i x_{i+1})$, in float32; nearer bins keep the exact `sinc-bin-integration` mean. Far bins telescope, so a line's integrated yield changes only by the omitted $\cos(2\pi x)/(2\pi^2x^2)$ term, at most $3/(2\pi^3K^2)$ ($1.2\times10^{-5}$ at $K=64$), and any bin mean by at most $1/(\pi^2K^2)$ of the line peak
- **Code:** `montecarlo/spectrum/cxr/_bin_quadrature.py::_host_bin_mean`, `SINCSQ_BIN_PREAMBLE::pyrite_sincsq_bin_mean_hybrid`, `::sincsq_bin_lineshape`, `::run_bin_mean_reduction_kernel`; `BIN_MEAN_EXACT_WIDTHS`, `montecarlo/spectrum/cxr/_bin_mean_jit.py::_near_far_bins` (#1320, the `_host_bin_mean` float32 far sequence)
- **Source:** `sinc-bin-integration` profile and bin convention; $\sin^2 u = \tfrac12(1-\cos 2u)$ and integration by parts of $\int \cos(2\pi x)/x^2$
- **Status:** rederived
- **Checks:** Required: envelope bin-mean closed form and its product (cancellation-free) form; the per-side oscillatory bound and its factor 3/2; telescoping over contiguous far bins; that only bins with both edges beyond $K$ on one side use the envelope; host/device agreement; $K\to\infty$ recovers the exact route; identical-trajectory yield and shape against exact bin-mean on production cases
- **Anchor:** `tests/montecarlo/spectrum/test_sinc_bin_integration.py`, `tests/montecarlo/spectrum/test_sinc_bin_integration_cuda.py`
- **Notes:** Introduced for #192: the FP64 sine integral at every (line, bin) pair made 5 MeV `high_energy` h-BN cases ~40x slower than node sampling on the consumer lab GPU. Bin-mean case identity is unchanged (the change is within the stated tolerance); checkpoints written with the all-exact mean remain valid to $1.2\times10^{-5}$ in yield. Fresh-context verification (2026-09-28) returned `discrepancy`: the FP64 resonance was rounded to float32 before the offset was formed, which broke the bound for $E_{\rm res}/w \gtrsim 2^{23}$ on the FP64 host/matrix routes. Fixed by the resonance head/remainder split, pinned by two MeV regressions; the re-check (same day) rederived it. Human sign-off pending. [derivation](radiation-physics/sinc-bin-far-envelope.md) ROCm (#1196): the envelope is unchanged; the sparse near/far route's ROCm yield loss came from a truncated launch grid, fixed in `_bin_sparse.py` (see `sinc-bin-near-far`). Post-fix ROCm chunks 0 and 78 agree with CUDA within this allowance (jobs 12657, 12659).

## `sinc-bin-midpoint-series`

- **Claim:** for a bin of width $\Delta \le 0.1$ first-zero widths centred at $|x_c| \ge 2$, the `sinc-bin-integration` bin mean equals its midpoint series $f + \Delta^2 f''/24 + \Delta^4 f^{(4)}/1920 + \Delta^6 f^{(6)}/322560$ at $x_c$ to within $2.5\times10^{-9}$ of the envelope $1/(2\pi^2x_c^2)$; other bins keep the two-tail evaluation.
- **Code:** `montecarlo/spectrum/cxr/_bin_quadrature.py::sincsq_midpoint_mean`, `SINCSQ_BIN_PREAMBLE::pyrite_sincsq_midpoint_mean`; `_SERIES_MAX_DX`, `_SERIES_MIN_X`
- **Source:** Taylor expansion of the bin average; Leibniz bound on $f^{(8)}$ for $f=(1-\cos 2\pi x)/(2\pi^2x^2)$ ([derivation](radiation-physics/sinc-bin-midpoint-series.md))
- **Status:** anchored
- **Checks:** Required: series coefficients; remainder bound at the window edge; cancellation-free trig forms; host/device agreement; dispatch leaves out-of-window bins bitwise unchanged; production spectra within float32 rounding of the series-free route.
- **Anchor:** `tests/montecarlo/spectrum/test_sinc_bin_integration.py`, `tests/montecarlo/spectrum/test_sinc_bin_integration_cuda.py`
- **Notes:** Issue #1003. On by default in device kernels; the host evaluator stays two-tail, and `CXR_BIN_REDUCTION_MODEL` changes with it. Fresh-context verification (2026-11-06) returned `rederived`, and corrected the page's proof of the bound (now $1.3\times10^{-9}$ of $e(x_c)$, inside the claim). Production agreement: $2.1\times10^{-10}$ per bin on CUDA flush sums (job 4036); end-to-end h-BN and silicon 5 MeV spectra within 2 float32 ulps of the pre-#1003 route in every bin above $10^{-6}$ of peak (job 4072). Anchored by the host test against adaptive quadrature; the matrix route is not separately compared. On ROCm (gfx1151, job 4075) the device series tests pass and fused flush sums match CUDA to $8.2\times10^{-12}$ per bin. Human sign-off pending.

## `sinc-bin-near-far`

- **Claim:** the large CUDA bin-mean reduction groups strictly far line intervals into a sorted tree. For $c_l=w_l/(2a_l^2)$, a far pair contributes $c_l/[(L-E_l)(H-E_l)]$ to bin $[L,H]$. A ten-moment expansion with node opening ratio at most $0.2$ has relative far-sum truncation below $4.6\times10^{-7}$ and per-line integrated-yield allowance below $7.3\times10^{-10}$ at $K=64$, in addition to the `sinc-bin-far-envelope` allowance. Leaves preserve the #192 near/far classification and exact near mean.
- **Code:** `montecarlo/spectrum/cxr/_bin_tree.py::build_cxr_tree`, `::run_tree_reduction`, `::reduce_host`; `montecarlo/spectrum/cxr/_bin_sparse.py::run_sparse_reduction`; `montecarlo/spectrum/cxr/_bin_quadrature.py::run_bin_mean_reduction_kernel`
- **Source:** partial-fraction integral of the far envelope; geometric expansion of $1/[(L-E_l)(H-E_l)]$ about a node center, with a positive-weight remainder bound
- **Status:** rederived
- **Checks:** Re-derived in fresh context: units, positivity, $K\to\infty$ and single-line limits, edge-pole exclusion, guarded #192 near classification. Host regressions cover nonuniform/overlapping-near cases. Device regression and host/device agreement in `test_sinc_bin_integration_cuda.py`; matched 5 MeV h-BN and silicon accuracy and timing in job 4072 (#1003).
- **Anchor:** `tests/montecarlo/spectrum/test_sinc_bin_near_far.py`, `tests/montecarlo/spectrum/test_sinc_bin_integration_cuda.py`
- **Notes:** Issue #248. A fresh-context verifier rederived the equation, units, positivity, limits, and guarded float32 near boundary at `bb5d80a8` (2026-09-28). The first pass at `5f4cd571` found an unguarded 64-width boundary mismatch, fixed by the radius guard and pinned on both sides. Small CUDA cases retain the #192 all-pairs reduction; host line-shape assembly is unchanged. RTX 5080 production-case accuracy and timing are pending. Human sign-off pending. [derivation and verification](radiation-physics/sinc-bin-near-far.md) The fused route (#1003, default from $10^9$ pairs) keeps this near/far partition and sums near pairs in fixed point, bitwise independent of order, with pair rounding at most $2^{-61}\sum|w_l|$. A fresh-context check (2026-11-06, `0db76d387`) rederived the partition, the bound and expression identity with the tree; it read the code before deriving, so it is not code-blind, and device behaviour rests on job 4059. Against the tree on CUDA flush sums: per-bin relative deviation at most $3.0\times10^{-9}$ with a floor of $10^{-6}$ of peak, integral at most $2.0\times10^{-12}$. The integral offset is systematic and negative: 29-38% of pairs round to exactly zero, and a host replay reproduces it to about 0.2%. Without the floor the agreement is not float32 at every bin: h-BN far-tail bins near $6\times10^{-11}$ of peak reach $2.3\times10^{-6}$ relative. Edge reuse (`14c430d5b`) passes each edge's `Si` tail down the warp; its sums are bitwise equal to the kernel without it (job 4067). End to end (job 4072), h-BN and silicon 5 MeV spectra stay within 2 float32 ulps of the pre-#1003 tree in every bin above $10^{-6}$ of peak. [derivation](radiation-physics/sinc-bin-near-far.md) ROCm (#1196, 2026-11-14): the fused near kernel launched one 32-lane warp per job; past $2^{32}$ work-items HIP silently truncated the grid (the claim was unaffected). On a layered MoS$_2$-on-Si g30 replay it kept 1.5% (chunk 78) and 3.3% (chunk 0) of the yield on a Radeon 8060S (jobs 12572, 12658, 12667). The kernel now grid-strides with at most $2^{16}$ blocks; the same chunks then match the CUDA near/far result (job 12657: yield $1-4\times10^{-9}$, $1.3\times10^{-7}$ of peak) and the Fourier route (job 12659: yield $8.4\times10^{-8}$). `test_sparse_near_keeps_every_job_past_a_uint32_thread_grid` fails before the fix on ROCm (yield 0.13) and passes after it (job 12585). CUDA verification on MoLab (NVIDIA RTX PRO 6000 Blackwell Server Edition, commit `748ef8250`): both `test_sinc_bin_integration_cuda.py` and `test_sinc_bin_sparse.py` pass with slow tests enabled (30 passed in 19.66 s); the uint32-grid regression passes in 1.31 s. **#1390:** since `fourier-auto-v9` NumPy-backend bin-mean lines on non-uniform axes are queued per target (`_bin_queue.HostSegmentedLineQueues`). Each maximal uniform run of at least 64 bins takes the host Fourier route on its own sub-axis (`_fourier_host.host_segmented_bin_mean_reduce`; a bin mean depends only on its own edges, so this is the `cxr-line-fourier-reduction` claim on each run, with no far envelope there). The remaining bins take the numba near/far evaluator `_bin_mean_jit.host_near_far_edges_reduce` on the actual edges: the same float32 far classification and envelope, the device-preamble `Si` for near pairs, FP64 per-bin sums independent of thread count, no $(\text{line},\text{bin})$ matrix. Fourier-run bins agree with exact bin means within $5\times10^{-6}$ of the peak and near/far bins with the dense `_host_bin_mean` within $10^{-12}$ (`test_sinc_bin_integration.py::test_host_segmented_queue_matches_exact_and_near_far_bin_means_per_run`, `::test_nonuniform_bin_mean_spectrum_takes_the_queue_and_matches_dense`). On the first #1320 thick-target flush (4.25M lines, 85,246 bins, 19 runs covering 85,194 bins) the segmented result is within $8.9\times10^{-8}$ of the peak of exact bin means (5k-line sample), versus $1.3\times10^{-5}$ for the 64-width envelope. `bin_reduction_override` keeps the dense host evaluator. The CuPy route mirrors this (`resolve_bin_reduction` → `"segmented"`): each run takes the device Fourier route and all leftover bins one near/far launch on a joined axis (`_fourier_host.leftover_bins`; gaps between runs become discarded bins), pinned per run against exact and near/far means by `test_fourier_cuda.py::test_segmented_device_route_matches_exact_and_near_far_per_run` (RTX 5080). The #1320 case at N=8 on that card went from 5327 s to 559 s end to end. Since `fourier-auto-v10` (#1392) the Fourier runs share one line transform on both backends (`cxr-line-fourier-reduction`); the leftover near/far bins are unchanged.


## `cxr-line-fourier-reduction`

- **Claim:** on uniform bins $c_k=c_0+kh$ (unclamped `bin_axis` convention), the incoherent bin-mean line sum $\sum_l w_l\bar S_{l,k}$ equals the period-$P$ Fourier synthesis $\frac1P[G_0+2\operatorname{Re}\sum_{j\ge1}G_je^{2\pi ijk/N}]$ with $G_j=\operatorname{sinc}(t_jh/2\pi)\sum_lw_l(\pi/a_l)(1-t_j/2a_l)_+e^{-i(E_l-c_0)t_j}$, $t_j=2\pi j/P$, modes folded modulo $N=P/h$, minus the closed-form envelope image sum $\sum_l\frac{w_l}{2a_l^2}(\pi/P)^2[\csc^2(\pi u_l/P)-(\pi u_l/P)^{-2}]$. With $D=P-U-h/2\ge h$ ($U$ the band-plus-resonance span), the remaining image error (oscillatory remainder plus bin-centre envelope evaluation) is at most $w_lW_l^2(1+h^2/D^2)/(6D^2)$ per bin and $W_l^2/(4\pi D^2)+\pi^2W_lh^2B/(270D^4)$ of the line mass in the yield of a band of length $B$, $W_l=\pi/a_l$
- **Code:** `montecarlo/spectrum/cxr/_bin_fourier.py::fourier_bin_mean_reduce`, `::fourier_period`, `::image_residual_bounds`, `::uniform_bin_axis`, `montecarlo/spectrum/cxr/_fourier_cutoffs.py::cutoff_rectangles`, `::partitioned_positive_modes`, `montecarlo/spectrum/cxr/_fourier_transform.py::direct_mode_transform`, `::finufft_mode_transform`, `::hybrid_mode_transform`, `montecarlo/spectrum/cxr/_fourier_cuda.py::cuda_fourier_bin_mean_reduce`, `::cuda_positive_modes`, `montecarlo/spectrum/cxr/_fourier_spread.py::es_mode_transform`, `montecarlo/spectrum/cxr/_fourier_host.py::host_fourier_bin_mean_reduce`, `::host_es_mode_transform`, `montecarlo/spectrum/cxr/_bin_quadrature.py::resolve_host_bin_reduction`, `montecarlo/spectrum/cxr/_bin_queue.py::HostBinLineQueues`, `montecarlo/spectrum/cxr/_fourier_runs.py::fourier_runs_reduce`, `::shared_run_period`, `::shared_broad_width`, `montecarlo/spectrum/cxr/_fourier_host.py::host_fourier_runs_reduce`, `::host_es_mode_evaluate`, `montecarlo/spectrum/cxr/_fourier_spread.py::es_mode_evaluate`, `montecarlo/spectrum/cxr/_fourier_cuda.py::cuda_fourier_runs_reduce`
- **Source:** `sinc-bin-integration` profile and bin convention; Fourier transform of $\operatorname{sinc}^2$ (triangle on $|t|<2a$); Poisson summation; $\sum_n(x+n\pi)^{-2}=\csc^2x$; integration by parts for the oscillatory remainder ([derivation](radiation-physics/cxr-line-fourier-reduction.md))
- **Status:** anchored
- **Checks:** Required: triangle transform and its support $2a$; Poisson identity and exact mode folding for lines narrower than a bin; envelope image closed form and its Laurent evaluation; both remainder bounds and the $P\ge2U'$ condition; period choice meets `residual_tol`; limits (total mass $G_0$, delta line, $P\to\infty$); host agreement with `_host_bin_mean(exact_widths=None)` on random, narrow and out-of-band line sets
- **Anchor:** `tests/montecarlo/spectrum/test_bin_fourier.py`; FP32 direct bound (v6): `tests/montecarlo/spectrum/test_fourier_cuda.py::test_fp32_direct_phase_factors_stay_inside_their_bound`; shared-mode runs (v10): `tests/montecarlo/spectrum/test_fourier_host.py::test_shared_mode_runs_match_the_per_run_route_and_exact_bin_means`, `::test_host_type2_matches_direct_sums_and_is_the_spreader_adjoint`
- **Notes:** Issue #1192. Fresh-context verification (2026-11-14, `7b216478d`) returned a narrow `discrepancy`: the yield bound omitted the bin-centre envelope term and the $\csc^2$ Laurent coefficients were misstated. Both were fixed, and the re-check rederived the claim; its 50-digit residual cases sit at 0.0026–0.20 of the corrected bounds. Anchored by the host regression against the exact bin mean. Human sign-off pending. Since `CXR_BIN_REDUCTION_MODEL = "fourier-auto-v4"` the CUDA bin-mean reduction uses this route by default on uniform unclamped axes (`_fourier_cuda.py`, `_bin_quadrature.py::resolve_bin_reduction`): cuFINUFFT on CUDA, the portable ES transform (`_fourier_spread.py`) on ROCm; other axes, CPU, and CUDA without cuFINUFFT keep the near/far and host routes. Layered g30 frozen chunks (job 12560, RTX 5080): 85–112× faster, yield within $8.4\times10^{-8}$, band metrics within $1.5\times10^{-6}$. Nonuniform and clamped axes are refused. The issue text's support $|t|<a$ is corrected to $|t|<2a$. **#1230:** `fourier-auto-v5` sends NumPy-backend bin-mean scoring on uniform axes through the host port (`_fourier_host.py`: serial numba ES spreader + `scipy.fft`, direct numba sums for small rectangles). It matches exact bin means to 5e-6 of peak and the host reference to 1e-9. `bin_reduction_override("auto")` keeps the dense host evaluator. Fresh-context read-only verification (2026-11-16, `5c301b0bf`) rederived the host transform and queue normalization; 47 focused tests passed, plus signed/narrow/out-of-band and large-rectangle comparisons. Requested ES accuracy is not a rigorous final-error bound; image and broad bounds exclude spreading and rounding error. Human sign-off remains pending. Since `fourier-auto-v6` (#1296) device direct rectangles use FP32 `sincosf` after FP64 range reduction, with stated bound $|\Delta\bar\rho_k|\le\sqrt2\,\delta\sum_l|w_l|$, $\delta=5\times10^{-7}$ ([derivation](radiation-physics/cxr-line-fourier-reduction.md#fp32-direct-phase-factors)); device ES $\epsilon=10^{-9}$. On the #1143 scenes plus mos2 30 keV / 15° (job 13650), v6 vs v5 on one transport: yield/centroid/band mass within $1.4\times10^{-9}$, peak/FWHM within $7.5\times10^{-8}$. Fresh-context read-only verification of the FP32 direct bound (2026-11-18, `61e7636b7`) rederived the per-factor, per-mode and per-bin bounds and the triangle-sum inequality; a CPU emulation gave a largest per-mode error of 0.20 of the bound (median 0.007) and a largest per-bin error of $2\times10^{-4}$ of $\sqrt2\,\delta\sum|w|$. The stated $\delta$ holds for $|j\phi_l|\lesssim2\times10^9$ rad. Not run on device hardware. **#1297:** since `fourier-auto-v7` the device ES transform spreads by sorted, batched warp gathers (`_fourier_spread.es_gather_moments`, `spread="gather"`; `spread="atomic"` keeps v6). It forms the same (source, point, $\phi$) terms, so only FP64 rounding changes (summation order, FMA contraction) ([derivation](radiation-physics/cxr-line-fourier-reduction.md#warp-gather-es-spreading)). Anchored by the device regression against the atomic spreader and direct mode sums across batches and the $2\pi$ wrap. On the mos2 30 keV / 15° flushes (job 13668, RTX 5080) flushes run 1.31–1.54× faster than v6 and match v6 to $\le4.7\times10^{-15}$ of the peak. On the #1143 scenes plus mos2 30 keV, v7 vs v6 on one transport: yield and band mass within $2.3\times10^{-15}$, centroid within $1.5\times10^{-11}$ eV, peak and FWHM unchanged. Fresh-context read-only verification (2026-11-18, `52796a913`) rederived the term-set equivalence. A CPU emulation of the key, item, batch and gather index logic reproduced the atomic spreader's (rectangle, source, point, unwrapped $l$, $z$) multiset exactly on the regression's inputs and 40 randomized cases. The verifier did not run it on a device. The gather has not been run on ROCm; its reduction avoids warp shuffles for portability. **#1320:** since `fourier-auto-v8` device lines with more than `FOURIER_MAX_LINE_MODES` $=2^{22}$ positive modes ($\lfloor Pa_l/\pi\rfloor=\lfloor P/W_l\rfloor$) take the near/far kernel (`sinc-bin-near-far`) on the same bins; the bin mean is linear in the lines, and removing lines only shrinks $U$, so $P$ keeps the image bounds of the remaining set. Before, one line far narrower than $P$ (itself $\gtrsim408\,W_{\max}$ of the widest Fourier line) sized the FFT, fine grid and a 4 GiB kernel-transform workspace (thick-target MeV OOM). The host route (`_fourier_host.py`) applies the same cap; its over-cap lines take the numba near/far evaluator `_bin_mean_jit.host_near_far_bin_mean_reduce`, equal to `_host_bin_mean(..., exact_widths=64)` within $\approx4\times10^{-14}$ of the peak (`test_fourier_host.py::test_jit_near_far_matches_the_numpy_bin_mean`). The ES kernel transform and the gather batch grid are now evaluated in bounded blocks (FP64 order only). The Fourier image bounds are unchanged, but over-cap lines carry the `sinc-bin-far-envelope` allowance instead: pointwise $\le1.2\times10^{-5}$ of the line peak (suppressed by $W_l/h$ for $W_l<h$) and yield $\le3.9\times10^{-6}$ of the line mass, against $2\tau$ and $0.48\tau$ on the Fourier route; with $W_l\ge h$ (possible only near $10^6$ bins) the yield term can exceed the Fourier yield bound by up to $8\times$. The device near/far kernel takes float32 line inputs, so `_near_far_lines` passes $E_l-c_0$ and edges$-c_0$ (the bin mean depends only on $E_l-\epsilon$): the position error is $2^{-24}|E_l-c_0|\le2^{-24}U$, not $2^{-24}E_l$. The host JIT keeps FP64 positions. Fresh-context verification (2026-11-19, `cc7a915bb`) rederived the near/far mean, the routing by linearity, the blocking and the batching, and found the float32 position loss and the allowance statement fixed here; status stays `anchored`. Anchor for the device position fix: `test_fourier_cuda.py::test_over_cap_near_far_keeps_fp64_positions_at_high_energy` (device-only). **#1392:** since `fourier-auto-v10` the uniform runs of a `"segmented"` axis share one exact-cutoff line transform per flush, at the largest run period and broad threshold. Each run takes its multiplier $\operatorname{sinc}(t_mh_j/2\pi)$ and an ES type-2 evaluation at its own centres, the adjoint of the type-1 spreader ([derivation](radiation-physics/cxr-line-fourier-reduction.md#shared-mode-runs-on-piecewise-uniform-axes-1392)). The synthesis holds at any centre and any period that meets the clearance, and the image and broad bounds fall as $P$ and $W_0$ grow, so every run keeps its own bounds; the type-2 adds the ES tolerance $\epsilon$. Uniform axes are unchanged. On the #1320 flush (host, 16 threads) the runs take 20.9 s instead of 93.6 s and match the per-run route within $1.3\times10^{-9}$ of peak. Fresh-context verification (2026-11-23, `252375548`) rederived the synthesis at arbitrary centres and periods, the common-$P$/$W_0$ bound inheritance, the type-2 adjointness and the single-run limit; host numerics only, device code compared on paper. Status stays `anchored`. **#1403:** since `fourier-auto-v11` the device near/far kernel splits lines across chunks when bins are few (grid `(bins, chunks)`, `_line_chunks`), and the `"segmented"` flush evaluates only its real leftover bins. Each chunk keeps its own FP64 near and compensated float32 far sum, and the chunk totals add in FP64, so only summation order changes. The chunk count follows the SM count, so last-bit results can differ between devices.

## `cxr-line-broad-interpolation`

- **Claim:** for scalar sinc-squared lines of first-zero widths $W_l\ge W_0$, a 64-root Chebyshev interpolant of uniform-bin means on centre span $L$ has absolute error at most $[2(\pi L/(2W_0))^{64}/64!+127\cdot2(\pi h/W_0)^8/8!]\sum_l|w_l|$ before floating-point errors; the second term includes amplified four-point Gauss bin-averaging error
- **Code:** `montecarlo/spectrum/cxr/_bin_broad.py::broad_bin_mean_reduce`, `::broad_bin_mean_error_bound`, `montecarlo/spectrum/cxr/_bin_fourier.py::fourier_bin_mean_reduce`
- **Source:** triangular transform and bin convention of `cxr-line-fourier-reduction`; derivative bound from compact positive Fourier measure; Chebyshev interpolation remainder and exactness of four-point Gauss quadrature through degree seven ([implementation-context derivation](radiation-physics/cxr-line-fourier-reduction.md#broad-line-split))
- **Status:** rederived
- **Checks:** Required: fresh-context verification of derivative, interpolation and amplified quadrature bounds; source-to-code agreement; broad-only and mixed exact-bin comparisons; signed weights, remote resonances and vanishing bin width; refusal of insufficient resolution; frozen chunk comparison with production energy-dependent factors kept explicit
- **Anchor:** `tests/montecarlo/spectrum/test_bin_broad.py`
- **Notes:** Issue #1192. Used by the default CUDA route (`cuda_fourier_bin_mean_reduce`, $W_0=1000$ eV); the host reference keeps `broad_width_eV=None`. Fresh-context verification (2026-11-14) returned `rederived` ([verification](radiation-physics/cxr-line-broad-interpolation.md)): every factor matches; independent mpmath bin means stay within 1.1e-12 against bounds of 2.7–4.5e-6. The bound is conservative (127 against a Lebesgue constant of 3.61; the moment bound is loose by $(m+1)(m+2)/2$), so some valid splits are refused. Mixed splits add the broad and Fourier pointwise bounds. Human sign-off pending.

## `cbs-amplitude`

- **Claim:** `U_g` CBS potential amplitude + relativistic 1/γ braced terms; production `A_PXR`/`A_CBS` are the exact second-order elements (quantum intermediate denominators and convective transition currents) whose ħ → 0 limit is the braced form
- **Code:** `materials/crystal.py::U_g`; `montecarlo/spectrum/cxr/_recoil.py::_quantum_line_factors`, consumed by `montecarlo/spectrum/cxr/_kernels.py::_cxr_amp_sq_core`, `montecarlo/spectrum/cxr/_per_hkl.py::_accumulate_reflection`, `montecarlo/spectrum/cxr/_batched.py::_batched_coherent_block` and `montecarlo/spectrum/cxr/_batched.py::_batched_incoherent_block`; CUDA twins `montecarlo/spectrum/coherent_prologue_jit_kernel.py::_quantum_pxr_numerator` and `montecarlo/spectrum/coherent_prologue_jit_kernel.py::_quantum_cbs_factor`
- **Source:** Feranchuk 2000; Shi et al., Nat. Commun. 15, 7803 (2024), SI Eqs. (S12)–(S15) with the exactly conserved convective transition current `(p+p')/(E+E')` in place of Shi's printed `u_q ≈ u_qi` approximation (which drops the `{g;e}` term, #1103)
- **Status:** anchored
- **Checks:** units+limits+signs (`U_g` rederived from Poisson); braced `A_CBS` tensor prefactor, single `1/γ`, `(v·g)` / `(v·g)²` denominator placement, and the PXR+CBS relative sign independently rederived from the relativistic equation of motion plus the Liénard–Wiechert radiation integral, with `A_PXR` rederived in the same normalization; certified numerically against direct RK4 trajectory integration (rel. dev. `<3e-6` at 30/100/300 keV, both polarizations; the `1/γ²` variant is rejected by exactly a factor `γ`); 2026-11-12 fresh-context (#1134): exact second-order elements rederived (two-ordering second-order PT, convective currents); closed form, cancellation identity, h → 0 limit, CUDA twins and the k/g²/k² inputs at all four production sites verified; independent 60-digit reference ≤ 4e-13 over 10 keV–1 GeV
- **Anchor:** `tests/montecarlo/spectrum/test_quantum_cb_elements.py::test_reference_pin_si_222_30kev`: production `pxr_num`/`f_cbs`, quantum and classical, σ and π, at Si (222) 30 keV, θ = 100°, exact vacuum root, against an independent 60-digit mpmath reference (`checks/quantum_cb_elements_1134.py::reference_pin`, no PyRITE physics imports) to rtol 1e-12; the classical σ value pins the single `1/γ`
- **Notes:** `U_g` independently rederived term-for-term from Poisson: `(4π e²/V g²) Σ(Z−f) e^{ig·r} e^{−W}`, Mott–Bethe `Z−f`, single Debye–Waller, eV units (Si(111) \|U_g\|≈5.2 eV). **2026-08-15**: the braced assembly is now rederived too. Code's **single `1/γ`** is correct — the exact equation of motion `a = (1/γm)(1 − ββ)·F` pairs one `1/γ` with the transverse projector, so `1/γ²` would double-count the longitudinal suppression the projector already carries. Relative PXR/CBS sign is correct via two cancelling flips (the `+e φ_g` branch of `U_g`, and the explicit leading `−` in `A_CBS`), leaving a shared overall `−1` that `\|·\|²` removes. `1/γ` vs `1/γ²` changes the CBS amplitude by 5.5 / 16.4 / 37.0 % and a real Si(111) line intensity by 3.3 / 9.9 / 21.1 % at 30 / 100 / 300 keV — **distinguishable from ~10 keV up, not only ≳100 keV** as previously recorded. Convention notes: returns `+\|U_g\|` (`−U_electron`) branch, phase fixed downstream; **#513 resolved: `U_g` is the static `Z − f0` potential for all elements** (no `f'`, `use_henke` ignored; `f'`/`f''` stay in `χ_g`); Si (220) `B=0.48` Å² now 4.3164 eV at every energy; `CXR_ESCAPE_MODEL` bumped to v4. Awaiting fresh-context confirmation. **Transcription caveat:** neither Feranchuk Eq.(14) nor Zhai SI Eq.(6) text is in-repo, so what is certified is that coded `A_CBS` is the correct CBS amplitude with the correct relative normalization against coded `A_PXR` — *not* that it faithfully transcribes those equations; a human with the papers should confirm the citation. **2026-08-20: the `v·g → 0` filter's justification is corrected.** That filter passed "with the cutoff noted as load-bearing", meaning the production `E_res > 10 eV` window was believed to bound `v·g` away from zero. It does not: it bounds `v·g = ω denom`, so as `denom = 1 − Re n (v·n̂)` collapses it stops bounding `v·g`. A real thick-target sample (graphite, 100 μm, 300 keV) reached `v·g = 1.727e-3` — four orders below the median `|v·g|` — at `E_res = 4942.7 eV`, well inside the window, violating this row's own validity condition `|U_g| g²/(γ mc² (v·g)²) ≪ 1` by 15.6× and inflating the integrated characteristic line by ten orders, finite so nothing flagged it. What bounds the limit is the convergence guard now carried by the in-medium resonance solve (`xray-in-medium-resonance`), which rejects exactly those samples; the 10 eV cut remains as an energy-window/out-of-grid filter only. Derivation, `U_g`, the braced assembly, the single `1/γ` and the relative sign are untouched and `rederived` stands. **Owed to this row:** the write-up's cheap-filter table row is corrected in place and carries a 2026-08-20 addendum, but wants rewording alongside a fresh-context re-run by whoever owns this validation, not by the branch (`feature/relativistic-bethe-stopping`) that found the defect. Human sign-off pending. [write-up](radiation-physics/cbs-amplitude.md) **2026-11-12 (#1134):** amplitudes moved to the exact second-order elements in a cancellation-free closed form; `h = ħc/mc² → 0` reproduces the braced classical form identically (any `k`), an independent derivation of the single `1/γ`, the `(v·g)` denominators and the relative sign. Sum line-yield change on Shi Fig. 2 Si rows +1.3 to +6.2 % at 10–30 keV, +1.0 to +1.7 % at 100 keV, ≤ 0.2 % at 5 MeV; Huang HOPG/hBN (002)+(004) −1.9 / −1.7 %; per row up to tens of percent. `CXR_ESCAPE_MODEL` → `segment-mean-v7-quantum-elements`. Fresh-context re-verification returned `rederived` (2026-11-12). Scope: second order in χ_g/U_g, plane-wave states, spinless convective current; Dirac magnetic-moment terms are O(h²) in intensity (~2.6 % at 10 keV on the Shi rows). [#1134 section](radiation-physics/cbs-amplitude.md#2026-11-12--exact-second-order-elements-1134); check `checks/quantum_cb_elements_1134.py`. **2026-11-12 (#1144):** anchored to the 60-digit reference pin (production agrees to 7.8e-16).

## `line-grid-kinematic-bandwidth`

- **Claim:** automatic case-local line grids bound their upper edge by `E_res <= ħc β |g|_max / (1 − β)`, the direction-maximized form of the PXR/CBS resonance `E_res = ħc (v·g)/(1 − n̂·v)`, evaluated at the speed of the declared upper incident energy `E_up = E₀(1 + 6 f)` (`f = energy_spread_frac`; `E₀` for a monoenergetic beam; the sampler clamps its Gaussian draw at `|z| ≤ 6`, so no electron starts above `E_up`) over the case's own reflection set — in a layered target, the union of every radiating layer's reflection set, since one line axis carries every radiator's lines. Externally imported GPT/GDF per-electron energies are outside this claim
- **Code:** `_cxr_grid_policy.py::kinematic_cxr_stop_eV` (consumed by `campaign/sweep.py::_automatic_cxr_grid_policy`)
- **Source:** no new physical law — the resonance is `line-energy-dispersion` / `finite-time-lineshape`, implemented in `montecarlo/spectrum/cxr/_kernels.py::_cxr_kin_core`. This row claims only the bound taken from it
- **Status:** rederived
- **Checks:** Required: that maximizing numerator and denominator independently over direction is legitimate here (it is a bound, so independence is conservative, not exact); that `β` at the incident energy is the maximum over the trajectory (slowing down is monotone, so lines only move down); that the reflection set is complete for the case. Numeric sanity against the measured `energy_grid.derive` stops: the bound sits 1.6–2.8× above the derived 95%-coverage `stop` at 300 keV for mose2/hopg, and reproduces the 30–60 keV derived stops within ~5% when the fixed-geometry denominator `1 − β cos θ_obs` is used instead — i.e. the gap at high energy is multiple scattering rotating `v` toward `n̂`, which is exactly what the bound is there to cover
- **Anchor:** `tests/energy-grid/test_line_grid_policy.py::test_kinematic_stop_bounds_the_resonance_over_random_directions`; `tests/energy-grid/test_line_grid_policy.py::test_kinematic_stop_is_attained_and_matches_reference_values` (aligned-direction supremum and independent reference values)
- **Notes:** This is a BANDWIDTH policy, not an accuracy claim: it says the grid cannot clip a line, not that the line is sampled finely enough. Accuracy is the separate resolution policy (`sinc_feature_spacing` under a per-observable tolerance). Being a bound rather than an estimate, it is deliberately wider than the measured `coverage-0.95` artifacts — an automatic grid costs more points than a derived one, and that is the correct trade when no derived row exists. It is *not* a bound on the coherent-route CBS continuum, which is not a resonance; the #97 handoff records that the coherent route's aliasing was never analyzed. Human sign-off pending Fresh-context review (2026-11-11, #4 batch 2, verdict `discrepancy`; [derivation](beam-transport/line-grid-kinematic-bandwidth.md)): the closed form, ħc/γ/β, reflection set (the truncated `hkl_list` the kernels receive) and `ceil` rounding match an independent derivation, and the separate direction maximization is the exact supremum (both extremes at n̂ ∥ v̂ ∥ ĝ); the in-medium correction keeps the denominator ≥ 1 − β for Re n ≤ 1. First divergent factor: β is taken at nominal `energy_keV` (`campaign/sweep.py::_automatic_cxr_grid_policy`), while `energy_spread_frac` samples an unbounded Gaussian E_e = E0(1 + f z_e), so the bound is not strict for spread beams (f = 0.01, +3σ, HOPG-like |g|: +3.3 % at 300 keV, 26534 eV vs the 25700 eV stop; absorbed by rounding at 30 keV). Remediation (owner choice, not applied): scope the claim to monoenergetic beams, or evaluate β at a declared upper incident energy and re-verify. Also unverified: GPT/GDF per-electron energies; Re n > 1 soft-x-ray windows (catalog scan: only PtTe2 at 10 keV touches the unrounded bound, below the rounded stop). The docstring now states the monoenergetic and `Re n <= 1` scope and the corrected `spectrum/cxr/_kernels.py` path. **Remediation (2026-11-11, #1101, implementation context; owner choice):** β is evaluated at the declared upper incident energy `E_up = E₀(1 + 6 f)` via `montecarlo/transport/beam_entry.py::upper_beam_energy_keV`, and `initial_energies_keV` clamps the standardized draw to `|z| ≤ 6` (previously bounded only by the inverse-CDF limit 8.2095), making the bound strict for analytic spread beams. f = 0.01 HOPG at 300 keV: stop 27400 eV (was 25700), covering the 26534 eV +3σ resonance. The energy-conservation cap of `line-grid-emission-ceiling` uses `E_up` too. Spread-beam automatic cases change identity through the hashed `line_grid_policy`. Anchor `tests/energy-grid/test_line_grid_policy.py::test_spread_beam_bounds_use_the_declared_upper_incident_energy`. **2026-11-11 (#1101), fresh-context re-verification (declared upper energy): `rederived`.** β at `E_up`; the bound is the attained supremum and increases with kinetic energy, so the maximum incident energy suffices; 14/14 independent mpmath stops (three |g|, 30–1000 keV, f 0–0.05) match exactly; real `Sweep`/`build_cases` HOPG stops 25700, 27400 (300 keV, f 0.01) and 4400 eV (30 keV, f 0.05) match. Route trace: every analytic sampler uses the clamped draw, launched passes carry no spread, transport never gains energy, GDF sweeps take the realized maximum; stored catalog rows remain nominal-energy and are not automatic grids. `Re n ≤ 1` not re-examined. [Re-verification](beam-transport/line-grid-kinematic-bandwidth.md#independent-re-verification-fresh-context-2026-11-11-declared-upper-energy). **2026-11-13 (#1164), implementation context:** the reflection set was the film's only, so in a layered target the substrate's lines ran past the axis stop (MoS₂ 2000 Å on 20 µm Si, 30 keV, `n_families=4`: stop 3.0 keV where the Si reflections need 4.5 keV; 1.08–1.15e-3 of line yield above it; #1143 Plan 3, jobs 12280/12282). `_automatic_cxr_grid_policy` now takes `|g|` over the film and every crystalline layer radiator (`campaign/sweep.py::_stack_line_radiators`, the same `layer_radiator` reflections the case scores), and the `line-grid-emission-ceiling` data limit as the lowest over those radiators. Such cases record `sources["bandwidth_radiators"] = "stack-radiator-union-v1"` and change identity through the hashed `line_grid_policy`; bare-slab policies are bit-identical. Anchor `tests/scan/test_sweep.py::test_layered_sweep_grids_span_every_layer_and_leave_the_film_alone`. **Layered rerun (#1164, job 12293, 12115 transports, 3 seeds):** line yield above the production stop at the default `n_families=4` is 1.87–1.92e-4 (was 1.08–1.15e-3), and ≤ 8.6e-4 at every ladder point n = 1–16 (was 21–23 % at n=1). **2026-11-13 (#1164), fresh-context verification of the radiator union: `rederived`.** `_stack_line_radiators` uses the same `layer_radiator` calls as `_TargetGeometry.lower` (`max_reflections` truncates the film only in both); max over radiators is the bound for a shared axis (independent MoS₂ on Si 30 keV: 4464.8 eV → 4500 eV); min data ceiling is required on a shared axis (same 966200 eV stop for HOPG on W at 3 MeV); amorphous-only stacks keep the bare-slab payload. The identity fork fires for any crystalline layer even when the stop does not move (conservative). Stored or declared line grids bypass this policy (no catalog stack declares one).

## `line-grid-emission-ceiling`

- **Claim:** the automatic `kinematic-ceiling` line axis, and the ceiling of the measured `resonance-population` axis, stop at `min(E_kin, E_data, E_res,max)` on the 100 eV lattice (rounded up, except that a binding `E_data` rounds down to the largest node strictly below it, so the coherent route's output-axis refraction stays inside its table support): the `line-grid-kinematic-bandwidth` bound `E_res,max`, the declared upper beam kinetic energy `E_kin = E_up` (`E₀(1 + 6 f)`, see `line-grid-kinematic-bandwidth`; energy conservation: no photon exceeds the radiating electron's kinetic energy, while the no-recoil resonance bound grows as `~2γ²ħc|g|` and passes `E_kin` near 17 MeV for HOPG (004)), and the atomic-data emission ceiling `E_data = min` of the NaN onsets of the line tables (anomalous `χ_g`, `Re n`, each absorber's `μ_i`; static `U_g` stays finite past the anomalous end; see `line-tabulation-nan-ceiling`). A line whose factors read NaN is dropped: the incoherent routes mask nonfinite total weight, and the coherent routes (per-hkl `_formation_good`, batched `_batched_coherent_block`, which also feeds the CUDA kernels) mask nonfinite complex polarization coefficients as well as scalar amplitude and formation terms. This is a numerical data-domain cut, not a physical end of emission. When energy conservation or the data ceiling binds, that limit is recorded as `sources["stop_eV"]` and joins case identity; a binding or tied resonance bound adds no entry. Measured-bandwidth (`resonance-population`) policies take this clamped stop as their ceiling (#1243), so their measured stop never passes the default stop
- **Code:** `campaign/sweep.py::_automatic_cxr_grid_policy`; `montecarlo/spectrum/cxr/_kernels.py::_cxr_emission_ceiling`, `::_cxr_table_nan_onsets`; masks `montecarlo/spectrum/cxr/_per_hkl.py::_formation_good`, `montecarlo/spectrum/cxr/_batched.py::_batched_coherent_block`; upper-only helper `directional.py::line_window_ceiling_eV` (omits the data cap by design)
- **Source:** energy conservation for `E_kin`; the atomic-table domains of `materials/atomic.py::henke_dispersion` (Chantler interior) and the EPDL2025 attenuation band (`E ≤ EPDL_E_MAX_EV = 100 GeV`) for `E_data`. The resonance itself is `line-energy-dispersion`
- **Status:** anchored
- **Checks:** HOPG: the stop is unchanged at 30 keV–3 MeV (kinematic bound 3.7–686.7 keV is lowest), and capped below `E_data` at 5, 10 and 50 MeV — stop 966.2 keV under the 966266.74 eV carbon Chantler end (binding through `χ_g`/`Re n`; 966.3 keV, rounded up, from issue #274 until #1101), 800.1 keV (the Elam scattering end) before it — against kinematic bounds of 1.71, 6.24 and 144.4 MeV. With `E_data` removed, 50 MeV caps at `E_kin = 50 MeV`. A `resonance-population` policy takes the same clamped ceiling: HOPG 5 MeV 966.2 keV; MoS₂ under the 75 Å⁻¹ selection cap stops at `E_kin` = 30 and 60 keV (#1243; before, the unclamped cap let the measured stop reach 72.8 and 98.7 keV, SLURM 13257/13260)

## `line-grid-resonance-bandwidth`

- **Claim:** the `resonance-population` bandwidth (the automatic default since #1243; `kinematic-ceiling` where it does not apply, see Notes) stops the line axis at the smallest `stop` (rounded up to 100 eV, capped by the clamped `line-grid-emission-ceiling` stop, itself at most `line-grid-kinematic-bandwidth`) where (1) the production-weight mass of the case's in-medium resonance population above `stop` is at most `ε/2` (`BANDWIDTH_PROXY_SAFETY`), using the per-line bound `min(1, w/(π² D))` on the `sinc²` tail beyond distance `D` for first-zero width `w = π/a_w`, and (2) every emitted characteristic line keeps all but `ε` of its Lorentzian mass, via `Γ/(2πD)`; with `ε = DEFAULT_BANDWIDTH_TRUNCATION = 1e-4` of integrated incoherent line yield. The stop is then raised, still under the ceiling, to cover every diffracted-bremsstrahlung/Kossel Bragg window the ceiling axis admits (both corrections are exactly zero outside that finite window). The spectrum phase re-evaluates the bound for lines inside the ceiling's keep window (resonances below `C + 0.2 (C − start)` for ceiling `C`; lines at or above `S` count wholly) and refuses the case when the upper-edge fraction exceeds `ε`, except that an axis already at its ceiling warns instead. The certificate covers native PXR/CBS and characteristic lines; DBR/Kossel are covered by window containment only, and a signed Kossel correction is not part of the `ε` denominator. Flight-grouped numerical substeps (`max_dE_frac > 0`) are refused by every truncation audit (`collect`, edge, `mass_only`, adaptive); no flight-field tail certificate exists
- **Code:** `montecarlo/spectrum/cxr_seeds.py::sincsq_upper_tail_bound`, `::resonance_population_stop_eV`, `::characteristic_stop_eV`, `::case_cxr_stop_eV` (edge); `montecarlo/spectrum/cxr/_kernels.py::_accumulate_edge_truncation`, `montecarlo/runner/cxr_grid.py::check_cxr_truncation` (audit); policy `_cxr_grid_policy.py::resolve_cxr_grid_policy` (`bandwidth = "resonance-population"`); default selection `_cxr_grid_policy.py::default_bandwidth_policy`, windows fallback `::_resolve_bandwidth`; post-transport fallback `::kinematic_fallback_payload`, `montecarlo/runner/bandwidth_fallback.py::measured_bandwidth_fallback` with stop extension `::extension_stop_ladder`, `::extension_stop`; unaudited-axis fallback `montecarlo/runner/cxr_grid.py::_auditable_policy` (scalar, observation and streaming), Bragg-window coverage `::_cover_bragg_support` with `montecarlo/runner/diffracted_brem.py::bragg_support_stop_eV` and `montecarlo/spectrum/diffracted_brem.py::bragg_window_upper_eV`, replay guard `montecarlo/runner/cxr_grid.py::check_recorded_line_axis`; `_cxr_grid_policy.py::uniform_step_divisor` (legacy `resonance-local` guard, #1265)
- **Source:** no new physical law — the `sinc²` lineshape and `a_w` of `finite-time-lineshape`, the in-medium resonance of `line-energy-dispersion`/`xray-in-medium-resonance` as solved by `line-window-seeding`, and the Lorentzian lines of `characteristic-radiation`. The tail bounds are elementary: `∫_D^∞ sin²(a x)/(a x)² dx ≤ 1/(a² D)` against the whole-line integral `π/a`; `1/2 − arctan(2D/Γ)/π ≤ Γ/(2πD)`
- **Status:** rederived
- **Checks:** Required: that each line's weight is energy-independent in both incoherent routes (it is evaluated at `E_res`, so truncated mass is exactly weight × tail); both tail inequalities and their direction; that collection and audit see every line both routes deposit (after the `keep`/finite-weight filters); that the ceiling cap preserves `line-grid-kinematic-bandwidth`; that the characteristic line set matches `characteristic_line_seeds`; limiting case — a single straight flight gives `stop = E_res + w/(π² ε/2)` before rounding
- **Anchor:** `tests/energy-grid/test_line_grid_bandwidth.py` (incl. `::test_selected_reflections_feed_the_measured_bandwidth_audit`, slow); `::test_defaulted_measured_bandwidth_falls_back_to_the_explicit_kinematic_payload`, `::test_defaulted_refusal_rescores_on_the_ceiling_and_explicit_refusal_raises`, `::test_audited_keep_window_reaches_the_kinematic_window`, `::test_unmeasured_line_axis_routes_resolve_the_kinematic_payload`, `::test_bragg_support_bounds_every_dbr_and_kossel_window_node`, `::test_measured_stop_rises_to_the_bragg_support_under_the_ceiling`, `::test_measured_grid_cache_key_carries_the_coverage_revision`, `::test_observation_resolution_shares_the_unmeasured_axis_fallback`, `::test_replay_refuses_a_recorded_axis_short_of_todays_requirement`, `::test_measured_cache_key_separates_mosaic_from_perfect_crystals`; `tests/energy-grid/test_line_grid_windows.py::test_windows_keep_the_default_bandwidth_kinematic`; remote measurement via `python -m pyrite.energy_grid.convergence_job start-bandwidth`; P4 stop extension `::test_extension_ladder_bounds_match_the_edge_bound_at_each_stop`, `::test_refusal_records_the_smallest_rung_within_the_share`, `::test_defaulted_refusal_rescores_on_the_extended_stop`, `::test_a_refused_rescore_extends_again`, `::test_fallback_loop_refuses_a_stop_that_does_not_rise`, `::test_observation_directions_extend_one_joint_axis`, `::test_extensions_are_capped_before_the_ceiling`, `::test_fallback_rescore_survives_an_allocation_retry`, `::test_defaulted_bin_mean_axis_falls_back_with_its_resolution_unchanged`, `::test_resonance_local_implies_its_bandwidth_and_never_falls_back`, `tests/energy-grid/test_line_grid_selector_table.py`

Five OOM plans across a two-refusal chain gave bit-identical spectra (nesting depth = refusal count). Open: `directions.py::directional_outputs` ran the fallback per direction. A refusal in only some directions raised `ValueError: all input arrays must have the same shape`. When every direction refused, `line_grid_resolved` kept the joint measured record (13851 nodes) while `E_grid`/`spec_by_direction` spanned [50, 119000] eV with 39651 nodes. This predates P4. Suggested: cap the number of refusals; record the measured policy on rescored axes. **Remediation 3 (2026-11-17, #1243, implementation context):** the observation route scores every direction with the per-direction fallback disabled (`_joint_bandwidth_fallback`). On any refusal it rebuilds one axis for all directions through `resolve_observation_cxr_grid` at the extended stop and re-audits every direction on it, repeating until none refuses (`directions.py::_joint_directional_spectra`). It records `bandwidth_fallback` and per-direction `truncation_audits` in `line_grid_resolved`. Stop choice is shared (`runner/bandwidth_fallback.py::fallback_axis_payload`). After `MAX_EXTENSIONS = 2` refusals the axis goes straight to the ceiling. Rescored records name the audited policy (`bandwidth_policy`) and the axis-building one (`axis_policy`). Anchors `::test_observation_directions_extend_one_joint_axis` (reproduces the stacking error without the fix), `::test_extensions_are_capped_before_the_ceiling`. Status `discrepancy` until a fresh-context re-verification. **2026-11-17 (#1243), fresh-context re-verification of remediation 3: `rederived`.** Observation directions are scored on one axis and audited on it; a refusal in any direction (first, middle or last; hBN 1 MeV, 3 directions) rebuilds one joint axis (41800 → 80400 eV, step target 3.0 eV = min over directions) and re-audits all of them. Temporal grids are rebuilt on the new axis; the joint flag stays out of scalar results and identity. `MAX_EXTENSIONS` caps both routes (rungs 41800/46625/51148 eV, then the ceiling with `capped_at_ceiling` audits). The record's consumers copy it and read no field except `E_grid`. The scalar route is unchanged. 120 focused tests pass. Minor, not status-bearing: the rescoring warning precedes a re-raise at the ceiling; an extension can exceed the temporal sample cap (loud `ValueError`); directional `line_roulette`/`reflection_selection` records come from direction 0 (predates P4). Conditional on `line-grid-emission-ceiling` and `line-grid-kinematic-bandwidth`. Human sign-off pending (#277). **2026-11-17 (#1214 on #1243), implementation context:** remediation (A) is replaced. `kinematic_fallback_payload` excludes only `resonance-local` again; a `sinc-shape` axis, defaulted (#1214 bin-mean default) or chosen, falls back to the kinematic default's `sinc-oversampled` resolution (`W/8`) instead of refusing, and never to the `sinc-nyquist` step that (A) caught. Open: the oversampled bin-mean bound is `(π²/9)/64 = 1.7e-2` against `sinc-shape`'s 1e-2, so a refused bin-mean axis rescores under a looser bin-mean bound; anchor `::test_sinc_shape_falls_back_to_the_oversampled_step_not_the_nyquist_one`. This clause awaits fresh-context verification. **2026-11-17 (#1265), implementation context:** one fallback rule. `kinematic_fallback_payload` returns the kinematic payload exactly when the bandwidth is `resonance-population` with no `sources["bandwidth"]`; resolution, quadrature and budget are copied unchanged. No resolution depends on the bandwidth any more (`sinc-oversampled` takes the bin-mean shape step on every axis, `line-grid-sinc-shape-spacing`), so the #1214 `sinc-shape → sinc-oversampled` rewrite and its open 1.7e-2 bin-mean clause are retired. `resonance-local` with an unset bandwidth records `sources["bandwidth"] = "implied by resonance-local"` (`_resolve_bandwidth`), so it refuses rather than falls back, as before, now through the same rule. Every allowed combination and its fallback is enumerated against the resolver by `tests/energy-grid/test_line_grid_selector_table.py`, which also pins the table in `docs/repo-design/profile-settings.md`. The tail bounds, audit and ladder are unchanged. This clause awaits fresh-context verification. **2026-11-18 (#1265), fresh-context verification: `discrepancy`, then `rederived`.** First pass: the one rule rederives for payloads the resolver builds, but a pre-#1265 stored `resonance-population` + `resonance-local` payload (no `sources["bandwidth"]`) passed `kinematic_fallback_payload` and could rescore on a uniform sinc-Nyquist axis. Remediation: `uniform_step_divisor` refuses `resonance-local` (and `sinc-shape`) payloads, anchor `tests/energy-grid/test_line_grid_bandwidth.py::test_resonance_local_implies_its_bandwidth_and_never_falls_back`. Re-verification after the rebase onto #1257: every route such a payload reaches (measured refusal rescore, scalar and observation; channeling/TR/mixed `_auditable_policy`; `_kinematic_cxr_grid`; `node_local_axis`; replay) raises before building coordinates; the one-rule fallback otherwise rederives. Minor: the rescoring warning precedes that raise; a speed-cache entry written by the intermediate WIP commit could only exist in developer-local caches. Status stays `rederived`. **2026-11-17 (#1270), host cost only:** `resonance_population_stop_eV` bisects the `round_to_eV` lattice (`lost` is non-increasing in `stop`; at and below the lowest resonance it is exactly one) instead of 60 float halvings, and forms each block's products in one fused pass (`spectrum/_population_tail.py::tail_products`, blocks on threads) with NumPy's dtypes, operation order and pairwise block sums, added in block order. Bound, selector and equation text are unchanged; stops and summaries equal the former search except when a lattice point falls inside its final float bracket. Anchors `tests/energy-grid/test_line_grid_host_search.py::test_tail_products_match_the_numpy_expression_bitwise`, `::test_lattice_search_reproduces_the_float_bisection`. **2026-11-18 (#1275), device route, implementation context:** under a CuPy backend the sinc-spacing samples and quantile, the population collection and the stop search run on the device (`spectrum/_device_sizing.py`). Per-line values use the host route's operations, dtypes and order, one IEEE operation per elementwise kernel, and the tail kernel is compiled without FMA contraction. Only these differ: the `v·n` dot product is summed left to right instead of by BLAS (widths measured ≤ 1e-13 relative on hopg 1000 keV, growing toward forward emission; weights bitwise), and cumulative weight, aliased share and tail and mass sums are float64 device reductions (a float32 population's total mass is summed in float64, where the host sums it in float32). Grouped flights and CPU runs keep the exact host route. Bound, selector, lattice and equation text are unchanged. On hopg 1000 keV (qlmc job 13706, `4a0b6cc`, 2M and 3×20M) `E_grid` and the stops are identical to the host route; `proxy_truncated_fraction` moves 1.3e-9 relative and `aliased_weight_fraction` 9e-13. `LINE_BANDWIDTH_DEFAULT_MODEL` and `LINE_RESOLUTION_DEFAULT_MODEL` were bumped. Anchors `tests/energy-grid/test_line_grid_device_sizing.py` (NumPy-run route; `hardware` CUDA parity). **2026-11-18 (#1275), fresh-context verification of the device route: `rederived`.** The `_TAIL_PREAMBLE` products follow the host's dtypes and order: mass and `stop − energy` in the array dtype, then the float64 bound with the `min(1, ·)` cap. A D ≤ 0 line counts wholly. Emulating the preamble element by element gives the same bits as `tail_products`. The kept-line filter, `n_lines` and the resonance range are identical, the numerator differs by at most 2.1e-16 relative, and lattice stops are identical at three limits. The one precision change is valid: the float64 total of a float32 population is 2e-8 closer to the exact sum than the host's float32 block sums, and it accounts for the 1.3e-9 shift in `proxy_truncated_fraction`. Sinc weights are bitwise equal, grouped or out-of-order flights take the host route, and the quantile picks the same sample under ties without relying on sort stability. The collected population and its order are the same on both sides. CPU runs, CPU recovery (`BACKEND` rebound and read at call time, segments on the host) and the streamed evidence take the host route. 32 focused tests pass and 5 `hardware` tests skip. The evidence citation (job 13706), the 9e-13 figure, the measured width bound and the float32 total-mass note were corrected in the clause. Not anchored: the call-time `BACKEND` read under CPU recovery.
