## `line-grid-resonance-local-spacing`

- **Claim:** the opt-in `resonance-local` resolution uses the case's production line population to refine a 3 eV backbone around resonances whose first-zero width is narrower than the backbone. Each line's halo extends by `w/(π² η)` on either side, where `η` is the local halo share; within that halo the spacing is no larger than its first-zero width, subject to the global sinc spacing floor. Every line narrower than twice the backbone also gets a core of `±10 w` at spacing no larger than `max(w/2, floor)`, so no piece coarser than that lies within `±10 w` of the resonance; joins between finer levels may remain. The policy requires `bin-mean` quadrature, under which integrated yield is independent of spacing joins. Binning halos and cores at 100 eV and merging equal-resolution runs gives a bounded piecewise coordinate count without changing the bandwidth edge
- **Code:** `montecarlo/spectrum/cxr_seeds.py::local_spacing_seeds`; `montecarlo/runner/cxr_grid.py::_measured_cxr_grid`; `montecarlo/runner/cxr_grid.py::_local_spacing_grid`; `_cxr_grid_policy.py::resolve_cxr_grid_policy`; `_cxr_grid_policy.py::check_selector_combination`
- **Source:** `finite-time-lineshape` for the first-zero width and the same `sinc²` tail bound as `line-grid-resonance-bandwidth`; the 100 eV bin width and power-of-two resolution levels are numerical policy
- **Status:** rederived
- **Checks:** Required: verify halo coverage and spacing after binning and window-plan assembly; compare integrated yield, line shape, detected counts, precision, point count, runtime, and peak memory against a full-ceiling uniform reference on identical production trajectories; verify the global spacing floor does not mask significant narrow lines
- **Anchor:** focused regression pending; remote measurement via `python -m pyrite.energy_grid.convergence_job start-bandwidth`
- **Notes:** The halo argument bounds discarded line mass; under the required `bin-mean` quadrature integrated yield does not depend on the axis at all. The policy is opt-in; the `high_energy` profile selects it (since `508b7a74`, `bin-mean` since `8c6433f0`) with `bin-mean` quadrature, which keeps integrated yield exact across spacing joins; `resonance-local` with `node` quadrature is refused, since the core reduces but bounds no join term (re-check: up to ~5e-3 for cored lines, ~1e-2 for lines wider than twice the backbone). No line-shape bound is derived; the identical-trajectory comparison (SLURM 239, four 5 MeV h-BN cases) measured yield <=3.2e-5, detected counts <=1.7e-4, Timepix3 detected-shape L1 <=1.7e-3 and FP32 yield <=3.3e-6, while the intrinsic FWHM of ~3 eV lines widens by up to 16% under bin-mean. The core parameters are not in the policy payload, so case identity does not record the core rule; the run record's `local_spacing` summary does. Human sign-off pending. [derivation](beam-transport/line-grid-resonance-local-spacing.md) **2026-11-17 (#1265):** selecting `resonance-local` with an unset bandwidth implies `resonance-population` and records it in `sources`, so a `resonance-local` payload never falls back; it still requires `bin-mean`, and windows refuse it. The `sinc-shape` over-budget reroute to this policy is removed. Spacing rules unchanged; awaits fresh-context verification. **2026-11-18 (#1265), fresh-context verification: `rederived`.** An unset bandwidth beside `resonance-local` resolves to `resonance-population` with `sources["bandwidth"] = "implied by resonance-local"` and never falls back; `node` quadrature, explicit `kinematic-ceiling` and windows still refuse; `halo_limit` and the `_local_spacing_grid` construction are unchanged (only the removed `sinc-shape` overflow no longer enters it).

## `line-grid-sinc-shape-spacing`

- **Claim:** under `bin-mean` quadrature the `sinc-oversampled` resolution (the default, on any bandwidth; the former `sinc-shape` selector is its deprecated alias, #1265) puts the line axis on uniform bins of width `h <= (3/π) sqrt(ε_shape) W_q`, with `W_q` the case's sinc-Nyquist feature width (capped at `max_spacing_eV`) and `ε_shape = 1e-2`. An isolated line of first-zero width `W >= W_q` has highest-bin peak-height deficit bounded by `(π²/9)(h/W)² <= ε_shape`. Interpolated FWHM meets the tolerance in sampled isolated-line tests; no overlap guarantee is claimed. Bin means times widths preserve mass over identical outer edges. An axis above its point budget (1,000,000 by default under `bin-mean`) takes the `line-grid-node-local-refinement` axis at the same window step; there is no `resonance-local` reroute
- **Code:** `_cxr_grid_policy.py::shape_spacing_factor`; `_cxr_grid_policy.py::uniform_step_divisor` (reads the payload's `shape_rtol`); `_cxr_grid_policy.py::default_resolution_policy`
- **Source:** `finite-time-lineshape` (peak curvature `-(2π²/3)/W²` of `sinc²(u/W)`); bin-mean exactness from `sinc-bin-integration`; `ε_shape` is the #1143 shape margin and the coordinate budget is numerical policy from #944 Fourier timings
- **Status:** anchored
- **Checks:** Triangular Fourier representation and `1-cos(z) <= z²/2` prove the height bound; nearest-bin `|δ| <= h/2`, units, first-zero convention, proxy quantile and limiting case independently rederived. FWHM sampled at 2,001 offsets at the default spacing: 0.792912%; seven owning tests pass. Production peak height, FWHM and local-shape qualification on fixed tracks remains outstanding (#1230)
- **Anchor:** `tests/energy-grid/test_line_grid_shape_spacing.py`; `tests/energy-grid/test_line_grid_selector_table.py`
- **Notes:** Fresh-context read-only verification (2026-11-16, implementation `c9da59b95`) returned `rederived` after correction of the FWHM table and overbroad overlap claims. Anchored by the isolated-line regression and same-band fallback tests. The unresolved tail bounds `t_L²` proxy weight, not photon yield or peak error. Fallback has no shape guarantee. Issue #1230. Human sign-off pending. [derivation](beam-transport/line-grid-sinc-shape-spacing.md) **2026-11-17 (#1265), implementation context:** the step moves from the opt-in `sinc-shape` selector to `sinc-oversampled` under `bin-mean`, on every bandwidth (kinematic, fallback and channeling/TR/mixed axes included, which took `W/8`, bound only `(π²/9)/64 = 1.7e-2`). The `W_q` cap is now applied before the factor, as for `node` (`min(W_q, max_spacing)·(3/π)√ε`), which only refines the step. The over-budget reroute to `resonance-local` is removed: such an axis takes the #1257 node-local refinement at the shape step (user decision 2026-11-18). The isolated-line derivation is unchanged. **2026-11-18 (#1265), fresh-context verification (pre-rebase, refusal variant): `rederived`.** `shape_spacing_factor(1e-2) = 0.0954930`, matching `(3/π)·0.1`; `scipy.quad` worst deficit 9.928e-3 against the 1e-2 bound (W/8: 1.6925e-2 against 1.7135e-2); measured and kinematic axes take the same `h/min(W_q, 3) = 0.09549`. **Post-rebase re-check (2026-11-18): `rederived`.** Over budget, measured and kinematic bin-mean axes take `node_local_axis` with window step `s·min(W_q, h_max)`, the uniform step; lines left on the backbone have `h_b <= s w`, so the isolated-line peak bound also holds on the local axis.

## `line-grid-node-local-refinement`

- **Claim:** when the uniform `sinc-oversampled` axis (step `h_f = min(W_q, max_spacing) / K`, `K = 8` under `node`, `K = 1/shape_spacing_factor(1e-2) ≈ 10.47` under `bin-mean` since #1265) exceeds the point budget, the resolver takes a piecewise node axis instead of refusing: `h_f` inside merged windows around every line narrower than `8 h_b`, the backbone `h_b = max_spacing` (3 eV) elsewhere (`8 h_b` reads `K h_b`), accepted only within the same budget, and unchanged wherever the uniform axis fits. Each window extends `node_join_halo_eV` beyond its line, and a wider line's halo is refined whole when it meets a window, so every spacing join lies outside every line's halo. Under node quadrature the joins on each side of a line of width `w >= h_f` then shift its mass by at most `eta = 1e-4` (`2 eta` per line), beyond the out-of-band tail a uniform axis also omits; under `bin-mean` the bin means telescope over contiguous edges (`sinc-bin-integration`; to within the `sinc-bin-far-envelope` term, ≤ ~2.4e-5 per line), so the same halos are conservative. **Graded backbone (#1271):** where it has fewer nodes than the ungraded axis, and never on channeling, transition-radiation or mixed-transport cases, steps beyond every protected run (fine windows, each line's grading-free `±D`, DBR/Kossel spans) grow from `h_b` as `min(H, h_b + rho s)` (`rho = 0.1`, `H = 10 eV`). `node_graded_halos_eV` gives each line `d = D` from the tail bound `(w/π²)[(1 + h_b/(2d))/d + rho/(2D)]`, or a Peano split `(1 - theta) eta` on `d` and `theta eta` on `D` against `π[(h_b² + h_c²) G(πd/w) + (H² - h_b²) G(πD/w)]/(8w²)` (`theta = 0.5`), so the same `eta` per side holds. Mass only; no shape bound
- **Code:** `montecarlo/runner/node_local_grid.py::{node_join_halo_eV,node_graded_halos_eV,node_local_seeds,node_local_axis,graded_coordinates,_graded_gap,_backbone_parts,_components}`; `montecarlo/runner/cxr_grid.py::{_kinematic_cxr_grid,_measured_cxr_grid,_cached_grid}`; `montecarlo/runner/diffracted_brem.py::bragg_support_windows_eV`
- **Source:** `finite-time-lineshape` (`sinc²` first-zero width and envelope); Poisson summation for the band-limited central lattice; trapezoid Peano kernel `(x-p)(p+Δ-x)/2 <= Δ²/8`; `int_0^inf |g''| = 1.4743` for `g = sin²u/u²` (numerical quadrature); `eta` and the feature-window charge are numerical policy
- **Status:** anchored
- **Checks:** Author derivation of the per-side tail bound `(w/(π² d))(1 + h_b/(2d))` and Peano bound `π (h_b² + h_c²) G(π d / w) / (8 w²)`, the closed-form distances that meet `eta`, the limiting cases (uniform axis; narrow-line `resonance-local` halo; wide lines need no halo above `sqrt(1.5 π (h_b² + h_c²) / (8 eta))`), and units. Single-line sweep `w = 0.4-2000 eV` on its own axis: worst sampled `6.8e-5` against `2e-4`; attainable worst ≈ `0.47 eta` per side (≈ `9.4e-5` per line) when `h_b` is a multiple of `w`; a 100x looser limit reaches `1.0e-3`. Fresh-context read-only verification (2026-11-17, `ca9a7e7d0`) returned `rederived`: all distances and branches of `node_join_halo_eV` match, merged/overlapping and adversarial multi-line sweeps stay below `0.5 x 2 eta`. Assumes an absorption-free `sinc²`; absorption is argued, not derived. Shape (peak/FWHM/mass L1) is qualified separately under #1257, outside this mass claim
- **Anchor:** `tests/energy-grid/test_line_grid_node_local.py`
- **Notes:** Over-budget fallback only (#1257 decision 1); the grid record's `local_axis` gives the route, uniform count and reason. Join limit charged, like the `resonance-local` halo, to the feature-window row of `tbl-line-budget-allocation` (`2e-4` of `5e-4` with the `1e-4` bandwidth share). Lines narrower than `h_f` alias on both axes, inside the aliased-weight budget. Issue #1257. Human sign-off pending. [derivation](beam-transport/line-grid-node-local-refinement.md) **2026-11-18 (#1265), implementation context:** the route now also serves bin-mean `sinc-oversampled` axes at their shape step: `node_local_axis` and `uniform_over_budget` read `_cxr_grid_policy.py::uniform_step_divisor` (8 node, ≈10.47 bin-mean) instead of the integer `oversampling`, and `narrow_below = K h_b` takes the float divisor. Node-quadrature axes are unchanged; `NODE_LOCAL_REVISION = 3` (speed cache only). This clause awaits fresh-context verification. **2026-11-18 (#1265), fresh-context verification of the bin-mean clause: `rederived`.** Node axes are bit-identical to #1257 (400 random lines, three `W_q`, two budgets; only `local_axis.revision` differs). Bin-mean: `K = 10π/3`, `narrow_below = 31.4 eV`, peak steps within `h_f` or `w/K`; exact bin masses over the local axis equal the outer-edge mass to 6.7e-16. Production bin-mean scoring uses the averaged envelope beyond 64 first-zero widths (`sinc-bin-far-envelope`), so its grid dependence is at most ~2.4e-5 per line, under `2 eta`. `NODE_LOCAL_REVISION` keys the speed cache only. Status stays `anchored`. **2026-11-18 (#1271), implementation context:** graded-backbone clause and its derivation ([section](beam-transport/line-grid-node-local-refinement.md#graded-backbone-1271)) added; `NODE_LOCAL_REVISION = 4`. Graded single-line sweep `w = 0.4-2000 eV`: worst `7.8e-5` against `2e-4`; dropping `D` reaches `6.2e-3` at `w = 40 eV`. **2026-11-18 (#1271), fresh-context read-only verification of the graded clause at `b626e148e`: `rederived`.** It reproduced (tail-g), (Peano-g), the θ split and the no-cap counterargument. 150 random and 60 adversarial populations (band-edge lines, zero-halo lines wider than 1.04 keV, up to 60 Bragg spans, D spans crossing fine windows) gave 0 step-profile violations and at most 2.4e-5 per line against the uniform h_f axis. Graded anchors: `test_graded_halos_bound_single_line_node_mass`, `test_graded_backbone_needs_the_grading_free_distance`. Remote qualification: jobs 13603/13617/13618. Status stays `anchored`.

## `coherent-line-grid-fringe-spacing`

- **Claim:** on the coherent route the line-grid Nyquist step is `h_coh = pi hbar c / D`, where `D` is the span of the per-segment retardation scalar `d_j = t_mid,j - n_hat . r_j` (in the reducer's geometry), not the per-segment sinc width; consequently automatic `sinc-nyquist` resolution does not bound the coherent route and is refused there when the policy carries no feature windows (#117); a windowed policy resolves it under `coherent-line-grid-windowed-resolution` (#350)
- **Code:** `montecarlo/spectrum/diagnostics.py::coherent_fringe_spacing` (estimator), `montecarlo/spectrum/diagnostics.py::coherent_retardation` (reducer geometry per route), `montecarlo/runner/cxr_grid.py::_refuse_coherent_resolution` (policy), `energy_grid/convergence_case.py` (route-switchable ladder)
- **Source:** no new physical law — the coherent reducer's own phase `phi_j(E) = d_j omega(E) - g.r_j - L_esc,j delta_omega(E)` (`lines/_setup.py`, `lines/_per_hkl.py::_accumulate_reflection_coherent`); this row claims only the band limit that phase implies and the refusal taken from it
- **Status:** rederived
- **Checks:** Required: that the cross-term oscillation rate is the difference of per-segment phase slopes (it is, since `|A|^2` pairs `exp[i(phi_j - phi_k)]`); that the `-g.r_j` term is energy-independent and drops out; that neglecting the in-medium `L_esc delta_omega` slope is safe (`1 - n_re ~ 1e-5` for x-rays); that the per-segment increment identity `Delta d = (1 - beta v.n) t_L` holds exactly, making this estimator and `sinc_feature_spacing` differ in principle rather than calibration; that the single-segment limit returns `inf` (no inter-segment fringe; the per-segment envelope step `pi hbar c / Delta d` then governs), not the incoherent width; that `coherent_retardation` follows `cxr/_setup.py::_coherent_offsets` on every route (no offsets: sampled; offsets with `physical_electrons` and no analytic average: realized `t0` and sampled positions; otherwise offset-free). Numeric: `coherent_fringe_spacing` reproduces the measured spans (hopg 30 keV, 2.5298e7 Ang all-electron, 4.1553e5 Ang grouped)
- **Anchor:** `tests/energy-grid/test_coherent_line_grid.py::test_coherent_step_follows_the_total_span_not_the_per_segment_increment`
- **Notes:** Closes the gap the `line-grid-kinematic-bandwidth` row and the #97 handoff both flag, that the coherent route's aliasing was never analyzed. Measured on identical trajectories (hopg 30 keV, tilt 5 deg, Ne=40, `bunch_length_fs` 100, band 10-3700 eV, sinc estimate 1.504 eV): at h = 1.0 eV the incoherent yield is converged to 3.08e-8 while the coherent yield is still 5.01e-2 from convergence, and 4.72e-3 at h = 0.5 eV, against the 1e-3 intrinsic-source tolerance; the coherent centroid drift does not shrink monotonically, the signature of newly resolved fringes rather than converging quadrature. Band-limit ratios against the incoherent estimate span 2055-31640x all-electron and 65-230x per-electron over hopg 30/100 keV and wse2 30 keV. Resolving the all-electron fringes costs ~1.5e7 points over a keV band against 2453 at the incoherent estimate, so the policy refuses rather than silently aliasing or silently refining; explicit `E_grid_line`, stored catalog rows, and the incoherent route are unaffected. `decoherence_active` is true for standard `beam_fwhm_mm = 1.0` cases, so the grouped floor takes the per-electron span and the `|sum_e S_e|^2` term the all-electron span; the latter's fringes are one realization of the sampled offsets (speckle, which `lines/_setup.py` notes does not shrink with electron count) and whether they are an observable at all is a separate modelling question, not settled here (issue #155). Ladder is the CPU-affordable subset; 100/300 keV and tilt 85 deg belong on `pyrite remote`. **2026-10-05 (#350):** the refusal is scoped to policies without feature windows; a windowed policy now seeds per-row coherent windows (`coherent-line-grid-windowed-resolution`). The estimator now reads `d` through `diagnostics.coherent_retardation`, the reducer's own geometry: on an infinite slab with active decoherence each electron's transverse launch offset is removed, as the reducer (`cxr/_setup.py`) does without `physical_electrons`, so the all-electron span there no longer counts the beam footprint; with `physical_electrons`, which the case driver always passes on the coherent route, the reducer pairs realized fields and the production span includes the projected footprint and the `t0` spread (#1098). The former note that finite-footprint cases, including every measurement above, were unchanged is stale since `917ec678a` (#370), which made the reducer's finite-footprint branch offset-free; the measurements above predate it and their all-electron spans include the projected footprint (historical, not re-measured). Human sign-off pending. [validation write-up](beam-transport/coherent-line-grid-fringe-spacing.md) Fresh-context review (2026-11-11, #4 batch 3, verdict `discrepancy`; [independent verification](beam-transport/coherent-line-grid-fringe-spacing.md#independent-verification)): the band limit $\pi\hbar c/D$, the slope-difference rate, the energy independence of $-\mathbf g\cdot\mathbf r_j$, negligibility of the in-medium $L_{\rm esc}\delta\omega$ slope, the exact increment identity $\Delta d=(1-\beta\hat{\mathbf v}\cdot\hat{\mathbf n})t_L$ and the refusal logic are rederived (synthetic 40-segment closed-form spectrum: trapezoid yield error 1.8e-9 at $h$, 2.4e-3 at $2h$; estimator step/span agree exactly). First divergent term, the "reducer's geometry" clause: `diagnostics.py::coherent_retardation` restores sampled transverse offsets only `if not finite_footprint`, but since `917ec678a` (#370) `cxr/_setup.py::_coherent_offsets` phases finite-footprint cases offset-free too, so the estimator over-states $D$ there (conservative); on the `physical_electrons` route without analytic offsets the estimator removes offsets and $t_0$ that the reducer keeps (under-states $D$, unsafe). The refusal fires unconditionally for unwindowed coherent cases, so only the reported step/span in its message are affected; no other production consumer. The finite-footprint "unchanged" note above predates `917ec678a`. The single-segment check returns $\infty$ (the envelope step $\pi\hbar c/\Delta d$ then governs), not the incoherent width. Remediation applied (#1098): `coherent_retardation` follows `_coherent_offsets` per route and `_refuse_coherent_resolution` passes the case's `physical_bunch_electrons`; regression tests compare against the reducer's own `d_all_geom` ([remediation](beam-transport/coherent-line-grid-fringe-spacing.md#remediation-1098)). Fresh-context re-verification (2026-11-12, #1098, verdict `rederived`; [independent re-verification](beam-transport/coherent-line-grid-fringe-spacing.md#independent-re-verification-2026-11-12-1098)): the reducer's `d_all_geom` was rebuilt per route from `_coherent_offsets`, `_offset_free_geometry` and the `physical_electrons` override before reading the estimator; an independent FP64 script over 15 route configurations (no offsets, infinite slab with `t0`/transverse/both offsets and `physical_electrons` unset/set/zero, finite footprint, bunch-front gradient, absent `t_ang`, `electron_limit` below the population) agrees row by row to <=1.4e-15 relative. Residuals, non-blocking: the estimator spans unexpanded transport rows, so the reducer's escape-piece span exceeds it by at most `max Delta d` (inside the midpoint convention) and cutoff clipping or per-layer reduction only make it conservative; a hand-built record with per-row `t0_ang` but no `initial_*` population fields is outside the docstring's assumption and never emitted by transport.

## `coherent-line-grid-windowed-resolution`

- **Claim:** a windowed automatic line-grid policy resolves `coherent_emission` inside per-`(reflection, orientation)`-row coherent windows. Envelope: the row's resonance band widened to a bisected edge where the jump-spectrum tail bound, times `1 + F (N_e - 1)`, is at most `1e-4` per side. The bound uses the reducer's own per-piece coefficients (`a_{j,p} = c_{j,p}/dd_j`, `c = sqrt(alpha omega/(4 pi^2 hbar c)) t_L (A_PXR + A_CBS)` per polarization, damped by `exp(-tau_abs/2)` at the piece ends; joints of one electron enter as amplitude differences because the phase is continuous along the track), takes a Minkowski upper bound `T_J` on each jump term's two-carrier tail, bounds jumps closer than `M hbar c / u_min` (`M = 64`) together by the triangle inequality `(sum sqrt T_J)^2`, and adds separated clusters' cross terms, at most `4 (1 + ln n_C) u_min / M * sum_C (sum_J B_J)^2` with `B_J` the jump's decreasing-piece modulus at the edge (`|a1 - a2|/|z1| + |a2| |z2 - z1|/(|z1| |z2|)` with `z = (E - E_j) + i hbar c lambda_j` and `|z2 - z1| = sqrt(dE^2 + (hbar c d lambda)^2)`, root-sum-square over polarizations), over the Parseval power `2 pi sum |a|^2 dd <exp(-tau_abs)>`; step: `pi hbar c / D` over the time support of every radiating piece of the row, per-electron span where `F_max N_e <= 1e-4` over a 100 eV bin and the all-electron span otherwise (`F` bounded only by the analytic finite-footprint `F_z`; `F = 1` without offsets or on an infinite slab), sampled at eight nodes per Nyquist step; plans above the `max_points` budget are refused with their count, never coarsened; scoped to float64 retardation phases (a float32 reducer warns) and `sinc_cutoff = None` (refused). **Dispersion certificate (#372, fresh-context `rederived` 2026-10-10):** with the material law, each window edge also meets a dispersive bound: at fixed `E` the reducer's phase `-delta_omega(E) L(d)` is affine along each piece, so the production field is exactly its endpoint terms on carriers `E_j + hbar c g_j delta_omega(E)` (`g = dL/dd`) with endpoint phase `E tau/hbar c - delta_omega L`; the certified smooth-interval enclosure of `delta_omega` bounds the diagonal on blocks, and cross terms keep the frozen allowance times `1 + hbar c r_e TV(delta_omega')/(2(1 - hbar c M1 r_e))` (`r_e` the largest consecutive `|dL|/dtau` of an electron's jumps, `M1` the slope bound, knot jumps bounded explicitly; one cluster per electron when `hbar c M1 r_e >= 1`), with near/far splits of each omitted band; the frozen edge is widened only when this bound exceeds `1e-4` per side, a carrier that can shift into an omitted band is never certified, and the bound is relative to the frozen Parseval reference with no sampling certificate. Dispersive sampling inside the windows rests on same-trajectory convergence against uniform full-axis references (HOPG 30/60 keV, 1 mm, physical charge) plus the opt-in runtime full-axis yield/centroid audit
- **Code:** `montecarlo/spectrum/coherent_windows.py::coherent_window_seeds`, `::decoherence_bound`, `::coherent_case_seeds`, `::coherent_edge_leak`, `::dispersive_edge_leak`, `::CoherentRowCollector`, `::CoherentRowField`, `::_dispersion_window_audit`; `montecarlo/spectrum/coherent_dispersion.py`; `montecarlo/spectrum/coherent_normalization.py`; `montecarlo/spectrum/coherent_sample_certificates.py`; `montecarlo/spectrum/coherent_band_audit.py`; `montecarlo/spectrum/coherent_form_factor.py::gaussian_form_factor_bounds`; `montecarlo/spectrum/coherent_spectrum_audit.py`; `montecarlo/spectrum/cxr/_spectrum.py::mc_cxr_spectrum` (`coefficient_capture`, read-only); `montecarlo/runner/cxr_grid.py::_coherent_rows`; `montecarlo/spectrum/diagnostics.py::coherent_retardation`; `montecarlo/runner/cxr_grid.py::_windowed_cxr_grid`, `::_check_coherent_budget`, `::_refuse_coherent_resolution`; `_cxr_grid_policy.py` (`DEFAULT_COHERENT_LEAK_LIMIT`, `DEFAULT_COHERENT_DECOHERENCE_LIMIT`, `COHERENT_NYQUIST_OVERSAMPLING`, `COHERENT_WINDOW_BIN_EV`, `COHERENT_JUMP_CLUSTER_SEPARATION`)
- **Source:** no new physical law — the coherent reducer's phase and `(1-F) sum_e |S_e|^2 + F |sum_e S_e|^2` blend (`coherent-line-grid-fringe-spacing`, `coherent-inter-electron-decoherence`); Parseval, Poisson summation, integration by parts, and Cauchy-Schwarz give the window rules
- **Status:** anchored
- **Checks:** Required: the time-domain representation of a row (carrier at `omega_j`, amplitude `c_j/(1 - beta v.n)`, support `dd_j`) and its Parseval normalization; phase continuity at joints of one electron (so joints enter as differences); the signed-attenuation complex endpoint denominators, positive Minkowski tail bound and one-carrier limit; that the captured coefficients are the reducer's own, and that the cluster bound (triangle inequality inside clusters; cross terms between them by integration by parts with decreasing real majorants for the complex carrier products and their derivative norms, `2 hbar c B_J B_K / dtau`) recovers `sincsq_upper_tail_bound` for one piece and covers clustered joints; that the Cauchy-Schwarz span argument forces every radiating piece of a row into `D`; the `F N_e` bound on the inter-electron term and `F_z` monotonicity; the factor-eight Nyquist oversampling as numerical policy. Limits: single segment gives the incoherent `sinc^2` tail bound and Nyquist step `w/2`; `F -> 0` gives the per-electron step; an exact collinear split leaks what the whole flight leaks at every edge, including separated endpoints and many-piece subdivisions: identically zero captured jumps (equal endpoint amplitudes and complex denominators, or both amplitudes zero) are excluded before clustering, without a tolerance
- **Anchor:** `tests/energy-grid/test_coherent_windowed_line_grid.py::test_windowed_auto_matches_a_fine_explicit_grid_on_identical_trajectories` (yield, centroid and FWHM within 1e-3, of a uniform axis at a quarter of the window step, one transport); production-reducer tail `::test_the_production_reducer_leaks_less_than_the_window_bound`; clustered joints `::test_clustered_joints_stay_inside_the_bound`, `::test_a_scattered_track_with_moving_carriers_stays_inside_the_bound`, `::test_near_cancelling_joints_far_apart_stay_inside_the_bound`; scope `::test_a_windowed_policy_refuses_a_sinc_cutoff`, `::test_a_float32_reducer_warns_that_the_bound_needs_float64_phases`; genuine gaps `::test_real_gaps_do_not_cancel_under_retardation_translation`; attenuation `::test_continuous_attenuation_cusp_stays_inside_the_tail_bound`, `::test_near_cancelling_joint_keeps_a_positive_tail_bound`, `::test_capture_preserves_extreme_attenuation_endpoints_and_slopes`; limiting cases `::test_single_segment_leak_is_the_incoherent_sinc_tail_bound`, `::test_a_collinear_split_leaks_exactly_what_the_whole_flight_leaks`, `::test_vanishing_decoherence_takes_the_per_electron_step_everywhere`; policy `::test_uniform_automatic_policy_still_refuses_coherent_emission`, `::test_a_plan_above_the_budget_is_refused_with_its_count`, `::test_coherent_and_incoherent_grids_never_share_a_cache_entry`; dispersive certificate (#372) `::test_production_field_beyond_the_certified_windows_stays_inside_the_dispersive_bound` (slow; production field beyond the certified windows of the CPU anchor, nonzero on every omitted side), `::test_dispersive_leak_bounds_the_production_field_beyond_the_edge`, `::test_dispersive_leak_with_no_material_phase_is_the_frozen_bound`, `::test_a_shifted_carrier_is_never_certified_and_the_window_widens_over_it`, `::test_the_material_slope_jump_bound_covers_every_knot`

## `line-grid-sinc-convergence`

- **Claim:** reproducible convergence-instrument contract for the incoherent CXR line spectrum: every rung uses bit-identical transport segments; `h <= pi/a_width` is a sufficient exact-integration bound rather than an empirical acceptance ceiling; the Richardson gate requires a passing suffix of triples; and float32/FP64 comparisons share one serialized transport
- **Code:** `energy_grid/convergence.py` (grid-agnostic harness: nested ladders, segment fingerprints, observables, monotone Richardson gate), `energy_grid/convergence_case.py` (one runner transport; production line/characteristic/brem reductions), `energy_grid/precision_ladder.py`
- **Source:** no new physical law — the `sinc^2` finite-time lineshape and `a_width` of `finite-time-lineshape`; band-limited trapezoid exactness for `h <= pi/a_width`; tolerances are `_cxr_grid_policy.DEFAULT_RTOL`
- **Status:** anchored
- **Checks:** Confirmed: every rung comparison is on bit-identical segments (fingerprint, and the case rung equal to the runner spectrum phase bit-for-bit); the photon grid does not enter the transport RNG stream; the `sinc^2` transform gives exact infinite-grid trapezoid area for `h <= pi/a_width`; an analytic aggregate can be accepted above that sufficient bound when phase-spread aliases cancel and its yield still matches the independent integral; the grossly aliased coarse triple fails; the Richardson gate matches its equation including the yield floor, `0.1 tol`, and all-finer-triples suffix; float32 and FP64 evaluations share one serialized transport and reject fingerprint mismatches
- **Anchor:** `tests/energy-grid/test_convergence.py`, `tests/energy-grid/test_convergence_case.py`, `tests/energy-grid/test_precision_ladder.py`; remote ladders via `python -m pyrite.energy_grid.convergence_job start|start-precision`
- **Notes:** Gate: accept `h` when (`d1 < tol` or `d1 == 0`) and (`d2 <= d1` or `max(d1, d2) <= 0.1 tol`), with the near-zero yield floor; 0.1 is a harness policy margin, not a derived physical constant. Yield and centroid use the `DEFAULT_RTOL` intrinsic 1e-3, detected counts use its 1e-2, and dominant-line FWHM and line/background use the harness-only `SHAPE_RTOL` at 1e-2. The retired remote ladder/precision artifacts cannot support quantitative validation claims; their historical summaries remain provenance-only in the write-up. Human sign-off pending. [validation write-up](beam-transport/line-grid-sinc-convergence.md) **2026-11-16 (#1214), implementation context:** the automatic resolution default is now `sinc-oversampled`, `h = min(w_q, DEFAULT_MAX_SPACING_EV) / LINE_NYQUIST_OVERSAMPLING` with K = 8 (`_cxr_grid_policy.py::resolved_coordinates`); `sinc-nyquist` (K = 1) stays selectable and is the opt-out an over-budget refusal names. K is grid policy measured on the #1143 scenes, not derived: `h <= pi/a_w` keeps line area exact but the node peak and interpolated FWHM converge with the step (HOPG 1 MeV at K = 1: peak −23…−31 %, FWHM +63…+78 %); K = 8 is the smallest value inside the 1e-2 shape margins on every scene and seed, while K = 6 and 7 each miss once at 1 MeV ([shape oversampling](beam-transport/line-grid-sinc-convergence.md#shape-oversampling-issue-1214)). `diagnostics.py::sinc_feature_samples` now takes one sample per `(owner, flight_id)` group, width `2 pi hbar c / sum_i kappa_i t_i` and weight `(sum_i t_i)^2`, matching the flight-grouped reducer's summed field; one-row flights reproduce the per-row samples exactly (`tests/energy-grid/test_sinc_spacing.py`). Under `max_dE_frac > 0` per-substep widths had saturated the quantile at the 3 eV maximum. The grouped width is the first zero of a constant-rate phase sum and ignores the resonance drift between substeps (bounded by `radiation-error-estimators`). **2026-11-16 (#1214), fresh-context verification: `rederived`** (grouped width, oversampled plumbing, K-sweep numbers); see [verification](beam-transport/line-grid-sinc-convergence.md#fresh-context-verification-of-the-1214-change-2026-11-16). Human sign-off pending (#277). **2026-11-17 (#1265):** the production `sinc-oversampled` default keeps `W/8` under `node` (this harness's K-sweep); under `bin-mean` it now takes the derived `line-grid-sinc-shape-spacing` step `(3/π)√1e-2·W ≈ W/10.5` on every bandwidth, not `W/8`. The harness contract is unchanged. **2026-11-18 (#1265), fresh-context verification: `rederived`.** Node keeps `W/8`; the bin-mean `sinc-shape` arm of jobs 13528–13539 used today's bin-mean default step wherever `W_q <= max_spacing_eV` (finer above), so that evidence covers the new default on measured-axis scenes. Kinematic routes (channeling, TR, mixed, fallback) have the isolated-line bound by construction but no direct scene qualification at the new step.

## `line-window-seeding`

- **Claim:** piecewise line-axis windows are seeded deterministically, without a pilot spectrum, from (1) each reflection's `t_L²`-weighted population of the in-medium resonance `E_res = ħc (v·g)/(1 − Re n(E_res) v·n̂)`, solved by the kernels' own `_in_medium_kinematics` on their `Re n` table, over the case's own segments, with `g` built as the line kernels build their rows (orientation and mosaic quadrature), covering the `[ε/2, 1 − ε/2]` weighted quantiles plus two sinc feature widths at `pi/a_width / 8` spacing; (2) the absorption jumps of both tables the line route reads: the Chantler `f2` couplings, anchored on both nodes of the steepest bracket within 5% of each xraydb edge, and the EPDL2025 photoionization edges of the escape `μ` (issue #274), anchored as a pair straddling each exact discontinuity at the same shell's Chantler native spacing; (3) every characteristic line the emission model deposits, ±10 natural FWHM at FWHM/8
- **Code:** `montecarlo/spectrum/cxr_seeds.py::kinematic_cxr_seeds`, `::absorption_edge_seeds`, `::characteristic_line_seeds`; planner `_cxr_windows.py::build_window_plan`; interval-wise precision floor `_grid_semantics.py::validate_backend_coordinates`
- **Source:** no new physical law — the resonance of `line-energy-dispersion` with the Snell dispersion of `xray-in-medium-resonance` (`lines/_per_hkl.py` step 1), the `a_width` of `finite-time-lineshape`, `mu` from `narrow-beam-total-attenuation` (EPDL) and `f2` from `absorption-length`, and the xraydb lines/widths of `characteristic-radiation`. Spacing heuristics (8 samples per feature, two tail widths, ±10 FWHM, ±3 native edge nodes) are grid policy, not physics
- **Status:** anchored
- **Checks:** Required: that the seed `g` rows and velocity convention match the kernels exactly (orientation, mosaic rotations, `beta v_hat`, the 10 eV floor); that production seeding uses the same in-medium root and feature-width denominator as the kernels; that the dropped-reflection rule bounds the backbone share of the `t_L²` proxy weight by about `2ε`; that each modelled Chantler jump gets its own native-node bracket even near a stronger shell; and that the characteristic cutoff mirrors `mc_characteristic_spectrum` per composition. Acceptance additionally requires the #101 window ladder: yield, centroid and FWHM converge under window refinement on identical segments against a dense uniform reference. **2026-09-17 fresh-context result:** kernel `g`-row/velocity/orientation/mosaic/10 eV conventions match exactly; the in-medium root and feature width match the kernel; `2ε` drop bound holds (`U ≤ (2ε − εd)W`, relative to all radiating weight including out-of-band); Chantler-bracket locations and secondary-shell selection pass, including Se L2 and independent W M4/L1 and Mo/Ga/As/Ti L2 probes; characteristic line sets match over 26 elements; both limiting cases and the targeted regression anchors pass. The earlier vacuum-root and secondary-edge discrepancies are closed.
- **Anchor:** `tests/energy-grid/test_line_seeds.py`, `tests/energy-grid/test_line_windows.py`
- **Notes:** Limiting cases: a single straight flight gives a window centred on its closed-form resonance with half-extent `tail_widths` feature widths; no seeds reproduces the uniform `linspace` bit-for-bit. `t_L²` ignores `|A|²`, absorption and the line-electron cutoff, so kinematic bands are conservative. Catalog sweep of 169 xraydb edges over 26 elements: every edge with jump ratio ≥ 2 has a steepest Chantler pair ratio ≥ 1.43; minor shells below 1.05 are skipped and reported. Wired into automatic resolution behind the opt-in `windows` policy block (`_cxr_grid_policy.windowed_coordinates`, `montecarlo/runner/cxr_grid.py::_windowed_cxr_grid`); off by default until convergence and point/time/memory measurements land. Human sign-off pending. Per-row `t_L` understates flight width under opt-in `max_dE_frac` substeps. Window-ladder acceptance cannot see seed placement (positions fixed across rungs); only the capped uniform reference can. **2026-09-17 remediation:** edge seeding visits shells strongest-first and selects the steepest qualifying bracket outside claimed native-node windows; Se L2, W M4/L1 and Mo/Ga/As/Ti L2 now receive their own anchors. The policy refuses `tail_widths = 0`. Production kinematic seeding passes the case band and composition, solves the kernels' in-medium root on the same table, rejects the same unsettled pairs, and derives widths from the same denominator as `a_width`; the vacuum root remains only as the no-band limiting case. `SEEDING_REVISION` keys the resolved-grid cache. **Fresh-context re-verification:** units, limits, signs, in-medium roots/widths, secondary-edge selection, characteristic lines, and source-to-code agreement pass. Independent pointwise-Brent and Se L2 regressions anchor both remediations. The inherited positive-`g` convention is the `line-energy-dispersion` convention reconciled in #338. Seeds depend on the resonance only. [validation write-up](beam-transport/line-window-seeding.md) **Issue #274:** EPDL edge seeds (`(EPDL)` labels, `SEEDING_REVISION = 4`) join the Chantler ones because the escape `μ` jumps at EPDL's edges, a few eV from Chantler's for light elements; anchored by `tests/energy-grid/test_line_seeds.py::test_epdl_edge_anchors_straddle_the_exact_discontinuity`. The fresh-context verification above predates them.

## `line-energy-dispersion`

- **Claim:** `ω = v·g / (1 − v·n̂)` tunable line energy, with the line resonant on `g(hkl)` coupling through the Friedel mate `χ_line = conj(χ(−hkl))`, `U_line = conj(U(−hkl)) = U(hkl)` (momentum transfer `−g`; module `exp(+iωt)`, `f′+if″` convention; kernel conjugate-field phase `exp{i[ωt−(k+g)·r]}`)
- **Code:** `montecarlo/geometry.py::tilted_geometry` / `src/pyrite/validation/_anchor_conditions.py::line_energy_eV` / `materials/emission.py::photon_energies` / `materials/crystal.py::emission_coupling_tables` / `montecarlo/spectrum/cxr/_kernels.py::_reflection_tables`
- **Source:** Zhai 2025 Eq.(10), published main-text Eq.(1); Feranchuk–Spence 2000; crystallographic `F(q)=Σf exp(+iq·R)` with `f″>0` (exp(+iωt) convention, Henke/Chantler)
- **Status:** anchored
- **Checks:** units and positive Doppler denominator agree; zero-velocity, orthogonal-harmonic, reciprocal-vector reversal, and opposite-tilt limits checked; numerator sign is the `g_Z = −g_repo` relabelling; fresh-context re-derivation (2026-10-05) matches; Friedel regression discriminating (old pairing gives inverse PXR ratios, mixed `{conj χ(−hkl), U(−hkl)}` pairing fails the total-yield guard)
- **Anchor:** `tests/montecarlo/spectrum/test_line_friedel_pairing.py::test_line_couples_to_the_friedel_mate_of_its_resonant_harmonic`; `::test_total_yield_matches_the_unconjugated_native_pairing`; `tests/materials/test_crystallography.py::test_emission_coupling_tables_are_the_conjugate_friedel_mate`; `tests/materials/test_emission.py::{test_root_export_and_hopg_vacuum_anchor,test_oblique_reflection_full_orientation_and_friedel_pair,test_matches_existing_validation_anchor}`
- **Notes:** **2026-10-05 (#338):** the historical `−v·g` vs `+v·g` numerator was relabelling only (`coherent-emission`: `S(+g)` reconstructs as `χ_g exp(−ig·r)`). It hid a real pairing error. That derivation took `exp[i(k·r−ωt)]` while `χ_g` carries `f′+if″` in the module's `exp(+iωt)` convention. So the production line on `+g(hkl)` used `χ(+hkl)`, but the correct coupling is the transfer-`(−g)` coefficient `conj(χ(−hkl))`. The two differ only for noncentrosymmetric crystals with `f″≠0` (catalog: 4H/6H-SiC, PtBi₂, WTe₂; tens of per cent near edges). Fixed in `emission_coupling_tables`, which is bit-identical when `f″=0`. [validation write-up](radiation-physics/line-energy-dispersion.md). **2026-07-11**: tilt convention flipped to Zhai's positive θ; the default beam-aligned line energy is even in opposite polar tilt

## `longitudinal-target-timing`

- **Claim:** positive-basal-harmonic target timing: `kγ = β\|g\|cos(tilt)/(1−βcos(theta_obs))`, `Eγ=ℏc kγ`, `T=h/Eγ`, and Gaussian `σt=sqrt(−ln η)/Ω` with `Ω=2π/T`
- **Code:** `campaign/longitudinal.py::resolve_longitudinal_distribution`
- **Source:** Zhai 2025 Eq.(10); Gaussian characteristic function
- **Status:** anchored
- **Checks:** units distinguish `kγ` [Å⁻¹] from `Ω` [rad/fs]; `η→1`, `η→0+`, `β→0`, and `ΩT=2π` limits; independent HOPG/h-BN × 30/100 keV values at `theta_obs=90°`, `tilt=45°`

Uses the catalog-pinned positive `(002)` branch, the member with `v·g>0` that radiates. **2026-11-18 (#1302):** the default target is the catalog primary reflection of the case's surface cut (`structure-factor` `_primary_hkl`), falling back to the first positive basal reflection of the case list; for HOPG/h-BN this is the same `(002)`, and every formerly pinned crystal keeps its target. The line on `+g` takes momentum transfer `−g`, so `+v·g/(1−v·n̂)` is the line energy. The coupling pairing (`conj(χ(−hkl))`) does not enter timing, and HOPG is centrosymmetric. [validation write-up](beam-transport/longitudinal-target-timing.md)

## `closed-form-flux`

- **Claim:** Eq.(12) closed-form line flux (single-segment reference)
- **Code:** `src/pyrite/validation/_anchor_conditions.py::feranchuk_line_flux`
- **Source:** Feranchuk 2000 Eq.(12)
- **Status:** anchored
- **Checks:** prefactor, `α`/`ℏc` bookkeeping, solid-angle and energy-bin conventions, return units, and the `1/(1−β cos θ_obs)` Jacobian all reproduce the independent derivation symbolically and to twelve digits, and are corroborated by the Monte Carlo at sub-percent (anchor ratio `0.99722` at 17.5 keV, `0.99664` at 25 keV). Escape length now carries `|n_z|`: thin limit `→ t`, thick limit `→ |n_z| L_abs`, `|n_z|→1` recovers the bare form, `n_z→0` gives 0, monotone in thickness
- **Anchor:** `tests/notebooks/test_anchor_figures.py::test_escape_length_carries_the_exit_obliquity` (pins the thick-target saturation and all four limits), `::test_bulk_line_flux_is_escape_weighted` (pins the bulk/film ratio at the escape-weighted ceiling); `src/pyrite/validation/_anchor_conditions.py::single_segment_anchor` (ratio≈1, retained — but **structurally blind to this defect**: it passes `L_abs_ang=1e12` against `L_seg_ang=290`, so the geometry factor cancels to first order, which is why the new anchors are thick-regime)
- **Notes:** reference, not pipeline. **2026-08-15 DISCREPANCY:** the escape length omits a geometric `\|cos θ_obs\|`. Code (`validation/feranchuk_spence.py::photons_per_electron`) computes `L_eff = L_abs[1 − exp(−t/L_abs)]`; the escape integral `∫₀ᵗ exp(−μ(t−z)/\|n_z\|) dz` gives `\|n_z\| L_abs [1 − exp(−t/(\|n_z\| L_abs))]`. `n_z` *is* computed in the function but is used only in the Jacobian, never in `L_eff`. At the anchor's `θ_obs=119°` this reaches `1/0.4848 = 2.06×` in the thick-target limit, growing monotonically with `t/L_abs`; the two agree only for `t≪L_abs`, which is the sole regime the anchor exercises. **2026-09-13 FIXED.** `escape_limited_length_ang(L_z, L_abs, n_z)` in `validation/feranchuk_spence.py` now owns the Eq. (9) length and `photons_per_electron` calls it: `L_eff = |n_z| L_abs [1 − exp(−t/(|n_z| L_abs))]`. Both exit senses give the same integral, so only `|n_z|` enters. Measured at the anchor geometry: thick-target ratio to the bare form is exactly `2.0627 = 1/0.48481`, the thin limit is unchanged (the single-segment anchor still reads 0.997), and `figure_enhancement`'s duplicated copy of the bare expression now calls the same helper, moving its no-transport ceiling `68.5× → 33.5×` as predicted. Secondaries on this row also closed: `absorption_length_ang("C", …)` is replaced by a new `materials/crystal.py::crystal_absorption_length_ang`, which sums inverse lengths over the entry's own basis elements — so a non-carbon override no longer mixes carbon `f₂` with that crystal's density, and reduces exactly to the old call for all-carbon hopg; and `Validation: closed-form-flux` markers are now on `photons_per_electron` and `feranchuk_line_flux`. The corrected escape term has NOT had an independent fresh-context pass; the rest of the expression had one before this change. **Production `mc_cxr_spectrum` does NOT share the error** — it computes a real per-segment escape path; feeding it contiguous segments recovers `\|n_z\|=0.4848` to four digits. Since this expression is the yardstick the MC bulk results are compared against, every bulk-regime comparison made against it BEFORE 2026-09-13 overstated flux by up to 2.06× and should be re-read. Note the equation lives in `validation/feranchuk_spence.py::photons_per_electron`, not the ledgered wrapper. `figure_enhancement` recomputed the same bare expression (ceiling 68.5 vs escape-weighted 33.5) — that is the separate `enhancement-bulk-film` row, and the shared defect is fixed in both by the shared helper. [validation write-up](radiation-physics/closed-form-flux.md)

## `spxr-physical-population`

- **Claim:** for a fixed nonnegative integer physical population of independent, identically distributed electron channel amplitudes with finite second moment, the ensemble spectral yield separates into `N <|E|²> + N(N-1) |<E>|²`, with physical `N = Q/|e|` for nonnegative bunch-charge magnitude `Q` (equivalently `|Q_signed|/|e|` for an electron-only bunch), independent of numerical sample count
- **Code:** proposed only; no production estimator
- **Source:** expansion of the squared field sum under independence; spontaneous/coherent bunch contributions in Feranchuk, San and Skoromnik (2022), DOI 10.1103/PhysRevAccelBeams.25.120702, Eq. (36), p. 120702-7 (exact double sum); Eqs. (49)–(50), p. 120702-8 use continuum `N²`, distinct from exact pair count; [research derivation and assumptions](../research/physics/superradiant-pxr.md)
- **Status:** rederived
- **Checks:** fresh-context source-first derivation and 21 exact-rational finite-ensemble enumerations (zero residual); dimensionless population multiplies single-channel spectral-yield units; zero mean field leaves the linear self term, identical deterministic fields give the quadratic population limit, and one electron has no pair term
- **Anchor:** —
- **Notes:** research-only normalization requirement, not validation of current coherent output. Finite-sample pair-estimator bias, correlated bunches and physical-charge convergence remain to be independently derived and tested. Fresh-context verification (2026-11-11, #1101) rederived the fixed-count self/pair decomposition before reading the research note; explicit charge-magnitude and finite-moment qualifications match that model. No production estimator, correlated-bunch model or absolute yield is certified; no existing coherence claim is advanced. [Independent verification](radiation-physics/spxr-physical-population.md). Human sign-off remains separate.

## `quantum-pair-population-normalization`

- **Claim:** research claim (#501). For N electrons with reduced density matrices `ρ⁽¹⁾`, `ρ⁽²⁾` and the first-order one-photon channel operator `M̂` acting on one electron, the photon number is `⟨n⟩ = Σ_a Tr[ρ⁽¹⁾_a M̂†M̂] + Σ_{a≠b} Tr[ρ⁽²⁾_{ab}(M̂† ⊗ M̂)]`. For separable i.i.d. pairs (`ρ⁽²⁾ = ρ ⊗ ρ`) this is `N Tr(ρM̂†M̂) + N(N−1)|Tr(ρM̂)|²`. For a classical mixture of trajectory wavepackets with `M̂|j⟩ ≈ E_j|j⟩` it reduces to `spxr-physical-population`. That requires negligible recoil and localization below 1/k along k; otherwise the pair term carries the overlap `⟨j|j′⟩`. Reduced matrices are unit-trace marginals over ordered pairs. For any state `|Tr[ρ⁽²⁾(M̂†⊗M̂)]| ≤ Tr(ρ⁽¹⁾M̂†M̂)` (equal marginals), so `⟨n⟩ ≤ N² Tr(ρ⁽¹⁾M̂†M̂)`. The fermion exchange term `−Tr(γM̂†γM̂) ≤ 0` is at most degeneracy δ times the linear term and is dropped for δ ≪ 1.
- **Code:** `docs/research/physics/quantum-coherent-emission.md` (research claim; no production implementation)
- **Source:** expansion of `Â†Â` with `Â = Σ_a M̂⁽ᵃ⁾`; [research derivation](../research/physics/quantum-coherent-emission.md) §4; i.i.d. limit of `spxr-physical-population`; entangled-pair case, Karnieli et al., Phys. Rev. Lett. 127, 060403 (2021); modulated-wavepacket N² term, Zhang et al., arXiv:2401.05978
- **Status:** rederived
- **Checks:** fresh-context derivation and numerical spot check (Notes). Units: `⟨n⟩` in the channel's units, N dimensionless. Limits: N = 1 has no pair term; `Tr(ρM̂) = 0` leaves the linear term; identical `M̂`-eigenstates give N²; a classical mixture recovers `N⟨|E|²⟩ + N(N−1)|⟨E⟩|²`.
- **Anchor:** —
- **Notes:** Research-only normalization generalization. No estimator or production channel. Two-body evolution as `(Φ_z ⊗ Φ_z)` of independent single-electron Lindblad maps assumes uncorrelated environments (paths farther apart than the phonon/plasmon correlation lengths). **2026-10-15 fresh-context verdict `rederived`** ([write-up](radiation-physics/quantum-pair-population-normalization.md)): the reduced-density-matrix expansion, the i.i.d. limit, the Cauchy–Schwarz bound (general form `(Σ_a √T_a)²`) and the exchange sign and bound re-derive. A numerical spot check on `(ℂ⁴)^⊗3` agrees to ~1e-14 (random antisymmetric mixed, entangled label-symmetric, Slater and product states). The verdict was conditional on wording fixes to the c-number reduction, the N² eigenstate condition and the exchange statement; all three are applied here and in the research note. Human sign-off pending.

## `longitudinal-energy-diagonal-emission`

- **Claim:** research claim (#629). For one electron on an energy comb `E_n = E_0 + n ħΩ` with density matrix `ρ_nm`, emitting one photon in a static medium (`M̂|E_n⟩ = A_n(ω)|E_n − ħω⟩`), the reduced photon state is `ρ_ph(ω,ω′) = Σ_nm ρ_nm A_n(ω) A_m*(ω′) δ(E_n − E_m − ħ(ω − ω′))`. Its diagonal, the frequency-resolved photon count `Σ_n ρ_nn |A_n|²`, and photon–electron coincidence counts that are energy-resolved (or energy-integrated) for the electron are independent of the longitudinal coherences `ρ_{n≠m}` for any time-independent (position-dependent) interaction and exact recoil; coherences appear only at `ω − ω′ = (n − m)Ω`. For a laterally infinite static slab at normal incidence, the fixed-energy longitudinal mismatch `Δ = [p_i − ħg_z − ħk_z − p_f,z]/ħ`, `p_f,z = √(p(E − ħω)² − ħ²|g⊥ + k⊥|²)`, vanishes at the `line-recoil-resonance` root for a forward final electron (`p_i − ħg_z − ħk_z > 0`), with formation amplitude `∫₀ᴸ e^{iΔz} dz`
- **Code:** `quantum/_longitudinal.py::{photon_density_matrix,slab_mismatch_inv_ang,slab_formation_amplitude}`
- **Source:** first-order one-photon state with energy conservation in a static medium; [research derivation](../research/physics/longitudinal-coherence-recoil.md) §2–3.1; photon-state dependence on electron shape, Zhang et al., arXiv:2401.05978; wavefunction-independent spectrum with wavefunction-dependent off-diagonal coherence, Karnieli et al., Sci. Adv. 7, eabf8096 (2021) and Kfir et al., Sci. Adv. 7, eabf6380 (2021); recoil convention of Shi et al., Nat. Commun. 15, 7803 (2024), Eq. (6)
- **Status:** anchored
- **Checks:** explicit partial trace over final electron energies (not assumed); pure, dephased and re-phased states with exact-recoil slab amplitudes give equal counts (1e-13 of max); photon coherence zero off comb multiples and zero for mixtures; closed form of `ρ_ph(ω, ω − ℓΩ)`; Hermiticity; mismatch zero at the #249 root in three geometries with positive slope; slab amplitude `Δ → 0` limit
- **Anchor:** `tests/montecarlo/quantum/test_longitudinal_coherence_reference.py::{test_single_electron_photon_counts_ignore_longitudinal_coherence,test_photon_coherence_appears_only_at_comb_multiples,test_slab_mismatch_vanishes_at_vacuum_recoil_root,test_slab_formation_amplitude_limits}`
- **Notes:** Research reference only; no production channel. Amplitudes `A_n` are caller-supplied (no absolute coupling, absorption, refraction or polarization). Assumes no phonon creation in the emission vertex and ideal comb teeth. **2026-11-08 fresh-context verdict `rederived`** ([write-up](radiation-physics/longitudinal-energy-diagonal-emission.md)): the partial trace, comb-multiple support, slab mismatch and formation amplitude re-derive. `[S, H₀] = 0` makes every `H₀`-commuting observable blind to `ρ_{n≠m}` at all orders, which justifies the position-dependent, exact-recoil scope. Electron detection that is not energy-diagonal, and time-resolved photon detection, do see the coherences. The verdict was conditional on the coincidence-scope and forward-branch wording fixes, now applied here, in the research note and in the docstring. Independent checks: a general static S-matrix with degenerate final channels (3.6e-15), and the root mismatch in five geometries (≤ 5e-13 Å⁻¹). The owner applies `anchored` with the named passing tests. Human sign-off belongs to #277.

## `longitudinal-quantum-bunching`

- **Claim:** research claim (#629). For separable i.i.d. electrons on an energy comb, photons at `ω = ℓΩ` carry the pair amplitude `Tr(ρM̂) = Σ_n ρ_{n,n−ℓ} A_n`, bounded for any state by `max|A| Σ_n √(p_n p_{n−ℓ})` (energy-distribution overlap with its `ħω` shift; attained by amplitudes `√p_n` with constant `A`; Gaussian rms `σ`: `exp(−(ħω)²/8σ²)`). The bunching coefficient after vacuum drift `z` is `B_ℓ = Σ_n ρ_{n+ℓ,n} e^{i(k_{n+ℓ} − k_n)z}` with exact on-shell `k_n`. For ideal PINEM `c_n = J_n(2|g|)e^{in arg g}` and quadratic dispersion, `|B_ℓ| = |J_ℓ(4|g| sin(2πℓz/z_T))|`, `z_T = 4π(pc)³ħc/((mc²)²(ħΩ)²)`, reducing to the classical `|J_ℓ(ℓX)|`, `X = 8π|g|z/z_T`, as `ℓz/z_T → 0` at fixed `X`; its drift maximum is `max_{x ≤ 4|g|} |J_ℓ(x)|`
- **Code:** `quantum/_longitudinal.py::{comb_wavenumbers_inv_ang,talbot_length_ang,pinem_amplitudes,bunching_coefficient,pinem_bunching_closed_form,klystron_bunching,pair_amplitude,pair_amplitude_bound}`
- **Source:** pair expansion of `quantum-pair-population-normalization`; positivity `|ρ_nm|² ≤ ρ_nn ρ_mm`; Neumann–Graf addition theorem for Bessel functions; [research derivation](../research/physics/longitudinal-coherence-recoil.md) §3.2; PINEM amplitudes `e^{iN arg g} J_N(2|g|)` from Feist et al., Nature 521, 200 (2015), Eqs. (13)–(15); attosecond focus of Priebe et al., Nat. Photon. 11, 793 (2017) (2.75 mm for `g = 3.95`, 120 keV, 800 nm)
- **Status:** anchored
- **Checks:** Talbot length against exact on-shell curvature (1e-5); exact-`k` bunching against the closed form (2e-6; observed ≤7e-7 is float64 cancellation in `k_{n+ℓ} − k_n`, cubic dispersion ≤6e-9 at `z/z_T ≤ 0.05`); klystron limit with `O((z/z_T)²)` convergence; quantum cutoff deep beyond the coherent comb width (`ℓ = 2.5·4|g|`: `< 1e-5` where classical `> 0.15`; suppression is exponential only for `ℓ − 4|g| ≫ ℓ^{1/3}`); Gaussian factor (1e-12) and zero for dephased mixtures; Cauchy–Schwarz self-term bound; energy-overlap bound holds and is attained; exact-`k` peak density at Priebe's 2.75 mm (0.1 mm), fixing the `J_n(2|g|)` factor and the `z_T` convention
- **Anchor:** `tests/montecarlo/quantum/test_longitudinal_coherence_reference.py::{test_talbot_length_matches_exact_dispersion_curvature,test_pinem_bunching_matches_neumann_graf_closed_form,test_quantum_bunching_reduces_to_klystron_limit,test_quantum_bunching_vanishes_beyond_coherent_comb_width,test_gaussian_wavepacket_pair_factor_and_dephased_mixture,test_pair_amplitude_is_bounded_by_self_term,test_pair_bound_from_energy_distribution_holds_and_is_attained,test_pinem_attosecond_focus_matches_priebe_2017}`; regime tables `checks/longitudinal_coherence_regimes.py`
- **Notes:** Research reference only. Supplies the theorem behind the #629 decision that a classical energy spread or bunch duration gives no collective `N²` term; does not change `coherent-inter-electron-decoherence`. Proposed record-only admissibility screen is follow-up I1 in the research note. **2026-11-08 fresh-context verdict `rederived`** ([write-up](radiation-physics/longitudinal-quantum-bunching.md)): the pair amplitude and index convention, positivity bound and attainment, Gaussian factor, Talbot length (4π, exact-revival convention), Neumann–Graf closed form and fixed-`X` classical limit re-derive. Code matches; independent 50-digit checks agree. Wording fixes applied: residual attribution, §5 grid artifacts (now an exact `max_{x≤4|g|}`), the 2.3e-121 bound, the soft (Airy) cutoff, constant-`A` attainment. Owner source check 2026-11-08: Feist 2015 Eqs. (13)–(15) give exactly the `c_n` convention; neither paper states `z_T` in closed form, but exact-`k` propagation reproduces Priebe's 2.75 mm focus, so both conventions are confirmed. The owner applies `anchored` with the named passing tests. Human sign-off belongs to #277.

## `zhai-hbn-921-detected`

- **Claim:** end-to-end detected-spectrum anchor vs Zhai SI Fig. S5b (h-BN 921 nm, 17.5–25 keV, tilt 17°/130°)
- **Code:** `src/pyrite/validation/anchor_figures.py::_supplementary_detected_spectrum` (+ `model_coherent_spectra`)
