These paper values are not digitized measurements with an uncertainty budget. Returning to the pre-#338 Friedel pairing changes each source spectrum by <2.3e-16 of its maximum: the fix does not affect this panel. No missing global beta/gamma, angular-frequency-to-eV, solid-angle or current factor was found. The model follows the SI's central-ray density times 0.066 sr and Gaussian aperture/EDS broadening; it does not perform angular quadrature. Nominal QE increases the convolved-peak ratio relative to unit QE from 0.612 to 0.664 across the energy series; the baseline/opacity-free peak ratio rises from 0.596 to 0.666. These identify modeled energy-dependent factors, **not an established cause of the experiment/model mismatch**, because the SI does not specify the actual detector QE or efficiency-correction convention. Source approximation differences (frozen resonance prefactor/couplings, omitted cross-reflection interference, in-medium kinematics) remain separately visible in the write-up. The retained `(004)` family supplies only **0.54/0.40/0.23/0.18%** of the detected peak at a common response width. Changing B33=0.6 to 3.45 Å² lowers the mixed peak by **0.8772/0.8778/0.8785/0.8787**; B11 cannot affect these basal-only reflections. Thus changing the Debye–Waller mix does not explain the trend. Bin-mean instead of 1 eV node quadrature changes detected peaks by ≤0.012%. 200→2000 electrons changes peaks by up to 3.1%, much smaller than the residual; the earlier ≤2% statement is not a current convergence bound. An **assumed** ±20% thickness sensitivity (736.8/1105.2 nm, not measured error bars) leaves the excess and trend; no numerical thickness uncertainty is published for this sample. SI reports ~1 mrad divergence and <1% current fluctuation, with no experimental spot-size energy dependence for this panel. **History:** after the July aperture/window correction the excess was 1.35–1.76×; after the Pease B33 correction, September peaks were 0.983/1.031/1.032/1.030 (1.16–1.47×). Their inferred substantial `(004)` admixture is not supported by the current family breakdown. The validation-only `closed-form-flux` repair is outside this source path. The residual cause remains unresolved; do not treat the supplementary-panel absolute normalization as validated. **2026-11-15 #1235 RECHECK (catalog h-BN B33 3.45 → 1.73, `hbn-debye-waller-00l`):** the anchor reads catalog `B_ang2` through `build_cases`. The detected spectrum is linear in each family's e^{−2Bs²}: rescaling the recorded f3b44c1e 2000-electron `(002)`/`(004)` fractions reproduces the recorded `zero_B` and `retired_B_0p6` peaks to 5 decimals, and gives B=1.73 peaks **1.1074/1.1550/1.1653/1.1629** (×1.081–1.082), i.e. **1.303/1.444/1.554/1.661×** the approximate paper peaks. Same-seed local 200-electron runs at branch head `9ce1a5595` + this change (CPU fp64, baseline only; anchor omits `bremslib_tables`, so uncoupled radiative scoring) give B=3.45 **1.0816/1.1518/1.1668/1.1057** and B=1.73 **1.1708/1.2454/1.2617/1.1954** (B-only factor 1.081–1.083, matching e^{2·1.72/c²}=1.081), ratios 1.38/1.56/1.68/1.71× at ne=200. The B=3.45 head run sits 4.4–5.0% above the recorded f3b44c1e 200-electron peaks; that interim drift predates this correction and is not attributed here. The uncoupled radiative path is shared with the f3b44c1e record and does not explain the drift. The correction widens the excess and does not change the rising trend. **2000-electron recompute (SLURM 13218, qlmc-queen, CPU fp64, revision `a41ea1280`, same seeds 92100–92103; uncoupled radiative scoring because the anchor omits `bremslib_tables`, despite installed tables):** B=1.73 detected peaks **1.1558/1.2085/1.2219/1.2201** = **1.360/1.511/1.629/1.743×** the approximate paper peaks; in-run B-only factor vs `retired_B_0p6` is 0.949–0.950. The B-independent `zero_B` variant is **+4.4/+4.6/+4.9/+4.9%** above the f3b44c1e record and every source peak sits **8 eV lower** (798/845/888/928 vs 806/853/896/936 eV) with identical segment counts, so the drift is in emission-model changes since f3b44c1e, not transport or this correction; its origin is not attributed here. Result kept as diagnostic evidence only; `check-records/zhai_hbn_energy_trend.json` is not republished (requires the reviewed `--publish` path). Enabling coupled BremsLib transport would change this anchor configuration and is outside #1235.

## `kinematic-validity-envelope`

- **Claim:** Photon Born screen: $D = \omega^2\lvert\chi_g\rvert/\lvert\Delta\rvert$, $L_{\rm ext}=2/(\omega\lvert\chi_g\rvert)$ and $R=L_{\rm ext}/\min(t,L_{\rm abs})$, using $\omega=E/(\hbar c)$ and vacuum $\Delta=g^2+2\omega\hat{\mathbf n}\cdot\mathbf g$. Runtime warning requires both $D\ge0.01$ and $R\le1$; conservative screening policy, not a dynamical solution or quantified error bound.
- **Code:** `materials/crystal.py::kinematic_validity`, `campaign/kinematic_validity.py::{_radiator_audit,_radiator_optics}`
- **Source:** Feranchuk et al., Phys. Rev. E 62, 4225 (2000), photon eigenmode, reproduced in Zhai et al. (2025), SI Eq. (3); resonant scalar two-beam coupled-wave rate $\omega\lvert\chi_g\rvert/2$.
- **Status:** anchored
- **Checks:** units; zero coupling and thickness; exact Bragg detuning; no absorption; sign symmetry. Case adapter retains the offline audit's positive-g resonance and plus-g detuning; tests cover orientation and crystalline layers.
- **Anchor:** `tests/materials/test_kinematic_validity.py::test_reference_parameters`, `::test_thick_silicon_and_thin_hopg`; `tests/campaign/test_kinematic_validity.py`
- **Notes:** Issue #342. Fresh-context verifier rederived the scalar parameters and source-to-code agreement; analytic and Si/HOPG anchors pass. Central incident ray and scalar detector direction, vacuum optical kinematics. No certification over scattering, mosaic domains, energy spread or detector acceptance; the extinction flag only says that a Bragg exchange length is reachable. Optical-data misses are recorded explicitly. Thresholds are project policy. [Independent derivation](radiation-physics/kinematic-validity-envelope.md). Human sign-off belongs to #277.

## `cxr-high-order-kinematic-scope`

- **Claim:** Scope of the incoherent first-order PXR/CBS line model for high-order reflections, on every non-forbidden reflection with $\lvert g\rvert\le 30$ Å⁻¹ in the #642 scenes (Si 300 keV / 10 µm; MoS₂ 2000 Å on 20 µm Si, 30 keV; 45° tilt). The scope rests on four statements.
  - **(i) Photon Born screen** (`kinematic-validity-envelope`). For $f_0$, $\omega^2\chi_g\propto F_g e^{-W}$ does not depend on $\omega$. At the classical resonance, $\lvert\Delta\rvert/g^2=\Gamma=(1-\beta c_m)/(1-\beta c_v)$ is independent of $\lvert g\rvert$ and bounded: $g^2/\lvert\Delta\rvert\le(1+\beta)/(1-\beta)$, which is 7.95 at 300 keV and 1.98 at 30 keV, but about $4\gamma^2$ at MeV energies. So $D\propto\lvert F_g\rvert e^{-W}/g^2$, the `dominant_reflections` ranking metric, up to $\Gamma^{-1}$ and $\lvert F(E_{\rm line})/F(E_{\rm ref})\rvert$. Higher orders are more kinematic, not less. Central-ray $D\le2.8\times10^{-4}$ over all 33,764 evaluated reflections, and over all emission directions $D\le1.6\times10^{-3}$ (Si 300 keV), $4.1\times10^{-4}$ (Si 30 keV) and $1.1\times10^{-3}$ (MoS₂). None reaches $0.01$.
  - **(ii) Harmonic isotropic Debye–Waller.** Line intensity scales as $e^{-2W}$ with $W=B(\lvert g\rvert/4\pi)^2$, so $\partial\ln I_g/\partial B=-\lvert g\rvert^2/(8\pi^2)$, which is −11.4 Å⁻² at $\lvert g\rvert=30$ Å⁻¹.
  - **(iii) Atomic data range.** $f_0$ (Waasmaier–Kirfel) holds for $s=\lvert g\rvert/4\pi\le6$ Å⁻¹, i.e. $\lvert g\rvert\le75.4$ Å⁻¹.
  - **(iv) Recoil kinematics.** The resonance root is exact in every line route (`line-recoil-in-medium-root`). The 206 keV figure for Si at 300 keV is the kinematic bound $\hbar c\beta g/(1-\beta)$; the central-ray maximum is 45.5 keV.
- **Code:** `materials/crystal.py::dominant_reflections`
- **Source:** Feranchuk et al., Phys. Rev. E 62, 4225 (2000), Eq. (3) as reproduced in Zhai et al. (2025) SI; Debye–Waller $W=B\sin^2\theta/\lambda^2$ (International Tables for Crystallography Vol. C, §6.1.1.3); Waasmaier & Kirfel, Acta Cryst. A51, 416 (1995), validity $s\le6$ Å⁻¹.
- **Status:** rederived
- **Checks:** central-ray screen `campaign/kinematic_validity.py::case_kinematic_validity` over the full g30 sets of both scenes (#1173 P1), reproduced independently. An independent $\chi_g$ (xraydb $f_0$ + Chantler $f',f''$, $e^{-W}$, CODATA $r_e$) matches the code's $D$ to about $10^{-8}$ on four high-order Si reflections. The $\Gamma$ bound gives the all-direction $D$ bound. $\partial\ln I/\partial B$ matches the finite difference of `debye_waller`². The limit $g\to\infty$ gives $D\to0$.
- **Anchor:** `tests/campaign/test_kinematic_validity.py::test_high_order_silicon_set_stays_kinematic_and_ranks_with_born_parameter`. It pins Si 300 keV at $\lvert g\rvert\le22$ only; the g30 and MoS₂ figures are not pinned.
- **Notes:** Issue #1173 P1. The screen is central-ray and vacuum only (no scattered flights, mosaic or detector acceptance), the same scope as `kinematic-validity-envelope`; the $\Gamma$ bound extends $D$ to every direction. Extinction is reachable ($R\le1$) for 132 Si (300 keV), 47 MoS₂ and 275 Si-substrate reflections, but never together with photon mixing. 23 reflections (5 Si, 13 MoS₂, 5 Si-substrate) have $\mathbf g\cdot\hat v=0$. Their central-ray resonance is at zero energy (roundoff ≤ $3.3\times10^{-12}$ eV), below the optical tables, so they are recorded `outside_optical_data` and not screened. **Not claimed:**
  - Site-resolved or anisotropic ADPs. MoS₂ uses one crystal-wide scalar $B=0.47$ Å² from (00l)-scoped provenance (`debye-waller-catalog-provenance`), while its g30 set is dominated by nonparallel reflections. Its "converged" g30 reference is therefore converged within the model, and its absolute high-order yield inherits the scalar-B error. Si is cubic with one site, so a scalar $B=0.4632$ Å² (Spackman; was 0.46 before #1189) is exact by symmetry within the harmonic model.
  - The spinless convective line amplitude (`cbs-amplitude`). Magnetic-moment terms are dropped at $O(h^2)$, and they grow with $(\hbar g/mc)^2$ (0.116 at $g=30$) and $\hbar\omega/mc^2$ (0.40 at 206 keV).
  - A $q$-independent $f'+if''$ added to $f_0(s)$. It exceeds $f_0$ for 10–24 % of high-order Si reflections.
  - Phonon-assisted (thermal-diffuse) emission carrying the $1-e^{-2W}$ remainder (not a line, omitted), anharmonicity, and dynamical diffraction.
- **Verification:** 2026-11-13, fresh context. Verdict `discrepancy` on the claim wording only, with no fault in the code or the numbers. The suggested edits (zero-energy records, the $\Gamma$ bound, $f_0$-only $\omega$-independence, recoil narrowed to the root, the spinless-amplitude and $q$-independent anomalous caveats, anchor scope) are applied here, and the row is `rederived` on that basis. [Derivation](radiation-physics/cxr-high-order-kinematic-scope.md).

## `reflection-yield-tolerance-selector`

- **Claim:** For supported incoherent production scoring, shell densities add on a fixed case transport, energy axis, direction and material/absorption model, up to floating-point reassociation and production-kernel approximation errors. Each radiator's shell yield is computed with trapezoid weights for `node`, or production bin widths for `bin-mean`.
  - **Estimate:** after at least three positive finite shells with two consecutive strict decreases, use $r_k=\max(s_k/s_{k-1},s_{k-1}/s_{k-2})$ and $\tau_k=s_kr_k/[(1-r_k)\sum_{j\le k}s_j]$.
  - **Stop rule:** stop at the first shell with $\tau_k\le\mathrm{tol}$. A zero-yield shell prevents convergence; exhaustion retains all candidates and reports unconverged.
  - **Net metric (v4, #1250 P5c):** for a radiator whose continuum is lattice-share-corrected (`brem-lattice-coherent-fraction`), $s_k=L_k-S_k$: the shell's in-band line yield less the in-band continuum yield of its share-only tables (production continuum scorer, the case's continuum electrons, line-cutoff floor, 96 log nodes per decade, trapezoid). Decay and ratios use $|s_k|$; $\tau_k=|s_k|r_k/[(1-r_k)(\sum_{j\le k}s_j+C_r)]$ with $C_r$ the radiator layer's uncorrected in-band continuum; a non-positive denominator or a zero $|s_k|$ gives $\infty$. Shell continuum shares add because the scorer is linear in the table (exact except at clamped DDCS nodes). Uncorrected radiators keep the line metric.
  - **Not a bound:** this is extrapolation relative to kept yield, not a certificate for actual omitted yield. Bounding every future ratio by $r_k<1$ is sufficient for the geometric remainder to bound the true tail; two observed ratios do not establish that condition. The max-of-two rule reduces sensitivity to a recent dip without bounding unobserved shells. Under the net metric a zero crossing of $s_k$ can stop the search early: PXR and interference fall faster than CBS, so $s_k/L_k\to1-S/\mathrm{CBS}$ at large $G$, and with $S/\mathrm{CBS}\gtrsim1$ a small $|s_k|$ near the crossing reads as a converged tail (synthetic case: stop at $K=5$, estimate 3.2e-4, true omission 2.9e-2). The #642 probes stop at $s_k/L_k$ = 0.15–0.21, away from a crossing.
  - **Quadrature and scope:** bin-width weighting integrates represented bin means; numerical accuracy inherits the selected production reduction route. The far-envelope allowance is relative to complete line mass, not arbitrary in-band yield. Enumeration is limited by the form-factor cap and metric floor.
- **Code:** `montecarlo/runner/reflection_cutoff.py::{geometric_tail_fraction,select_reflection_cutoff,select_case_reflections,selected_line_spectrum,axis_band_eV,validate_reflection_selection,iter_reflection_shells,_selected_set_record}`; fiber domain `materials/crystal.py::_reflection_families`, `materials/_parse.py::_crystal_texture`; net-metric continuum `montecarlo/runner/lattice_share.py::{SelectionContinuum,share_only_tables}` and `montecarlo/runner/emission.py::_layer_brem_from_segments`; spectrum-phase hook `montecarlo/runner/case_driver.py::_line_phase`
- **Production scoring (v5, #1243):** supported ungrouped automatic `kinematic-ceiling` and `resonance-population` axes select on coarse bin-mean bins spanning the common payload start and clamped ceiling. Shell line yields, continuum shares and background all use that band. Production separately scores the selected union on its own axis; reference evaluations do not feed its truncation audit. Fixed axes, grouped flights, truncated sinc profiles and explicit numeric node-support windows retain production-axis shell scoring. Bin-reducer approximation limits remain applicable. Fresh-context review: `rederived`; new-anchor CI unobserved.
- **Electron-prefix selection (v6, #1281):** shell yields, continuum shares and background are scored on the counter-addressed prefix `elec_id < SELECTION_HISTORIES` (4096) of the line and continuum populations; the selected set is then scored on every history. The cutoff is therefore a function of the first 4096 histories only: its cost no longer grows with $N$, and its sampling noise does not shrink with $N$. The selected set is identical for every $N\ge4096$ at fixed seed only on counter-addressed transport cores (per-electron, CUDA), with fixed $\min(N_e^{\rm brem},4096)$. An energy spread rebuilds transport tables over an $N$-dependent range, so agreement there is only to round-off. On lockstep (CPU default, grooved runs) the prefix histories depend on $N$. Exactly v5 when both $N_e$ and $N_e^{\rm brem}$ are within the budget; otherwise only the over-budget population is restricted. The record adds `selection_histories`. Engineering evidence (RTX 5080, `e52bd7465`): prefix 1024 and full selections agree on 11/11 automatic-selection cleo_sub runs (MoS₂ 30/100/1000 keV, WS₂ 30/100 keV; N = 4096–8192). Fresh-context review of v6 (2026-11-18): `rederived` after wording edits; anchor pins the elec_id cut, normalization and rescoring on one transport, not cross-$N$ invariance or unequal prefixes.
- **Fiber-texture domain (v7, #1301):** for catalog `texture = "fiber"` (HOPG; pyrolytic h-BN) candidates are the integer multiples $na$ of the primitive reciprocal surface axis within the form-factor cap ($hA\parallel aA\iff h\parallel a$ for nonsingular $A$), before Debye–Waller weighting; shells, tail, continuum shares and reconstruction share that domain, estimator and tolerance unchanged. Replaces the (002)/(004) pins, which are its first two shells. Code: `materials/crystal.py::_reflection_families`, `materials/_parse.py::_crystal_texture`, `montecarlo/runner/reflection_cutoff.py::{iter_reflection_shells,_selected_set_record}`. Anchor: `tests/montecarlo/runner/test_fiber_reflection_selection.py::test_frozen_fiber_shells_select_and_reconstruct_the_same_basal_set` (job 13676 frozen HOPG shells); `tests/materials/test_crystal_texture.py`. Fresh-context review (2026-11-18): `rederived` (independent Miller/Cartesian domain probes and a re-implemented stop reproduce the frozen cutoffs and tails); anchors green locally, new-anchor CI unobserved. [Fiber-texture domain](radiation-physics/reflection-yield-tolerance-selector.md#fiber-texture-candidate-domain-1301-v7).
- **Band-mass decision yield (v8, #1326):** a shell's yield on the reference band is $\sum_\ell w_\ell(\pi/a_\ell)[F(x^{\rm hi}_\ell)-F(x^{\rm lo}_\ell)]$ over its line arrays (`sinc-bin-integration`), the telescoped integral of the tiling bin means without the production far-envelope term (at most $1.2\times10^{-5}$ of a line's mass, `sinc-bin-far-envelope`), so a marginal shell may flip. The reference pass generates lines on the band and measures them with a `mass_only` audit (no bin reduction), and the selected set is rescored on the production axis, so the decision stays on the reference band alone. A production-axis single pass was measured and dropped: on thick targets it reduced the rejected trailing shells on the production axis and was never faster (#1326). Code: `montecarlo/spectrum/cxr/_band_mass.py::sincsq_band_mass`, `montecarlo/spectrum/cxr/_kernels.py::_accumulate_edge_truncation` (`band_eV`/`band_mass`), `montecarlo/runner/reflection_cutoff.py::{_selection_mode,_pilot_scorer,_band_evaluator}`. Anchors: `tests/montecarlo/spectrum/test_band_mass.py` (closed form against summed `band_bin_grid` bin means), `tests/montecarlo/runner/test_reflection_selection_spectrum.py::test_axis_spanning_the_band_selects_on_band_mass_then_rescores`, `tests/montecarlo/transport/test_streamed_selection.py::test_streamed_band_mass_selection_matches_retained`. Fresh-context re-verification (2026-11-21, `64934c9fa`, after the single pass was removed): closed form, limits, units and prefix-block linearity rederived (mpmath, 2.4e-16 of a line's mass over 300 random lines and bands); anchors `test_band_mass.py`, `test_axis_spanning_the_band_selects_on_band_mass_then_rescores` and slow `test_streamed_band_mass_selection_matches_retained` green locally and in PR #1328 CI (slow tier) at `321e02f4a`, whose code and tests match the reviewed head; device kernel anchor green on qlmc RTX 5080 (job 13841). [Derivation](radiation-physics/reflection-yield-tolerance-selector.md#exact-band-mass-decision-yield-v8-1326), [re-verification](radiation-physics/reflection-yield-tolerance-selector.md#band-mass-re-verification-v8-1326-2026-11-21).
- **Row-budget selection prefix (v9, #1368):** the v6 prefix ends at $n=\min(N_{\max},4096,k_R)$ electrons, $k_R$ the smallest count whose segment rows $\sum_{i<k}r_i$ reach `SELECTION_ROWS`; line and continuum prefixes are $\min(N_e,n)$ and $\min(N_e^{\rm brem},n)$. Whole-history cut, so conditional on $n$ the stop is v6 at that prefix; $k_R$ depends only on the first $k_R$ histories, so the v6 $N$-invariance (counter-addressed cores, fixed $\min(N_e^{\rm brem},n)$, monoenergetic beam) carries over when $N_e^{\rm brem}$ is fixed or $\ge k_R$. Thin films whose first 4096 electrons leave fewer rows keep v6/v8 exactly. Selection cost becomes $O(\texttt{SELECTION\_ROWS})$ rows on thick targets; the cutoff inherits the noise of $n<4096$ histories there. The record adds `rows` and `row_budget` to `selection_histories`. Code: `montecarlo/runner/electron_blocks.py::row_budget_prefix`, `montecarlo/runner/reflection_cutoff.py::_batch_selection`, `montecarlo/runner/streamed_selection.py::PrefixSelectingScorer`. Anchors: `tests/montecarlo/runner/test_reflection_selection_spectrum.py::{test_selection_scores_an_electron_prefix_then_every_history,test_row_budget_binds_before_the_history_cap,test_row_budget_prefix_counts_whole_electrons}`, `tests/montecarlo/transport/test_streamed_selection.py::test_streamed_row_budget_prefix_matches_retained`. `SELECTION_ROWS` $=2^{24}$: the cap binds on every measured 500 nm, 10 µm and 100 µm case (rows at 4096 electrons ≤ 1.42e7); 1 mm mos2 5 MeV (seeds 0–2) and mos2/mose2 30 MeV select the 4096-prefix set at R = 1.6e7 and 6.4e7 (879–1182 electrons at 1.6e7); 1 mm mos2/mose2 1 MeV, whose 4096 prefix runs out of device memory (#1416), now select; 1 mm mose2 5 MeV fails host-memory admission before selection (#1417) (RTX 5080, jobs 14524–14528, 15384). Acceptance at $R=2^{24}$: 1 mm mos2 5 MeV, N=4096, default route: selection 439 s vs 1860–1988 s, same set `00cb4b86` (job 15385). Anchor `tests/montecarlo/transport/test_streamed_adaptive.py::test_row_budget_selection_in_the_monitored_pass` (#1397 provisional scoring). Fresh-context review (2026-11-24): `rederived` after wording edits; the provisional-scoring record-presence difference it found is fixed (`streamed_selection.py::_realized_record`); anchors green locally, CI unobserved. [Re-verification](radiation-physics/reflection-yield-tolerance-selector.md#row-budget-re-verification-v9-1368-2026-11-24). [Derivation](radiation-physics/reflection-yield-tolerance-selector.md#row-budget-selection-prefix-v9-1368).
- **Source:** Additivity of incoherent line intensities over reflections (`line-hkl-batch`); Debye–Waller $W=B(\lvert g\rvert/4\pi)^2$ (`structure-factor`); geometric-series remainder.
- **Status:** anchored

The DW factor alone gives 0.51 and DW × shell-multiplicity ratio gives 0.58, so this does not test the multiplicity term. The single-ratio estimate at g22 was 6.6e-3 against 6.67e-3 measured to g40.

## `coherent-physical-bunch-population`

- **Claim:** Per-physical-electron coherent source power estimated with distinct incident Monte Carlo pairs; physical bunch charge weights the cross-electron excess, sample count normalizes the estimator
- **Code:** `montecarlo/spectrum/coherent_population.py::{physical_bunch_electrons,pair_scale,mixed_row_power,require_resolved_power}`; `montecarlo/spectrum/cxr/_temporal.py::add_coherent_row`
- **Source:** Independent-electron field expansion; Su et al., Nuclear Science and Techniques 29, 30 (2018), Eq. (2), identical-emitter `N[1+(N-1)F]` limit; SI elementary charge
- **Status:** rederived
- **Checks:** Implementation-context units, distinct-pair normalization, identical aligned fields, zero-mean iid fields, missed entries, Gaussian longitudinal limit, negative-estimate refusal, temporal and detector normalization; fresh-context independent derivation and source-to-code comparison match
- **Anchor:** `tests/montecarlo/coherent/test_coherent_physical_population.py` (including the Gaussian-arrival blend `::test_gaussian_arrival_spread_blends_by_the_longitudinal_form_factor` and the long-bunch incoherent limit `::test_long_bunch_reduces_to_the_incoherent_sum`); `tests/montecarlo/coherent/test_temporal_profile.py`
- **Notes:** [Derivation, assumptions and integration limits](radiation-physics/coherent-physical-bunch-population.md). Production supplies physical charge; low-level callers without it retain historical semantics. Nonnegative output is not a sampling certificate. Convex full-axis audits refuse signed weights they cannot enclose. Earlier remote evidence must be repeated under this operator. Does not promote the full windowed-resolution claim; human sign-off pending. **2026-10-08 (#370):** finite footprints pair offset-free fields with `F = F_z F_perp`; identity `physical-distinct-pairs-v2`. Infinite slabs still pair complete fields with `F = 1`, now including the tilted face-arrival delay in `t0`.

## `spectral-explicit-rate-normalization`

- **Claim:** Fixed stored photon yield per incident electron becomes per-second yield with I/|e| or per-ampere coefficient with 1/|e|, preserving native energy sampling and paired axis-density conversion; matching scalar acceptance is applied once, with explicit uniform-source acknowledgment or matching observation-average pixel solid angle
- **Code:** `results/_spectral_rate.py::{per_current_factor,rate_factor}`; `results/model.py::Result.accepted_spectra`; `results/quantified.py::QuantifiedSpectrum.rate`; `results/quantified_directional.py::QuantifiedDirectionalSpectrum.rate`; `results/quantified_spatial.py::QuantifiedSpatialSpectrum.rate`; `results/quantified_aperture.py::QuantifiedApertureSpectrum.rate`
- **Source:** [BIPM SI ampere](https://www.bipm.org/en/si-base-units/ampere), exact elementary charge `1.602176634e-19` C; matching solid-angle average definition and energy-coordinate Jacobian
- **Status:** anchored
- **Checks:** Units, signs, fixed-yield coherent bunch convention, zero current, per-current coefficient, native integrated yields/bin masses, source acknowledgment, matching observation acceptance, duplicate/invalid transforms, unsupported NaNs, no response/acceptance replay
- **Anchor:** `tests/results/test_explicit_spectral_rates.py`

## `diffracted-bremsstrahlung`

- **Claim:** every perfect single-slab crystal case (channeling on or off) adds, to both line spectra `spec` and `spec_coherent`, the diffracted bremsstrahlung (DBR) of each listed reflection with `n·g < 0` and a Laue Bragg line near the grid. That is forward incoherent bremsstrahlung emitted along `Q̂ = (k n + g)/|k n + g|`, Bragg-diffracted into `n` at `E_B = −ħc g²/(2 n·g)`. By reciprocity a row emitting the unattenuated brem density `j(Q̂, E)` at depth `z` contributes `j(Q̂, E)·½Σ_s|D_s(L − z, E)|²` per steradian of `n`. `D_s(u) = b[e^{iλ_1u} − e^{iλ_2u}]/(λ_1 − λ_2)` is the diffracted amplitude of the time-reversed two-beam Laue mode (unit transmitted, zero diffracted at the exit face), and `λ_1,2` are the eigenvalues of `N = [[kχ_0/2n_s, a],[b, q + k²χ_0/2Q_s]]`, with `q = −det/2Q_s`, `det = g² + 2k n·g`, `ab = k⁴|χ_g|²C_s²/(4k n_sQ_s)` and `|b|² = k⁴|χ_g|²C_s²/(4Q_s²)`. Kinematically, `∫dE|D|² = |b|²2πu ħcQ_s/|n·g|`, linear in the remaining depth `u`. `j` is the native track-length brem density (same continuum model, tables, cutoff and channeling flux weight) toward `Q̂` at `E_B`, without the escape factor; the mode carries both beams' absorption (`Im χ_0`), refraction (`Re χ_0`) and Pendellösung depletion (`ab`).
- **Code:** `src/pyrite/montecarlo/spectrum/diffracted_brem.py::{laue_mode_intensities,laue_diffracted_intensity,_segment_density,_depth_histogram,bragg_window,reflection_line,deposit,bragg_reflections,dbr_depth_weights,dbr_from_depth_weights,diffracted_brem_spectrum}`; `src/pyrite/montecarlo/runner/diffracted_brem.py::{case_diffracted_brem,DiffractedDepthAccumulator}`, called from `src/pyrite/montecarlo/runner/case_driver.py::_spectrum_case_impl` and, accumulated over blocks, `src/pyrite/montecarlo/runner/streamed_scoring.py::BlockSpectrumScorer.result`. Marker `DIFFRACTED_BREM_MODEL` (`two-beam-laue-dbr-v1`; `two-beam-laue-dbr-kossel-v2` since #1176) in every dataset identity and case content key. #1176 refactor, DBR output unchanged: `_depth_histogram` takes 2-D weights and `reflection_line` samples `bragg_window`.
- **Source:** reciprocity (photon-mode) emission into a crystal eigenmode, as in the Feranchuk PXR formulation (`pxr-amplitude`, `cr-planar-zone-pxr-modulation`); two-beam Laue dynamical diffraction (Authier, *Dynamical Theory of X-ray Diffraction*, slowly varying two-beam equations; kinematic integrated reflectivity `R_θ = πk|χ_g|²C²t/(2γ_0 sin 2θ_B)`); diffracted forward radiation as treated for channeling radiation (DCR) by Yabuki et al., Phys. Rev. B 63, 174112 (2001), and Korotchenko, Pivovarov & Tukhfatullin, NIM B 269, 2840 (2011) (Zotero `TEATYMDK`, `3EPVWNF4`); DBR is the incoherent-brem analogue. The derivation is the author's ([write-up](radiation-physics/diffracted-bremsstrahlung.md)).
- **Assumptions:** `χ_gχ_{−g}` replaced by `|χ_g|²` (no anomalous/Borrmann absorption: negligible at µm thickness, conservative for thick crystals); the two-beam depletion `(γ_h/γ_n)|D|²` of the direct emission toward `n̂` is not scored: its band is `−5.8e-6`, `−1.0e-5`, `−6.1e-6` of the DBR gain at the #1166 mirror rays (0 2 −2), (1 1 −3), (−1 3 1) (`j(n̂)/j(Q̂)` = 3–7e-6; #1176 one-ray estimate); isotropic lines take it in `diffracted-characteristic-radiation`; eigenvalue branch `Im(λ_1 − λ_2) ≥ 0`; two-beam Laue geometry (`n·ŝ > 0`, `Q·ŝ > 0`), both angles above `3√|χ_0|`; Bragg-case (backward) DBR not scored; perfect single slab, laterally infinite for the mode (no mosaic, layer stacks or grooves: those cases add nothing); first-order two-beam equations with `χ_g`, `χ_0` flat across the line and taken at `E_B`; unpolarized brem (`½Σ_s`); `j` and `Q̂` frozen at `E_B` across the eV-wide line; `|Q| = k` to first order in `χ`; rows spread uniformly over their depth range onto 64 depth bins; line sampled over `±200` kinematic widths plus 20 Pendellösung widths (≤ ±1.5 keV, ≤ 40 000 nodes, step ≤ a quarter `2π/L` width); point detector at `n` as native line scoring; the extinction dip of forward brem toward `n` itself (at most `j(n)∫|D|²dE`) is not subtracted from the continuum; routes that score lines through `_cxr_for_segments` alone (adaptive `"cxr"` stopping measure, energy-grid bandwidth/convergence probes, line roulette) do not see the DBR line.
- **Limiting cases:** the intensity solves the two-beam mode equations (`expm` of `N`, 1e-8); kinematic `∫dE|D|²` equals `|b|²2πuħcQ_s/|n·g|` (2e-3, window truncation); bin-mean deposit conserves the line band (1e-9); kinematic DBR equals the independent native brem toward `Q̂` × kinematic `R_E` × mean remaining depth ½ (within 3%: escape factor and the actual depth distribution).
- **Status:** rederived
- **Checks:** 17 MeV Si, 2 µm, BremsLib, channeling off, mirror rays (`Q̂` on the beam): DBR/native PXR band of the same reflection = 0.36–24 over ten reflections at 6–10 keV. Full / kinematic independent estimate = 0.94 (0 2 −2, 7 keV), 0.99 (1 1 −3), 0.98 (−1 3 1). At (0 2 −2) the dynamical depletion (`√(ab)·t ≈ 0.6`) is −5% and two-beam absorption −4%.
- **Anchor:** `tests/montecarlo/spectrum/test_diffracted_brem.py`
- **Notes:** #1166 item 2. It changes channeling-off output. The `two-beam-laue-dbr-v1` marker re-pins every dataset identity and content key. JUNGFRAU-square share at the #1148 centres pending (#1166). **2026-11-13 fresh-context verdict `concern`, `rederived` after two fixes** ([write-up](radiation-physics/diffracted-bremsstrahlung.md#fresh-context-verification-2026-11-13)): reciprocity, mode, kinematic `R_θ` relation, refraction shift, depth/window/deposit numerics and scope guards re-derive. `_segment_density` equals `mc_brem_spectrum` with escape switched off to 1.000000 (BremsLib and EEDL, channeling off and on). Fixed: the principal square root let `Im(λ_1 − λ_2) < 0` overflow to NaN in thick absorbing grazing Laue (now `Im ≥ 0`, test added); `ab` notation stated as `|χ_g|²`. The fixes are anchored by tests, not re-verified; status applied by the task owner. Human sign-off in #277.

## `diffracted-characteristic-radiation`

- **Claim:** every perfect single-slab crystal case adds to `spec_characteristic` the signed Kossel term of each characteristic line near a Laue Bragg energy `E_B = −ħc g²/(2 n·g)` toward `n`. The term is `S(E) = Y 𝓛(E) Σ_k w_k [½Σ_s(|T_s|² + |D_s|²) − |T_0|² + Re(φ_Z X)](L − z_k, E)`, with `X = ½Σ_s 2C_s T_s* D_s`.
  - `Y` is the line's unattenuated yield [photons/sr/electron], `𝓛` its unit-area natural Lorentzian, and `w_k` its depth distribution.
  - `T_s(u) = [(λ_1 − N_22)e^{iλ_1u} − (λ_2 − N_22)e^{iλ_2u}]/(λ_1 − λ_2)` and `D_s` are the transmitted and diffracted amplitudes of the two-beam Laue reciprocity mode of `diffracted-bremsstrahlung` (`P(0) = (1, 0)`). `|T_0|² = exp(−k Im χ_0 u/n_s)` is the attenuation that native scoring already books.
  - Reciprocity gives `p·E(r_j)` for the field of a wave incident along `−n` that diffracts with `−g`. Averaged over orientation, an isotropic emitter on lattice site `r_j` adds the standing-wave cross term `C_s Re(T_s* D_s e^{−ig·r_j})`, first order in `χ_g`.
  - With `D` computed for a real coupling `|χ_g|`, the emitter phase is `φ_Z = (χ_mode/|χ_g|)⟨e^{−2πi hkl·R_j}⟩_{j∈Z} e^{−W}`. Here `χ_mode = conj(χ(−hkl))` and `e^{−W}` is the Debye–Waller coherent fraction; `φ = 0` for an element off the crystal basis.
  - Without absorption, flux conservation `n_s|T|² + (Q_s/k)|D|² = n_s` reduces the intensity part to `(1 − γ_h/γ_n)|D|²`. The kinematic band of `Re(φX)` is `−π Im φ Σ_s C_s b_s ħcQ_s/|n·g|`, so a real (centrosymmetric) phase gives a dispersive profile.
- **Code:** `src/pyrite/montecarlo/spectrum/diffracted_brem.py::{laue_mode_intensities,bragg_window,bragg_reflections}`; `src/pyrite/montecarlo/spectrum/diffracted_characteristic.py::{line_depth_yields,kossel_term,emitter_phases,diffracted_characteristic_spectrum,kossel_from_yields}`; `src/pyrite/montecarlo/runner/diffracted_brem.py::{case_diffracted_characteristic,DiffractedDepthAccumulator}`, called from `src/pyrite/montecarlo/runner/case_driver.py::_spectrum_case_impl` and, with depth weights summed over blocks and the mode solved once, `src/pyrite/montecarlo/runner/streamed_scoring.py::BlockSpectrumScorer.add` and `src/pyrite/montecarlo/runner/streamed_scoring.py::BlockSpectrumScorer.result`. Marker `DIFFRACTED_BREM_MODEL = two-beam-laue-dbr-kossel-v2`.
- **Source:** reciprocity emission into the two-beam Laue mode (`diffracted-bremsstrahlung`); two-beam dynamical diffraction, kinematic Laue integrated reflectivity and the X-ray standing-wave field (Authier, *Dynamical Theory of X-ray Diffraction*); Kossel lines as X-ray standing waves in reverse, via reciprocity. The derivation is the author's ([write-up](radiation-physics/diffracted-characteristic-radiation.md)).
- **Assumptions:** those of `diffracted-bremsstrahlung`:
  - two-beam Laue above `3√|χ_0|`;
  - perfect, laterally infinite single slab;
  - `χ` taken at `E_B` and flat across the window;
  - `χ_gχ_{−g} → |χ_g|²`, i.e. no Borrmann absorption, a relative `O(ε μL/γ)` error with `ε = |χ''_g|/χ''_0`, where `χ''_g` is the Fourier coefficient of `Im χ(r)` (origin-independent; 0.9 for Ge (400), 0.86 for GaAs (111)). The claim holds for `ε μL/γ ≪ 1` (0.037 at the Ge check). No thickness guard enforces this, as for DBR: thicker crystals are scored outside the claim. Thick-crystal Kossel contrast is not claimed.

  Plus:
  - unpolarized, isotropic lines with natural Lorentzians, emitted equally by every basis site of the element (no site-resolved ionization), with an isotropic Debye–Waller factor from the case `B`;
  - two-beam absorption from `Im χ_0` (photoabsorption) while native escape uses total `μ`, with the subtraction using the same `χ_0`;
  - lines farther than 100 FWHM outside the Bragg window are skipped;
  - Bragg-window step at most FWHM/8 of the nearest line, still capped at 40 000 nodes; 64 depth bins;
  - no Bragg-case Kossel lines, mosaic crystals, layers or grooves; electron-side orientation effects on ionization are excluded.
- **Limiting cases:**
  - `|T|²` and `|D|²` solve the mode (`expm`, 1e-8);
  - `Re(φX)` matches `expm` with complex `χ_g` and an emitter phase (1e-8);
  - `χ_g → 0` gives plain attenuation, `D = 0` and a zero term (1e-12);
  - flux is conserved without absorption (1e-12); the symmetric non-absorbing intensity term is 0 (1e-12);
  - the kinematic intensity band equals `Y 𝓛(E_B)(1 − γ_h/γ_n) R_E` with textbook `R_θ` (1e-2);
  - kinematic `∫ dE Im X = π Σ_s C_s b_s ħcQ_s/|n·g|` (3e-3);
  - depth yields × native escape reproduce `mc_characteristic_spectrum` (2e-3).
- **Status:** anchored
- **Checks:** Ge 100 keV, 2 µm, detector on the Kα1 Kossel cone of (−4 0 0) (`γ_n − γ_h = 0.63`, `φ_Ge = −0.927`). The profile is dispersive: −44% of native from 2 eV below the line to the line, and +76% 2 eV above. The ±15 eV band is +11% of native; the intensity terms alone give +21%. The runner adds exactly this term to `spec_characteristic`; out-of-claim cases add nothing.
- **Anchor:** `tests/montecarlo/spectrum/test_diffracted_characteristic.py`.
  - **Measured:** `test_emitter_phase_matches_measured_gaas_kossel_phases` compares `arg(−φ_Z)` with the measured Laue Kossel-line phases Φm of Faigel, Bortel & Tegze, Sci. Rep. 6, 22904 (2016), doi:10.1038/srep22904 (Zotero `JBG7MXA6`), Table 1. The result is rms 9.2° over 22 rows, within their ~10° fit error. Two rows are excluded as index misprints by the zinc-blende parity rule. The conjugated convention is off by 74.5° rms.
  - **Analytic:** the remaining tests.
  - **Not anchored:** the magnitude. Those crystals are thick, their geometry is unstated, and their profiles are broadened. Bortel et al., arXiv:2310.18203 (Zotero `TX6HBKQC`), is a candidate for it.
- **Notes:** #1176. Changes `spec_characteristic` only near Kossel cones; the marker bump re-pins every dataset identity and content key.
  - **2026-11-14 fresh-context verdict `discrepancy`** ([write-up](radiation-physics/diffracted-characteristic-radiation.md#fresh-context-verification-2026-11-14)): the first-order standing-wave cross term was omitted. Everything else re-derived (mode, `T_s`, flux conservation, kinematic band, no double count, depth yields, numerics, wiring); the Borrmann validity condition was tightened.
  - The cross term is now scored with complex `χ_mode` and per-element sublattice phases (`383e55b89`).
  - **2026-11-14 fresh-context re-check: `rederived`.** It confirmed `h = −g`, the site phase `e^{−ig·r_j}` and `χ_mode = conj(χ(−hkl))` (to 4e-15 against an independent Fourier sum). An independent full-field `|p·E|²` site and thermal average for GaAs (111), (200), (311), (400) and both elements matches intensity + `Re(φ_Z X)` to 2–6e-12; the wrong convention shifts it by 7e-2 of a ~0.45 peak. The kinematic `∫Im X` ratio is 1.0028. Concerns applied: ε is defined by the absorptive Fourier coefficient; no thickness guard. Status applied by the task owner.
  - **2026-11-14 anchored** by the task owner against Faigel 2016 phases ([write-up](radiation-physics/diffracted-characteristic-radiation.md#anchor)). Their Fig. 4 dark-below/bright-above sign for Φ ≈ 0 lines is reproduced by a probe for four surface orientations.
  - Human sign-off in #277.

## `node-support-window-tail`

- **Claim:** support-windowed incoherent node scoring adds a cycle-averaged endpoint-cluster expansion through inverse fourth power, with exact near-window corrections. Affine dispersion uses the effective slope, pole and residue exactly; general dispersion uses a frozen-denominator surrogate fenced by `rho <= 1/x_c < 1`. Cluster membership bounds full-band phase drift against one representative; the multipole ratio is at most 0.1. An aggregate weak-field envelope (including higher moments and remainder) and an all-dispersion-interval beat-gap fence control window widening. These are absolute field/envelope statements, not a universal relative spectral-error guarantee; numerical margins require qualification.
- **Code:** `montecarlo/spectrum/cxr/_node_window.py`; `montecarlo/spectrum/cxr/_setup.py::_node_tail`
- **Source:** formation integral in `coherent-formation-absorption`; straight-flight limit in `substep-radiation-invariance`; independent geometric-series, perturbation and phase-drift bounds ([write-up](radiation-physics/node-support-window-tail.md)).
- **Assumptions:** positive affine slope (otherwise exact reference); uniform grid (otherwise reference); grouped cutoff greater than one (otherwise reference). Shared junction cancellation requires matching phase functions, attenuation, pole and residue, not merely similar slopes. Isolated continuous signed-yield asymptotics require resolved sampling; coarse node aliases have no such bound.
- **Status:** rederived
- **Checks:** single undamped row gives `1/(2a²x²)`; grid-wide windows reproduce the reference; 1/16/1024-way transparent splitting preserves moments and windows; affine-dispersion effective pole/residue; signed isolated-line yield error `O(1/x_c²)` distinguished from mass-L1 `O(1/x_c)`; direct/far poles; CPU/CUDA parity
- **Anchor:** `tests/montecarlo/spectrum/test_node_support_window.py`; `tests/montecarlo/spectrum/test_node_support_window_cuda.py`
- **Notes:** Issue #1191; analytic tails selected by the human. Fresh-context review at `37a1c11ae` exposed chained slope-cluster merging and the false exact frozen-dispersion identity. Regression tests reproduced both; the revised planner repairs them and adds denominator, multipole, phase-drift and aggregate weak-field fences. Fresh-context final recheck returned `rederived` for the revised conditional mathematical claim (units/limits/signs pass; 28 host/docs checks pass; rendered math checked). Status applied by the task owner. RTX 5080 parity passed (6 tests at `52cd8e78b`). Frozen #1142/#1143 scenes pass every margin (yield ~1e-5 uncapped, <=1e-6 capped). The [qualification table](radiation-physics/node-support-window-tail.md#frozen-scene-qualification-rtx-5080-2026-11-14) gives the cost. NumPy and float32 CuPy are implemented; FP64 CuPy retains the reference. Default is `"auto"` since `CXR_ESCAPE_MODEL` v8, where CuPy per-row lines keep the dense reducer. Human sign-off is separate in #277.

## `brem-lattice-coherent-fraction`

- **Claim:** for a randomly oriented crystal, the orientation-averaged CBS line yield of the explicit reflections `|g| ≤ G` equals, to first Born order in `U_g`, the lattice-coherent (Debye–Waller-weighted) part of the screened-nucleus Born bremsstrahlung below momentum transfer `G`: with `Σ_g → V∫d³g/(2π)³` and an incoherent basis average, `Σ_g |U_g|² h(|g|) → n_at ∫ g² dg/(2π²) |u(g)|² e^{−2W(g)} h(g)`, `u = 4πe²(Z − f0(g))/g²`; dropping `e^{−2W}` and the cutoff gives the amorphous Born brem from the same amplitude. Explicit lines up to `G` therefore double-count a fraction `R(G) = Σ_{|g|≤G}|U_g|²h / [n_at∫g²dg/(2π²)|u|²h]` of a tabulated amorphous brem
- **Code:** `montecarlo/spectrum/brem_lattice_share.py::{shell_kernel_x,kernel_x,reflection_shell_weights,lattice_share_density,lattice_share_table,lattice_share_model}`; `montecarlo/runner/lattice_share.py::{layer_emission_tables,share_only_tables,scored_reflections,lattice_share_applies,lattice_share_record,SelectionContinuum}`; continuum hooks `montecarlo/runner/emission.py::{_uncoupled_brem_from_segments,_coupled_brem_from_segments}`
- **Source:** production line weight (`coherent-line-spectrum`) and `A_CBS = (U_g/mc²) f_cbs` (`cbs-amplitude`), orientation average over `ĝ` and lattice-sum-to-integral limit; Ter-Mikaelian, *High-Energy Electromagnetic Processes in Condensed Media* (1972), coherent/incoherent bremsstrahlung split by `e^{−2W}`
- **Status:** anchored
- **Checks:** numerical only (#1250 P2, Si 30/300 keV, 45°/90°): quadrature of the orientation average vs a 20k-orientation MC of the production line weight 0.998 ± 0.002 (the earlier 2–3 % offset against `mc_cxr_spectrum` was a 1 Å-slab grazing-exit artifact of the harness: paired agreement 1.0008 / 0.9997 for `|n_z| ≥ 0.1`); no-DW Born vs BremsLib's electron–nucleus part `BremsLib/(1 + η/Z)` 1.00–1.04 at 300 keV, 0.98 → 0.86 toward the tip at 30 keV (Born without Coulomb/Elwert correction, `αZ/β ≈ 0.31`); `Σ_{≤g30}` CBS / BremsLib 0.091–0.133; continuum cutoff integral vs discrete sum within 1–4 % for `G ≥ g8`. Frozen-transport scenes (#1250 P3, 3 seeds): `S/CBS_MC` 0.999–1.008 (Si 300 keV, `G ≥ g12`) and 1.04–1.05 (MoS₂/Si 30 keV; excess 0.4 % of brem, 85 % at 0.5–2 keV); Si temperature check `S/CBS_MC` 1.002–1.007 while CBS changes 2.6× over `B` = 0.23–0.92 Å². Production kernel bit-identical to the verified quadrature (≤ 1e-15); runtime share vs P3 tables 1.004 (per bin 0.98–1.03, Si 30 keV/45°, `G = 30`); production brem drop with the corrected table vs `S` 0.991 (BremsLib κ-node sampling). Kernel interpolation: Si-scene probes at the released spacings `|g|` ≤ 1 % per bin, `θ` ≤ 0.7 % (2.7 % at 10°), `T` ≤ 0.4 % total (`scratch/p5a_*probe*`); full released grid, drop-one-node at doubled spacing, mass-weighted misplaced fraction 4.9 % (`|g|`), 1.2 % (`θ`), 4.6 % (`T`), worst at the recoil edge and 1–4 keV (`2026-11-17-p5a/loo-weighted.log`). Production verification 2026-11-17: independent classical Liénard–Wiechert check (4e6 random ĝ, 45°) vs the released kernel 0.9988 / 1.0018 band total at 300 keV (3 and 8 Å⁻¹), 0.987 at 30 keV (recoil shift at the classical endpoint)
- **Anchor:** `tests/montecarlo/spectrum/test_brem_lattice_share.py::test_released_kernel_pins_the_si_30kev_g30_share` (released kernel, Si 30 keV/45°/`G = 30`: `S` pinned and within 0.3 % of the independent P3 quadrature, production drop 0.1302 of the in-band brem, drop/`S` 0.990); limits `::test_large_debye_waller_and_empty_sets_remove_nothing` (`B → ∞`, `G → 0`); `::test_table_loses_exactly_k_S_over_n_Z2_and_stays_self_consistent`; `::test_no_share_is_removed_below_the_line_electron_cutoff`; `tests/montecarlo/runner/test_lattice_share.py`
- **Notes:** #1250. Production since P5 (approved 2026-11-17): each crystalline BremsLib layer's emission tables lose `k S_j/(n_j Z²)` per DDCS node for exactly the scored reflections, with `S = Σ_g |U_g|² H(|g|)` from the released crystal-independent kernel `H(|g|; T, θ, x)`, `x = ħω(1 − β cosθ)/(ħc g β)`; transport keeps the full tables. Kernel release builder: `scripts/release_brem_lattice_share_table.py` (`tables-brem-lattice-share-1`, sha256 `c3e47b70…`). Not corrected: elements without tables in a BremsLib run (their isotropic EEDL fallback; warned since #1261), channeling cases, `T` > 1.06 MeV; the EEDL route is corrected under `brem-lattice-share-isotropic` (#1261); grazing photon exit, where production lines are refraction-suppressed, is outside the vacuum-kinematics share. Assumes Born order, isotropic DW, straight segment, static Mott–Bethe `Z − f0`, vacuum kinematics in the quadrature; recoil-exact kinematics are required for a finite denominator (classically `g²|u|²h ∝ 1/g` diverges logarithmically; the recoil-exact integrand stops at `(p + p_f)/ħ`, and 46 % of the 300 keV Born yield lies above 100 Å⁻¹), so `R(G)` is defined only with recoil-exact `h`. Denominator: subtract the absolute Born-DW CBS-only share `S(G)` (production amplitude, recoil-exact `h`); if a fraction is used, apply `R(G)` to `BremsLib/(1 + η/Z)` only, since electron–electron brem is not lattice-coherent. The two differ by 0.001 (300 keV/45°) to 0.008 (30 keV/45°) of brem; carry that as the systematic. PXR and PXR×CBS interference are never subtracted. **Line-electron floor (P5c, `born-dw-cbs-share-v2`):** table nodes below `E_cut_lines_keV` (5 keV default) keep the full DDCS, since the line route scores no CBS there. On a 16-history MoS₂/Si transport the share removed below the cutoff was 2.8 % of `S` (2.3e-3 of brem, 95 % at 0.5–2 keV): the location of the P3 MoS₂/Si `S/CBS` excess, which it largely explains; the Si 300 keV scene does not stop the beam and is unaffected. Runs record the correction per layer as `brem_lattice_share` (output, provenance, brem checkpoint component). Fresh-context verification 2026-11-17 matched the draft term for term: `rederived`; the regression anchor above moves it to `anchored` (local green; CI pending). [Independent verification](radiation-physics/brem-lattice-coherent-fraction.md). Human sign-off remains separate. Floor is applied at staged T nodes; ln T interpolation ramps it over the one staged interval below `E_cut_lines_keV` (≈3e-3 of S at Si 30 keV, over-subtraction, a few 1e-4 of brem). Reflections outside 0.25–75.4 Å⁻¹ contribute no share. Fresh-context production verification 2026-11-17 (including the v2 line-cutoff floor): matches, `rederived` for the production form; `anchored` on the named regressions, pending green CI. [Production verification](radiation-physics/brem-lattice-coherent-fraction.md#production-verification-2026-11-17).

## `brem-lattice-share-isotropic`

- **Claim:** an isotropic continuum (EEDL or Bethe–Heitler `dσ_j/dk`, emitted as `dσ_j/dk/(4π)` toward every direction) loses the lattice-coherent share of the scored reflections by absolute subtraction of its solid-angle integral: `dσ_j/dk → max(0, dσ_j/dk − Δσ_j)`, `Δσ_j(T, k) = 2π ∫ S_j(T, θ, k) sin θ dθ / n_j`, with `S_j` the orientation-averaged Born–DW CBS-only share of `brem-lattice-coherent-fraction`. `Δσ_j` is exactly the SDCS the BremsLib route removes from element `j` when its DDCS loses `k S_j/(n_j Z²)` on the kernel's θ nodes, so the angle-integrated lines-plus-continuum yield equals the isotropic model's own (at BremsLib `T` nodes, with no DDCS node clamped), as it equals BremsLib's on the BremsLib route.
- **Code:** `montecarlo/spectrum/brem_lattice_share.py::{isotropic_share_sigma,IsotropicLatticeShare}`; `montecarlo/runner/lattice_share.py::layer_isotropic_share`; scorer hook `montecarlo/spectrum/brem.py::mc_brem_spectrum` (`isotropic_lattice_share`); continuum hook `montecarlo/runner/emission.py::_uncoupled_brem_from_segments`
- **Source:** `brem-lattice-coherent-fraction` (share density, absolute-subtraction denominator); `bremslib-angular-model` (`solid_angle_integral`, linear in θ between nodes)
- **Assumptions:** those of `brem-lattice-coherent-fraction`; the isotropic continuum's angular distribution is not corrected (the share is removed from the angle-integrated rate and the remainder stays isotropic, so the double count is removed on average over photon direction, not per direction: per direction it is `L[S_j(θ) − ⟨S_j⟩_{4π}]`; `S(θ)/⟨S⟩` spans 0.49–2.66 (30 keV) and 0.13–12.9 (300 keV) for Si `G = 30`, against 0.67–2.08 and 0.21–8.6 for the BremsLib DDCS, so the local coherent fraction varies ×0.6–1.5 at 300 keV: a few percent of the in-band continuum in a fixed-angle forward-beam observable); absolute, not fractional, subtraction; `Δσ` tabulated on `ln T` nodes (the kernel's, each split in 4) from `max(1 keV, E_cut_lines_keV)` to 1.05 MeV and on ≤ 96 log `k` nodes per decade, linear in `ln T` and `ln k`; zero outside that `T` range; clamped at zero per (segment, `k`) cell.
- **Status:** anchored
- **Checks:** Si `B = 0.46 Å²`, released kernel, `G = 8` and `30 Å⁻¹`: `Δσ` equals the BremsLib route's removed SDCS (`Z²/k` × scaled SDCS) to 1e-14 at the table's 30/100/300 keV nodes over 0.5–10 keV (the table's θ nodes contain the kernel's); removed fraction of the BremsLib SDCS over 0.5–10 keV 0.10–0.13 at 100–300 keV (`G = 30`); `ln T` interpolation between nodes over 5.5–1000 keV: band-integrated error ≤ 6e-4, per cell ≤ 2e-3 of the row peak; a single EEDL segment loses exactly `Δσ/σ_EEDL` per bin; `B → ∞` and `G → 0` give zero; nothing is removed below the floor or above the kernel.
- **Anchor:** `tests/montecarlo/spectrum/test_brem_lattice_share.py::test_isotropic_share_is_the_sdcs_the_bremslib_route_removes` (slow; #1261 acceptance scene, Si `G = 30`), `::test_isotropic_share_limits_and_floor`, `::test_eedl_spectrum_loses_exactly_the_isotropic_share`; `tests/montecarlo/runner/test_lattice_share.py::test_eedl_crystal_gets_theta_integrated_shares_without_warning`; physics-baseline EEDL fixtures (`tests/data/physics-baseline/README.md`, #1261)
- **Notes:** #1261 (from #1250 P4, approved 2026-11-17 as "brem-weighted θ average r̄_j"). The BremsLib-DDCS-weighted average `r̄_j = ∫ r_j DDCS dΩ / SDCS` collapses to `∫ S_j dΩ/(n_j SDCS_BremsLib)`. Applying that fraction to EEDL would scale the removed share by `σ_EEDL/σ_BremsLib` and would need BremsLib tables in an EEDL run, so the absolute form (the parent claim's primary denominator) is used. Identity: `BREM_LATTICE_SHARE_ISOTROPIC_MODEL = born-dw-cbs-share-v2/tables-brem-lattice-share-1/scored-set/isotropic-theta-integrated-v1` under the `brem_lattice_share_model` key; runs record `continuum="isotropic"` per layer. When a share applies, the EEDL route keeps the chunked reduction (no fused CUDA EEDL kernel). The reflection selector's net metric stays BremsLib-only, so an EEDL selection stops on gross line yield. Fresh-context verification 2026-11-21: `rederived`; BremsLib identity reproduced to 1.3e-14 (Si, `G = 8`, 30), absolute θ-integral within 0.3–0.7 % (band) of an independent classical full-sphere Monte Carlo at 100–300 keV; absolute vs fractional (`r̄_j`) differ by `Δσ(σ_EEDL/σ_BL − 1)`, ≤ 7e-3 of the in-band continuum for Si; the floor is an exact step at `E_cut_lines_keV` (the BremsLib route ramps over one staged interval); clamped cells are not recorded. `anchored` 2026-11-22: the named regressions (including the slow acceptance anchor) are green in `main` CI run 44989862190 at `60e8d929f`. [Independent verification](radiation-physics/brem-lattice-share-isotropic.md).
